## W261. E-Wallet & Digital Payment Settlement Reconciliation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Workflow ID** | W261 |
| **Name** | E-Wallet & Digital Payment Settlement Reconciliation |
| **Trigger** | Settlement report received from e-wallet provider (daily) |
| **Frequency** | Daily reconciliation; monthly full review |
| **Volume** | ~45,000 ecommerce orders/month × 40% e-wallet = ~18,000 e-wallet transactions/month + ~560,000 POS transactions/month × 30% e-wallet = ~168,000 POS e-wallet transactions/month = ~186,000 e-wallet transactions/month total |
| **Owner** | Treasury Analyst |
| **Participants** | Treasury Analyst (1), AR Supervisor (1), Controller (1), Store Manager (200) |
| **Time Estimate** | 1–2 hours/day for reconciliation; 4 hours/month-end review |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Settlement Report Import**: Treasury Analyst imports daily settlement reports from e-wallet providers (GCash, Maya, GrabPay, ShopeePay): (a) system receives settlement file via SFTP or API (daily, T+1 or T+2 depending on provider); (b) file contains: gross transactions, provider fees/commissions, net settlement amount, settlement bank account, settlement reference; (c) system parses and loads into settlement staging table | Treasury Analyst | Controller | 20 min |
| 2 | **Transaction Matching**: System auto-matches settlement line items to ERP transaction records: (a) POS e-wallet payments: match by terminal ID + transaction reference + date + amount; (b) ecommerce e-wallet payments: match by order ID + payment reference + amount; (c) QR code payments (POS): match by QR transaction ID + store location + amount; (d) system flags unmatched items for manual review | System | Controller | Automated |
| 3 | **Fee & Commission Verification**: System reconciles provider fees against contracted rates: (a) GCash: MDR (merchant discount rate) typically 1.5–2.0% per transaction; (b) Maya: MDR typically 1.5–2.5%; (c) GrabPay: MDR typically 2.0–3.0%; (d) cross-border or promo-specific fees (e.g., 0% MDR during bank-partner promos per W149); (e) system flags fee variances > 0.1% from contracted rate | System / Treasury Analyst | Controller | 15 min |
| 4 | **Unmatched Transaction Resolution**: Treasury Analyst investigates unmatched items: (a) **timing differences**: POS transaction on T, settlement report on T+1 or T+2 (carry forward for next-day matching); (b) **partial settlements**: provider settled net of disputed chargebacks; reverse chargeback amount to AR dispute queue; (c) **duplicate settlements**: provider sent duplicate file or duplicate line; verify with provider and reject duplicate; (d) **missing transactions**: POS shows e-wallet payment but provider has no record; escalate to Store Manager for proof-of-payment (customer screenshot) and contact provider; (e) **amount variances**: typically caused by promotional discounts absorbed by provider vs. merchant; cross-reference with promo calendar (W13) | Treasury Analyst | Controller | 30–60 min |
| 5 | **Store-Level Reconciliation**: For POS e-wallet transactions: (a) system generates per-store daily e-wallet settlement summary; (b) compares POS Z-report e-wallet total (W5F) vs. settlement report e-wallet total per store; (c) variances > PHP 500 per store per day flagged and sent to Store Manager for investigation; (d) common causes: offline transactions synced late (W5G), cashier tendered to wrong e-wallet type, customer-initiated refund processed outside ERP | System / Treasury Analyst | Controller | 30 min |
| 6 | **GL Posting**: System posts settlement entries: (a) Dr. Cash (bank account per settlement) for net settlement amount; (b) Dr. Merchant Discount Rate Expense for provider fees; (c) Cr. E-Wallet Clearing Account (interim account for unmatched); (d) matched transactions clear the interim account; unmatched items remain as reconciliation items | System | Controller | Automated |
| 7 | **Monthly Provider Review**: Treasury Analyst prepares monthly e-wallet reconciliation summary: (a) total gross transactions per provider; (b) total fees paid per provider vs. contracted rates; (c) unmatched transaction aging (> 7 days escalate to provider; > 30 days write-off per FIN-022); (d) settlement timeliness (T+1 vs. T+2 vs. delayed); (e) fee negotiation recommendations for Controller based on volume thresholds | Treasury Analyst | Controller | 2 hours |
| 8 | **Dispute Resolution with Provider**: For chargebacks and disputed transactions: (a) provider notifies of chargeback; system creates dispute case; (b) Treasury Analyst gathers evidence (POS transaction log, delivery proof, customer communication); (c) respond to provider within dispute window (typically 5–10 business days); (d) if dispute resolved in merchant favor: provider re-settles amount; (e) if dispute lost: system posts chargeback as expense; (f) dispute tracking feeds into provider scorecard for contract renewal (W149) | Treasury Analyst / AR Supervisor | Controller | 2–4 hours/dispute |

### System Touchpoints
- E-wallet provider API/SFTP integration for settlement file import (GCash, Maya, GrabPay, ShopeePay)
- Auto-matching engine: settlement line items ↔ POS transactions ↔ ecommerce orders
- Fee verification engine with contracted MDR rates per provider
- Per-store settlement reconciliation dashboard
- GL auto-posting for settlement, fees, and clearing entries
- Dispute case management module with evidence attachment
- Settlement aging report for unmatched items
- Provider performance dashboard (settlement speed, fee accuracy, dispute rate)

### Time Estimate
- Daily settlement report import: ~20 min/day across 4 providers (GCash, Maya, GrabPay, ShopeePay) (W261.1)
- Daily transaction matching: automated; ~5 min review of match results and exception count (W261.2)
- Daily fee and commission verification: ~15 min/day to review fee variance flags (W261.3)
- Daily unmatched transaction resolution: ~30–60 min/day for typical exception volume (~2–5% of ~6,200 daily e-wallet transactions = ~120–300 exceptions/day, most resolved by timing/carry-forward; ~10–30 require manual investigation) (W261.4)
- Daily store-level reconciliation review: ~30 min/day to review per-store variance flags across 200 stores (W261.5)
- Daily GL posting: automated (W261.6)
- Total daily reconciliation effort: ~1.5–2 hours/day (Treasury Analyst)
- Monthly provider review: ~2 hours/month per provider = ~8 hours/month across 4 providers (W261.7)
- Dispute resolution: ~2–4 hours per dispute case; typically 2–4 disputes/month = ~4–16 hours/month (shared with AR Supervisor) (W261.8)
- Month-end full reconciliation review: ~4 hours for comprehensive aging review, write-off processing, and Controller sign-off
- Total monthly e-wallet reconciliation effort: ~35–50 hours/month (Treasury Analyst) + ~4–16 hours/month (AR Supervisor for disputes) + ~2–3 hours/month (Controller review)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Volume complexity**: ~186,000 e-wallet transactions/month across multiple providers with different settlement cycles, file formats, and fee structures
- **Provider file format changes**: E-wallet providers frequently change settlement report formats without notice; auto-import breaks and requires manual intervention
- **Settlement delays**: Some providers settle T+2 or T+3; creates timing differences that complicate daily reconciliation and cash position reporting (W30)
- **Promo confusion**: During bank/e-wallet partner promos (W149), MDR may be 0% (bank-subsidized) or reduced; if promo data not synced to ERP, fee verification generates false variances
- **QR code matching**: QR PH (national QR standard) transactions may have different reference formats per issuing bank; matching logic must handle multiple reference formats
- **Offline e-wallet**: When POS is offline (W5G), e-wallet transactions may be processed manually via customer's app screenshot; difficult to reconcile against settlement

### Staffing Implication
- **1 Treasury Analyst**: daily e-wallet reconciliation adds ~1–2 hours/day + 2 hours/month-end review = ~30–40 hours/month; this is a significant portion of one Treasury Analyst's capacity but absorbed within the existing 2–3 person Treasury team
- **1 AR Supervisor**: dispute resolution with e-wallet providers adds ~4–8 hours/month during periods of elevated chargebacks; absorbed
- **1 Controller**: fee verification review and monthly provider review sign-off add ~2–3 hours/month; absorbed
- **No incremental headcount**: the ~186,000 monthly e-wallet transactions are processed by the auto-matching engine; human effort is primarily exception handling and monthly review

## W276. Asset Under Construction (AUC) & Mass Capitalization

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | A new store construction project begins (W225), requiring cost accumulation over several months before go-live. |
| **Frequency** | Project-based (10-15 per year) |
| **Volume** | Hundreds of AP invoices per project; typical new store build-out costs PHP 15–30M with 30–60 capitalized asset components (HVAC, racking, shelving, lighting, leasehold improvements, POS hardware, IT infrastructure, signage) |
| **Owner** | Fixed Asset Accountant |
| **Participants** | Engineering/Project Manager (W225), AP Clerk (W7), Fixed Asset Accountant, Controller (approval for capitalization), IT Asset Manager (for IT equipment classification) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Finance creates an Internal Order / WBS Element tagged as Asset Under Construction (AUC). | Fixed Asset Accountant | Controller | 30 min |
| 2 | AP posts all contractor invoices, materials, and direct labor against this AUC over 6 months. | AP Clerk | AP Supervisor | 10 min/invoice |
| 3 | Engineering confirms Store Operational Handover (W227). | Engineering/PM | VP Store Dev | Per W227 |
| 4 | Fixed Asset Accountant executes a mass settlement profile in the ERP. | Fixed Asset Accountant | Controller | 2 hours |
| 5 | The system zero-balances the AUC and distributes the millions in costs across dozens of distinct Capitalized Fixed Assets (HVAC, racking, leasehold improvements) based on predefined allocation rules. | System | Controller | Automated |
| 6 | Depreciation begins automatically on the newly created assets. | System | — | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- **Project System (WBS)**: Internal Order / WBS element creation for each new store project; cost accumulation by cost element (materials, labor, contractor fees, permits); integration with W225 (store construction project management) and W227 (operational handover confirmation)
- **Accounts Payable (W7)**: contractor invoices and material purchase orders posted directly to AUC WBS element rather than expense; 3-way match (PO → GR → Invoice) still applies but goods receipt is project-based rather than inventory-based
- **Fixed Assets module**: mass capitalization from AUC settlement; predefined allocation rules mapping WBS cost elements to fixed asset classes (e.g., cost element "HVAC materials" → asset class "Building Improvements"); individual asset records created with asset tag, location, useful life, and depreciation method per asset class
- **Integration with W21 (Capex)**: original capex request and approval tied to AUC WBS element; actual vs. budgeted capex cost report generated at capitalization; Controller validates that total capitalized cost does not exceed approved capex amount without additional approval
- **Integration with W39 (Disposal)**: if a store is closed (W45) before lease end, leasehold improvements capitalized through AUC may require accelerated depreciation or write-off

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Costs stranded in AUC indefinitely**: if Engineering delays Store Operational Handover (W227) or AP posts invoices to the wrong WBS element, costs accumulate in AUC without generating depreciation expense; this understates operating expenses and overstates assets until the issue is discovered — often during year-end audit (W9B); Fixed Asset Accountant must run monthly AUC aging report and escalate projects > 90 days post-handover without capitalization
- **Allocation rule accuracy**: predefined allocation rules must be updated when store format changes (e.g., a new "express" format with different asset mix than standard stores); incorrect allocation rules distribute costs to wrong asset classes with wrong useful lives, distorting depreciation for years until corrected
- **Threshold ambiguity for expense vs. capitalize**: contractor invoices may include items that should be expensed (e.g., maintenance on adjacent property, pre-opening marketing) but get incorrectly posted to AUC; Controller must review AUC line items before mass settlement to reclassify non-capitalizable costs to the appropriate expense accounts
- **Multi-vendor coordination**: a single store build-out involves 10–20 contractors and suppliers posting invoices over 6+ months; if the Engineering/Project Manager does not flag change orders or scope variations, the final capitalized cost may exceed the approved capex (W21) without triggering re-approval

### Time Estimate
4–6 hours per store capitalization (including AUC cleanup, allocation review, Controller validation, and asset register update)

### Staffing Implication
- **1 Fixed Asset Accountant**: AUC management and mass capitalization adds ~4–6 hours per store project × 10–15 projects/year = ~40–90 hours/year; with ~10 projects active simultaneously during peak expansion, the FA Accountant needs ~15–25 hours/month during construction season; absorbed within existing Finance team but is the FA Accountant's primary responsibility during Q4–Q1 expansion periods
- **1 Controller**: capitalization approval and AUC line-item review add ~2–3 hours per project; with 10–15 projects/year, this is ~20–45 hours/year; absorbed within existing Controller duties
- **AP Clerks (shared)**: posting contractor invoices to AUC WBS elements adds minimal incremental effort vs. standard W7 invoice processing (~10 min/invoice)
- **No incremental headcount**: the Fixed Asset Accountant absorbs AUC management as a core responsibility; however, if expansion accelerates beyond 20 new stores/year, a dedicated Project Accountant role should be considered

## W277. Freight Bill Audit & Payment (FBAP) Reconciliation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | 3PL or freight forwarder submits a consolidated invoice for hundreds of deliveries/transfers. |
| **Frequency** | Weekly / Monthly |
| **Volume** | Thousands of lines per invoice; estimated ~25,000–35,000 DC-to-store shipments/month (W4) + ~5,000 inter-DC transfers/month (W22) + ~17,000 home delivery shipments/month (W19) = ~47,000–57,000 freight-billable movements/month across 4 DCs and multiple 3PL carriers |
| **Owner** | Logistics Finance Analyst |
| **Participants** | Logistics Finance Analyst, Logistics Manager (rate card negotiation), 3PL Account Manager (dispute resolution), AP Clerk (payment processing), Controller (variance approval) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 3PL submits an EDI or portal-based invoice containing freight charges, fuel surcharges, and accessorial fees. | 3PL / Logistics Finance Analyst | Logistics Finance Analyst | 30 min/import |
| 2 | ERP Transportation Management module automatically audits the invoice against the negotiated rate cards, actual weight/cube, and route distance. | System | Logistics Finance Analyst | Automated |
| 3 | System flags discrepancies (e.g., overcharges, duplicate billings) beyond a tolerance of 1%. | System | Logistics Finance Analyst | Automated |
| 4 | Analyst reviews flagged exceptions and issues a dispute/short-pay notice to the 3PL. | Logistics Finance Analyst | Logistics Manager | 1–2 hours |
| 5 | Approved, reconciled amounts are released to AP for payment. | Logistics Finance Analyst | AP Supervisor | 30 min |

### System Touchpoints
- **Transportation Management System (TMS)**: shipment record with origin, destination, carrier, weight, cube, distance, service level; rate card engine with negotiated rates per lane (DC-to-store zone), per weight break, and per service type (standard, express, oversized); automatic freight cost computation per shipment for audit comparison
- **Accounts Payable (W7)**: reconciled and approved freight invoices released to AP payment queue; 3PL vendor master with payment terms (typically Net 30); AP Clerk processes payment via standard payment run
- **EDI / Vendor Portal**: electronic invoice receipt from 3PL carriers (e.g., 2GO, Xignex, local truckers) in standardized format; portal-based invoice submission for smaller carriers without EDI capability; reconciliation workspace showing carrier invoice vs. TMS-computed cost per line
- **Integration with W4 (DC-to-store replenishment)**: each store replenishment shipment generates a TMS record with expected freight cost; at month-end, total freight cost per DC feeds into Logistics Inc. IC fee calculation (W14)
- **Integration with W19 (home delivery)**: 3PL carrier invoices for last-mile delivery (Lalamove, Transportify) reconciled against per-order delivery records; freight cost per order feeds into ecommerce fulfillment IC fee
- **Integration with W44 (vendor scorecard)**: 3PL carrier freight billing accuracy (% of lines matching within tolerance) feeds into carrier performance scorecard; chronic overbilling carriers flagged for rate renegotiation or contract termination

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Overpaying millions in freight due to complex tariff structures**: 3PL rate cards include base rates per zone, weight breaks, fuel surcharges (adjusted monthly based on DOE pump prices), accessorial fees (loading/unloading wait time, oversized surcharges, weekend/holiday premiums), and volume-based rebates; manual auditing is impossible at ~50,000 shipments/month scale — the system audit engine is the only viable control
- **Rate card staleness**: negotiated rate cards expire or become uncompetitive as fuel prices and market rates change; if the Logistics Manager does not renegotiate rates quarterly, BuildRight pays above-market freight for months before the next scheduled rate review
- **Duplicate billing across carriers**: inter-DC transfers (W22) may be billed by both the originating DC's carrier and the receiving DC's carrier if system routing is ambiguous; duplicate detection must cross-reference shipment IDs across carrier invoices
- **Accessorial fee disputes**: 3PL carriers frequently charge for "detention" (driver waiting beyond free time) and "re-delivery" without providing timestamped evidence; disputing these charges requires TMS GPS/timestamp data that is not always captured for local Philippine trucking carriers with limited telematics

### Time Estimate
1–2 days per billing cycle (weekly for top 3PL carriers; monthly for smaller carriers)

### Staffing Implication
- **1 Logistics Finance Analyst**: freight bill audit and reconciliation for ~47,000–57,000 monthly shipments across multiple 3PL carriers requires a dedicated analyst role; weekly billing cycles for top carriers consume ~1–2 days/week; monthly cycles for smaller carriers consume ~2 days/month; total ~6–10 days/month — this is a full-time role
- **1 Logistics Manager**: rate card negotiation and dispute escalation add ~2–4 hours/week; absorbed within existing Logistics management duties
- **1 Controller**: variance approval for disputed freight charges > threshold adds ~1–2 hours/month; absorbed

## W435. Intercompany Service Level Agreement (SLA) Fee Billing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Month-end close cycle (W9A) |
| **Frequency** | Monthly |
| **Volume** | 5 Legal Entities |
| **Owner** | Intercompany Accountant |
| **Participants** | Controller, DC Managers, Ecom Manager, Finance Managers |

### Background

With 5 legal entities—**Holdings (Group), Depot (Retail), Logistics (DC), Digital (Ecom), and Property (Lease)**—there are significant cross-entity services. To maintain "arm's length" transfer pricing compliance (W235), the service-providing entities must bill the service-consuming entities (primarily BuildRight Depot) based on actual consumption or fixed SLA rates.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Data Extraction**: Extract monthly service volumes: (a) Logistics: pallets stored/moved, (b) Digital: ecom orders fulfilled, (c) Property: sqm leased, (d) Holdings: management service hours | Intercompany Accountant | — | 1 day |
| 2 | **Fee Calculation**: Apply the pre-approved SLA rates (per W305 rule master) to the actual volumes to determine the monthly fee per entity | Intercompany Accountant | Controller | 4 hours |
| 3 | **Intercompany Invoice**: System auto-generates Intercompany Invoices (Dr. Expense / Cr. IC Payable for Depot; Dr. IC Receivable / Cr. Service Revenue for service entity) | System | Intercompany Accountant | Automated |
| 4 | **SLA Performance Review**: Compare actual volumes vs. budget; if variances > 20%, provide justification for the management report (W35) | Intercompany Accountant | Finance Manager | 2 hours |
| 5 | **Settlement**: Trigger intercompany cash settlement per W14.8 to clear the IC balances | Intercompany Accountant | Treasury Manager | 1 hour |

### System Touchpoints
- Intercompany Transfer Pricing Master (W305)
- Automated IC Invoicing Engine (W14)
- SLA Performance Dashboard (W35)
- Warehouse Management System (WMS) for pallet volume data

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Transfer Pricing Audit**: Failure to document the *basis* of these fees (actual volumes vs. arbitrary percentages) leads to BIR transfer pricing adjustments and penalties.
- **GL Misalignment**: If the service entity posts revenue in Period 1 but the retail entity posts expense in Period 2, the consolidated group profit will be distorted.

## W461. Intercompany Fulfillment & Logistics Fee Settlement

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Month-end close cycle |
| **Frequency** | Monthly |

| **Owner** | Intercompany Accountant |
| **Participants** | Logistics Finance, Ecom Finance, Controller |

While W435 handles high-level SLA billing, W461 focuses on the **itemized execution** and reconciliation of logistics and fulfillment fees between **BuildRight Logistics, Inc.** (the service provider) and **BuildRight Depot, Inc.** (the inventory owner).

## W472. BOI/PEZA/LGU Tax Incentive Monitoring & Compliance

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | New store opening in an LGU with tax incentive program; new entity registration with BOI or PEZA; annual compliance review cycle; incentive expiration approaching |
| **Frequency** | ~2–3 new incentive registrations/year (as new stores open or entities qualify); quarterly compliance monitoring; annual reporting per incentive |
| **Volume** | Potentially 5–15 active incentive registrations across 5 entities and 200+ locations (varies based on qualifying entities and LGU programs) |
| **Owner** | Tax Accountant |
| **Participants** | Tax Accountant, Controller, CFO, Legal Head, Regulatory Officer, Treasury Manager |

### Background

The model company profile (Section 10.5) explicitly lists "PEZA/BOI Incentives — If applicable" with possible tax holidays for specific operations. The Corporate Recovery and Tax Incentives for Enterprises (CREATE) Act (RA 11534) and its implementing rules offer significant tax benefits for qualifying enterprises, including: (a) **BOI registration**: Income Tax Holiday (ITH) of 4–7 years for pioneering industries or projects meeting investment thresholds; (b) **PEZA registration**: 5% Gross Income Tax (GIT) in lieu of all national and local taxes for enterprises in economic zones; (c) **LGU incentives**: Local business tax reductions or waivers (1–5 years) for new businesses in certain LGUs, especially in development zones; (d) **CREATE Law provisions**: Reduced corporate income tax rate (20% for micro/small enterprises with ≤ PHP 5B total assets; 25% for others); enhanced deductions for specific activities (training, R&D, local labor costs).

For BuildRight specifically: (a) BuildRight Logistics, Inc. may qualify for BOI registration as a logistics/distribution enterprise in priority development areas; (b) new stores in LGU-designated investment promotion areas may qualify for local tax holidays; (c) BuildRight Digital Commerce, Inc. may qualify for CREATE incentives for digital innovation activities; (d) DC locations in economic zones (e.g., Clark, Laguna) may have PEZA or LGU incentive eligibility.

Potential annual tax savings from active incentive management: **PHP 10M–50M+** depending on qualifying entities, making this a financially significant workflow.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Incentive opportunity identification**: (a) When a new store location is selected (W116), Tax Accountant reviews the LGU's investment incentive code — many LGUs offer property tax holidays, local business tax reductions, or streamlined permitting for new businesses; (b) when a new entity is being formed or a DC location is selected, Tax Accountant assesses BOI or PEZA registration eligibility; (c) Tax Accountant monitors CREATE Law implementing rules and BIR revenue regulations for new incentive categories; (d) annual: Tax Accountant conducts a full incentive landscape review for all 5 entities and 205+ locations | Tax Accountant | Controller | 2–4 hours/review |
| 2 | **Registration and qualification**: (a) For BOI: Tax Accountant prepares registration application — project description, investment amount, employment generation plan, economic impact; Legal Head reviews and submits; BOI processes registration (3–6 months); upon approval, Tax Accountant records ITH start date, duration, and conditions; (b) For PEZA: similar process with PEZA Economic Zone administration; requires physical location within a PEZA-declared zone; (c) For LGU: Tax Accountant coordinates with Regulatory Officer to include tax incentive applications in the new store LGU permitting process (W54); negotiate incentive terms with LGU Investment Promotions Office | Tax Accountant / Legal Head | CFO | 10–20 hours/registration |
| 3 | **Compliance monitoring**: (a) Tax Accountant maintains incentive compliance register per entity/location: incentive type, registration number, start date, expiration date, reporting requirements, compliance conditions (e.g., minimum employment, minimum investment, local sourcing percentage); (b) quarterly: Tax Accountant verifies compliance with all active incentive conditions — if BuildRight Logistics, Inc. is BOI-registered with a minimum employment condition, quarterly headcount at DC locations is verified against the commitment; (c) system alerts Tax Accountant 6 months and 3 months before incentive expiration — enough lead time for renewal application or tax planning for reversion to standard rates | Tax Accountant | Controller | 2 hours/quarter |
| 4 | **BIR filing integration**: (a) For entities with ITH: Tax Accountant files BIR quarterly income tax returns (1702Q) with zero or reduced tax due, attaching BOI/PEZA certificate as supporting document; system applies reduced tax rate in tax computation module (W90, W260); (b) for entities with 5% GIT: Tax Accountant files 5% GIT return in lieu of regular corporate income tax and local business tax; system must support dual tax computation — standard rate for non-qualifying entities and 5% GIT for qualifying entities; (c) for LGU incentive: local business tax computation (W54, W9A.16c) reflects reduced or zero LBT for qualifying locations | Tax Accountant | Controller | Absorbed into existing tax filing |
| 5 | **Transfer pricing impact assessment**: (a) If one entity (e.g., Logistics, Inc.) has an income tax holiday while another (Depot, Inc.) does not, transfer pricing (W235) must be documented to demonstrate arm's-length pricing — BIR scrutinizes IC transactions between incentivized and non-incentivized entities under CREATE Law; (b) Tax Accountant and Treasury Manager review IC pricing rules (W305) annually to ensure IC service fees and goods pricing do not artificially shift profit to the incentivized entity; (c) documentation per W235 (Transfer Pricing Compliance) updated to include incentive status analysis | Tax Accountant / Treasury Manager | CFO | 4 hours/year |
| 6 | **Incentive benefit tracking**: (a) Tax Accountant calculates and records tax savings per incentive per quarter: ITH savings = taxable income × standard CIT rate (25%) minus tax paid (0% during ITH); GIT savings = (standard CIT + LBT) minus 5% GIT paid; LGU savings = standard LBT minus actual LBT paid; (b) quarterly report to Controller and CFO: cumulative tax savings vs. projection, compliance status, upcoming expirations; (c) annual: CFO includes incentive benefit in management reporting (W35) — total tax optimization from active incentive management | Tax Accountant | Controller / CFO | 2 hours/quarter |
| 7 | **Expiration management**: (a) 12 months before incentive expiration: Tax Accountant assesses renewal options — some incentives are non-renewable (ITH); others may convert to enhanced deductions (5% GIT after ITH expiry for BOI); (b) 6 months before: Tax Accountant prepares tax planning for reversion to standard rates — models the financial impact on entity-level and consolidated effective tax rate; (c) upon expiration: system updates entity tax rate to standard; BIR filing (W260) reflects standard computation; LGU LBT reverts to full rate; (d) if renewal is possible: Tax Accountant prepares renewal application with updated compliance documentation | Tax Accountant | CFO | 4–8 hours/expiration event |
| 8 | **Audit readiness**: (a) Tax Accountant maintains complete incentive documentation file per entity: registration certificate, approval letter, incentive terms, annual compliance reports filed with BOI/PEZA/LGU, quarterly benefit calculations, transfer pricing documentation; (b) file available for BIR tax audit (W77) — BIR specifically audits entities with tax incentives to verify compliance; (c) file available for Internal Audit review (W349 — Corporate Tax & Statutory Reporting Audit) | Tax Accountant | Controller | Absorbed into documentation practices |

### System Touchpoints
- Entity-level tax rate configuration per W293 (Tax & Regulatory Master Data Governance) — system must support different tax rates per entity based on incentive status (W472.4)
- BIR tax return generation (W260) — returns for incentivized entities include BOI/PEZA certificate reference and reduced tax computation (W472.4a–b)
- Local business tax computation (W9A.16c, W54) — LGU incentive reduces or zeroes LBT per location (W472.4c)
- Transfer pricing module (W235, W305) — IC pricing documentation includes incentive status analysis (W472.5)
- Management reporting (W35) — tax savings from incentives reported in quarterly and annual management reports (W472.6c)
- Location master (W254) — incentive status tracked per entity and location (W472.3a)
- Integration with W90 (monthly tax filing), W260 (BIR eFPS), W234 (IC profit elimination — affected by entity-level tax rates), W319 (debt covenant compliance — ETR affects covenant calculations)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **BOI/PEZA reportorial burden**: Registered enterprises must file periodic compliance reports (annual, semi-annual, or quarterly depending on incentive type) with BOI or PEZA; failure to file on time can result in incentive cancellation; Tax Accountant must track multiple filing deadlines across different agencies
- **BIR audit targeting**: Entities claiming tax incentives are priority audit targets for BIR under CREATE Law; BIR specifically examines whether IC transactions artificially shift profit to incentivized entities — this risk is heightened for BuildRight's 5-entity structure with active IC flows (W14)
- **LGU incentive reliability**: Some LGUs grant tax incentives via local ordinance that a subsequent administration may repeal or modify; a store that opened based on a 5-year tax holiday may lose the benefit mid-stream if the LGU repeals the ordinance — no legal recourse for the taxpayer
- **Transfer pricing complexity under CREATE**: CREATE Law mandates that related-party transactions between incentivized and non-incentivized entities be priced at arm's length; BIR now requires Transfer Pricing Documentation (TPD) for all large taxpayers — BuildRight's PHP 62.3B revenue places it firmly in the large taxpayer category; TPD preparation per entity is ~PHP 200,000–500,000 in external advisor fees annually
- **Incentive expiration cliff**: When a 5-year ITH expires, the entity's effective tax rate jumps from 0% to 25% (or 20% if qualifying under CREATE); this creates a significant P&L impact that must be budgeted (W26) and communicated to the Board (W231)

### Staffing Implication
- **Tax Accountant**: Absorbs incentive identification, registration, compliance monitoring, and benefit tracking (~20–40 hours/year for active management). This is within the existing Tax Accountant role; the incentive management duties are a high-value addition to existing tax filing duties (W90, W260).
- **Controller**: Reviews quarterly compliance and benefit reports. ~2–4 hours/quarter. Absorbed into existing duties.
- **CFO**: Approves registrations and reviews annual benefit reporting. ~2–4 hours/year.
- **External tax advisor**: Engaged for BOI/PEZA registration applications and annual Transfer Pricing Documentation preparation. ~PHP 200,000–500,000/year budgeted as professional fees.

## W473. BIR Electronic Invoicing System (EIS) API Transmission & Reconciliation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Sales transaction completed at POS (W5B), Ecommerce order invoice generated (W11/W19), B2B invoice generated (W8/W165/W166), or customer refund processed (W101) |
| **Frequency** | Real-time (at transaction completion) or near-real-time batch transmission (within 72-hour BIR mandate) |
| **Volume** | ~2.8M POS transactions, ~42.9K ecommerce orders, ~3.5M line items, and ~3,500 AR invoices monthly across the 5 entities |
| **Owner** | Tax Accountant |
| **Participants** | POS Cashier, Billing Clerk, IT System Administrator, BIR EIS Portal, Integration Middleware, External CAS Consultant |

### Background

Under Bureau of Internal Revenue (BIR) Revenue Regulations No. 8-2022 and 9-2022, Large Taxpayers (including BuildRight Depot Corporation with PHP 62.3B annual revenue) are mandated to comply with the Electronic Invoicing/Receipting System (EIS). Taxpayers must issue electronic sales invoices and official receipts and transmit these transaction data in a standardized JSON format via a secure API to the BIR EIS portal in real-time or near-real-time (within three calendar days from the transaction date). Each transmission must be digitally signed, and the portal will return a unique transmission receipt or validation error.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Data Capture and Schema Validation**: Upon POS checkout or B2B invoicing, the ERP/POS validation engine checks for mandatory BIR fields (Buyer name, buyer TIN, address, product code, description, quantity, unit price, breakdown of VATable/Exempt/Zero-rated sales, VAT amount, and withholding tax); system formats the transaction details into a standard BIR JSON schema. | System | — | Immediate |
| 2 | **Digital Signing**: The ERP security module applies BuildRight's digital certificate (secured in HSM/KeyVault) to sign the JSON payload, ensuring non-repudiation. | System | IT Admin | < 1 sec |
| 3 | **API Transmission**: ERP transmission service calls the BIR EIS API endpoints (using secure TLS 1.2+ connection) to transmit the signed JSON payload. | System | IT Admin | < 3 sec |
| 4 | **BIR Acknowledgment & UUID Storage**: BIR EIS portal processes the payload, validates the schema, and returns a 200 OK response with a unique transaction reference key (UUID); ERP saves this UUID and receipt timestamp in the Local Invoice Registry linked to the original sales invoice/POS transaction. | System | — | < 2 sec |
| 5 | **Transmission Exception Handling**: If the transmission fails due to network outage, BIR portal downtime, or schema validation error: (a) system flags the transaction as "Failed/Pending"; (b) routes validation errors to the Billing Clerk for correction; (c) for network/portal issues, automatically retries every 30 minutes; (d) queues offline POS transactions for automatic transmission upon restoration. | System / Billing Clerk | Tax Accountant | Automated / Varies |
| 6 | **Fulfillment & Customer Copy Delivery**: Once the UUID is received (or queued in offline mode), the POS prints the BIR-compliant receipt with a QR code containing the BIR validation link; or the ecommerce system emails the e-invoice to the customer. | POS System / Ecommerce Engine | Store Manager | < 5 sec |
| 7 | **Daily Reconciliation & Audit Log Review**: Tax Accountant runs the e-Invoicing Transmission Report daily: compares total sales invoice count from the General Ledger (W9A) and POS sales reports against the transmitted/accepted records in the Local Invoice Registry; flags and investigates any transactions pending transmission or rejected by BIR. | Tax Accountant | Controller | 30 min/day |
| 8 | **BIR e-Invoicing Compliance Reporting**: Tax Accountant prepares monthly EIS compliance summaries, verifying that all sales records are fully accounted for in the BIR portal before filing monthly VAT returns (W90/W260). | Tax Accountant | Controller | 1–2 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints
- POS Terminals (Offline cache, invoice signature, QR code printing)
- ERP Billing & Invoicing Module (Sales invoice generation, e-invoice formatting)
- Integration Middleware / API Gateway (BIR EIS endpoint connection, TLS, handshake)
- Digital Certificate KeyVault / Hardware Security Module (HSM)
- Local Invoice Registry (Database storing JSON payloads, transmission logs, BIR responses, UUIDs)
- Integration with W5B (POS selling), W8 (AR billing), W11/W19 (ecommerce orders), W90 (monthly tax filing), W260 (eFPS filing), W216 (BIR CAS audit compliance)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **BIR Portal Downtime**: The BIR EIS portal experiences periodic downtime or validation latency, causing API timeouts and building up a backlog of pending transmissions. If the portal is down for more than 72 hours, BuildRight risks non-compliance penalties.
- **Data Invalidation Rejections**: Incomplete or invalid buyer data (e.g. incorrect TIN format for B2B trade customers) causes immediate API rejection, requiring manual correction by the Billing Clerk and re-transmission.
- **POS Offline Sync Backlog**: In the event of a store network outage, POS continues selling offline (POS-002). Upon reconnection, thousands of cached transactions must be signed and transmitted, potentially overloading the API gateway or exceeding the 3-day window if the outage is prolonged.
- **MDR/API Rate Limiting**: Sending 2.8M transactions monthly requires robust throughput; if the BIR portal limits API calls per minute, BuildRight must queue transmissions, introducing latency.

### Staffing Implication
- **Tax Accountant**: Accountable for daily reconciliation and resolving transmission errors. Requires ~10–15 hours/month of active monitoring.
- **Billing Clerk / Cashier Supervisor**: Responsible for correcting rejected transactions (e.g. updating buyer TIN/address). Requires ~5–10 hours/month depending on rejection rate.
- **IT System Administrator (Database/Integration)**: Responsible for monitoring API gateway logs, updating digital certificates, and resolving network connection failures. Requires ~4 hours/month.
- **External CAS/EIS Consultant**: Retained for annual system updates and troubleshooting BIR technical changes. ~PHP 100,000/year budgeted as professional fees.

## W475. Customer Creditable Withholding Tax (CWT) Certificate (BIR 2307) Collection & Reconciliation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | B2B/Trade customer submits payment net of withholding tax (withholds 1% or 2% Expanded Withholding Tax) |
| **Frequency** | Daily cash application; monthly collections review; quarterly tax filing |
| **Volume** | ~3,500 trade/corporate invoices monthly across 5,200 active B2B accounts |
| **Owner** | AR Accountant |
| **Participants** | B2B Customers, AR Supervisor, Collector, Tax Accountant, Controller |

### Background

When trade and corporate customers pay BuildRight Depot Corp., they withhold 1% or 2% as Creditable Withholding Tax (CWT) per BIR rules. The customer pays the net amount and is legally required to issue a BIR Form 2307 to BuildRight. The AR Accountant must place the withheld amount in a dedicated CWT clearing account in the customer's ledger. BuildRight can only clear the customer's AR balance and claim the tax credit once the physical or electronic Form 2307 is received, validated, and logged.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Payment Posting (Net)**: Upon receipt of check, bank transfer, or PDC (W423), AR Accountant posts the cash received (98% or 99% of invoice) and moves the withheld 1% or 2% to a "CWT Clearing - Customer Suspense" account under that specific customer sub-ledger, keeping the invoice partially open. | AR Accountant | AR Supervisor | 15 min |
| 2 | **Form 2307 Request**: Collector requests the BIR Form 2307 from the customer. The customer must provide it either: (a) upon payment release (for check pick-ups), or (b) quarterly (within 20 days after the end of the quarter). | Collector | AR Accountant | 10 min |
| 3 | **Document Validation**: Upon receipt, AR Accountant validates the Form 2307: (a) Correct registered name and TIN of BuildRight Depot entity, (b) Correct customer name and TIN, (c) Matching Alphanumeric Tax Code (ATC) and withholding rate (1% or 2%), (d) Matching gross sales amount and tax withheld, (e) Valid signature of customer representative. | AR Accountant | AR Supervisor | 10 min/cert |
| 4 | **ERP Logging & AR Clearance**: AR Accountant enters the Form 2307 details (Certificate Number, Customer TIN, ATC, gross amount, tax withheld) into the ERP's Tax Module; uploads the scanned certificate (W255); system automatically posts a journal entry: *Dr. Creditable Withholding Tax Asset / Cr. CWT Clearing - Customer Suspense*, fully closing the customer's outstanding invoice balance. | AR Accountant | — | 10 min |
| 5 | **Form 2307 Aging & Follow-up**: Monthly, AR Accountant runs the CWT Clearing Aging Report. For any pending certificates > 45 days, Collector sends formal demand letters to customers. Unresolved items > 90 days are blocked from further credit sales (W24/W328) until the certificate is submitted. | AR Accountant / Collector | AR Supervisor | 2 hours/month |
| 6 | **Tax Credit Claim Reconciliation**: Quarterly, Tax Accountant reconciles total balance in *Creditable Withholding Tax Asset* account against the physical/digital Form 2307 register. The total sum is claimed as a tax credit in the Quarterly Corporate Income Tax Return (BIR Form 1702Q). Only validated, physically held certificates are claimed. | Tax Accountant | Controller | 4 hours/quarter |
| 7 | **Audit Prep & Archiving**: Original Form 2307 certificates are filed and archived by the Tax Team per the 7-year retention policy (W256) for potential BIR audits (W77). | Tax Accountant | Controller | 1 hour/quarter |

### System Touchpoints
- Accounts Receivable Module (Partial payment posting, customer suspense clearing)
- ERP Tax Module (BIR Form 2307 registry, ATC mapping, tax credit ledger)
- Document Management System (W255) for scanned certificates
- Integration with W8 (B2B Accounts), W24 (Credit application), W90 (Monthly Tax Filing), W108 (Collections), W140 (Corporate Income Tax Return), W260 (eFPS filing)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Lost Tax Credits**: If customers fail to provide Form 2307, BuildRight cannot claim the credit, resulting in direct write-offs to tax expense (W81).
- **Manual Data Validation**: Manually matching thousands of paper certificates to ERP invoices is a massive bottleneck.
- **Entity Mismatches**: B2B customers often issue the Form 2307 to the wrong BuildRight legal entity (e.g. issuing to BuildRight Depot, Inc. instead of BuildRight Digital Commerce, Inc. for online sales), rendering the certificate invalid.

### Staffing Implication
- **AR Accountant**: Accountable for daily entry, matching, and logging. ~20 hours/month of active processing.
- **Collector**: Responsible for customer follow-up. ~10 hours/month.
- **Tax Accountant**: Accountable for quarterly reconciliation and BIR Form 1702Q reporting. ~4 hours/quarter.

---

## W478. BIR Annual Inventory List Submission (RMC 57-2015)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Calendar year-end close (December 31) triggers the statutory compliance requirement |
| **Frequency** | Annual (submitted on or before January 30 of the following year) |
| **Volume** | ~80,000 active SKUs across 200 stores and 4 DCs |
| **Owner** | Tax Manager |
| **Participants** | Tax Accountant, Cost Accountant, Inventory Control Manager, IT Database Administrator, CFO, External Auditor |

### Background

Under Philippine Revenue Regulations and Revenue Memorandum Circular No. 57-2015, all taxpayers maintaining inventories of stock-in-trade must submit an annual inventory list in a prescribed electronic format to the Bureau of Internal Revenue (BIR) by January 30. This list must contain details of all stock items, including quantities, unit costs, and total values, and must reconcile with the audited financial statements. The BIR enforces strict layout validation rules (specifically columns for Sheet, Item Code, Item Description, Unit of Measure, Unit Cost, Quantity, and Total Value). Failure to submit, or submitting inconsistent values, triggers substantial penalties and places the company on the BIR priority audit list.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Reconciliation & Lock**: Following year-end physical inventory counts (W42) and system close (W9B), Cost Accountant performs inventory valuation verification and posts final variance adjustments. Inventory sub-ledgers are officially closed and locked. | Cost Accountant | Inventory Control Manager | 2 days |
| 2 | **Raw Data Extraction**: IT Database Administrator runs the specialized database script to extract ending inventory quantities and Weighted Average Costs (WAC) per SKU across all warehouses, yards (W3B), and stores as of December 31, 23:59. | IT Database Administrator | CFO | 2 hours |
| 3 | **Cost Validation**: Cost Accountant reconciles extracted quantities and costs against general ledger balances. If variance is found (e.g. from goods in transit or unposted receipts), adjusts details to match the final audited trial balance. | Cost Accountant | Tax Manager | 1 day |
| 4 | **BIR Spreadsheet Formatting**: Tax Accountant converts the validated inventory files into the mandatory format prescribed by RMC 57-2015, categorizing inventory under separate schedules (e.g. Merchandising Inventory, Supplies/Consumables). | Tax Accountant | Tax Manager | 1.5 days |
| 5 | **Audit Alignment**: Tax Accountant aligns with the External Auditor to ensure ending inventory lists match the audited financial statement disclosure schedules. | Tax Accountant | CFO | 4 hours |
| 6 | **Sworn Declaration & DVD Burning**: Tax Accountant drafts the required Sworn Declaration (attesting to the accuracy of the electronic data), routing it for notarization and signatures. The formatted Excel/CSV files are written to a DVD-R or prepared for secure BIR e-submission. | Tax Accountant | CFO | 3 hours |
| 7 | **Submission & Archiving**: Tax Accountant submits the physical DVD-R and notarized Sworn Declaration to the Large Taxpayers Service (LTS) or local Revenue District Office (RDO) before January 30. Uploads the stamped-received copy to the Document Management System (W255) and updates the Compliance Calendar. | Tax Accountant | Tax Manager | 4 hours |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP Inventory Valuation Module (Sub-ledger inventory value extraction)
- ERP Tax Module (Year-end compliance reporting templates)
- Document Management System (W255) for archiving submitted declarations and receipts
- Integration with W9B (Year-End Close), W42 (Annual physical inventory), W220 (SLOB provisioning), W260 (BIR eFPS filing), W90 (Monthly Tax Filing)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Rigid Format Rules**: Any slight deviation in columns or formatting results in immediate rejection of the submission by the BIR.
- **Late Count Reconciliation**: If physical count variances (W42) are not resolved by early January, the final inventory list cannot be prepared on time.
- **WAC Variance**: Discrepancies between the inventory sub-ledger unit costs and general ledger values can trigger BIR audit queries.

### Staffing Implication
- **Tax Accountant**: Accountable for formatting, sworn declaration preparation, and filing. ~24 hours/year.
- **Cost Accountant**: Responsible for inventory reconciliation and valuation audits. ~12 hours/year.
- **IT Database Administrator**: Technical support for query execution. ~4 hours/year.
