## W486. Related Party Transaction Disclosure & Reporting (PAS 24)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Quarterly financial reporting cycle; year-end close (W9B); material related party transaction event |
| **Frequency** | Quarterly (disclosure schedule update); annual (PAS 24 note preparation for AFS) |
| **Volume** | 5 legal entities with extensive intercompany activity; ~20–30 material related party transactions/quarter |
| **Owner** | Controller |
| **Participants** | CFO, Tax Manager, Corporate Secretary, Legal Head, External Auditor |

### Background

Philippine Accounting Standard 24 (PAS 24) — Related Party Disclosures requires BuildRight to disclose all material related party relationships and transactions in its financial statement notes. With 5 legal entities under a holding company structure and significant intercompany activity (leasing from BuildRight Property Management, logistics services from BuildRight Logistics, ecommerce services from BuildRight Digital Commerce), the volume and complexity of related party disclosures is substantial. W14 covers intercompany transaction processing operationally, and W234/W235 cover transfer pricing, but neither addresses the PAS 24 disclosure and reporting requirement — the financial statement note preparation, management compensation disclosure, and key management personnel identification required by the standard.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Related Party Register Maintenance**: Controller maintains the Related Party Register: (a) identify all related parties per PAS 24 definition: 5 legal entities, key management personnel (CEO, CFO, COO, CIO, CMO, CHRO, VP Legal, Board members), entities controlled or significantly influenced by key management personnel, associates and joint ventures; (b) classify relationships: parent-subsidiary, fellow subsidiaries, key management personnel, post-employment benefit plans; (c) update register for any changes (new board members, new related entities) quarterly | Controller | CFO | 1 day/quarter |
| 2 | **Transaction Capture & Classification**: Each quarter, Controller extracts all intercompany transactions from the ERP: (a) IC revenue and expense (W14 — intercompany logistics fees, ecommerce fees, leasing income); (b) IC receivables and payables balances; (c) IC loans and interest (W137); (d) IC guarantees and contingencies (W325); (e) IC dividends (W327); (f) management compensation and benefits per key management personnel (aggregated from payroll W10); (g) classify each transaction by related party, nature of relationship, and transaction type | Controller | Tax Manager | 2–3 days/quarter |
| 3 | **Arm's-Length Pricing Verification**: Controller verifies that all material related party transactions are conducted at arm's-length terms: (a) cross-reference transfer pricing documentation (W235) for goods and services; (b) verify IC leasing rates against market benchmarks (W275 — IFRS 16 disclosures); (c) verify IC service fees against SLA billing rates (W435); (d) flag any transactions where pricing deviates significantly from arm's-length; (e) document justification for any non-arm's-length terms | Controller / Tax Manager | CFO | 2 days/quarter |
| 4 | **Quarterly Disclosure Schedule Update**: Controller updates the Related Party Disclosure Schedule: (a) tabulate all material related party transactions by type, counterparty, and amount; (b) compute outstanding balances (receivable/payable) at quarter-end; (c) compute provisions for doubtful IC receivables; (d) compute management compensation per key management personnel category (short-term employee benefits, post-employment benefits, other long-term benefits, termination benefits — per PAS 19 categories) | Controller | CFO | 1–2 days/quarter |
| 5 | **PAS 24 Note Preparation (Annual)**: For the annual AFS (W9B), Controller prepares the PAS 24 note: (a) describe the related party relationship nature; (b) disclose all material transaction types and amounts; (c) disclose outstanding balances and terms/conditions; (d) disclose provisions for doubtful IC receivables; (e) disclose key management personnel compensation (aggregated by category, not by individual — per PAS 24 paragraph 17); (f) disclose any guarantees provided or received; (g) ensure consistency with GAN filing (W481) | Controller | CFO | 3–5 days/annual |
| 6 | **External Audit Coordination**: Controller coordinates with External Auditor on PAS 24 disclosures: (a) provide Related Party Register and Disclosure Schedule; (b) respond to auditor queries on specific transactions; (c) provide supporting documentation for arm's-length pricing; (d) incorporate auditor feedback into final disclosure note | Controller | External Auditor | 2–3 days/annual |
| 7 | **Board Reporting**: CFO presents related party transaction summary to Board (W124/W482): (a) quarterly: summary of material IC transactions; (b) annual: full PAS 24 disclosure note for Board approval before AFS filing (W481) | CFO | Board | Per W124 |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP Intercompany Module (W14): IC transaction extraction
- ERP Financial Reporting: disclosure note templates
- Transfer Pricing Documentation (W235): arm's-length pricing reference
- Payroll System (W10): management compensation data extraction
- Treasury Module (W137, W325): IC loan and guarantee data
- Location/Entity Master (W254): entity relationship hierarchy
- Integration with W14 (IC transactions), W234 (IC profit elimination), W235 (transfer pricing), W435 (IC SLA billing), W9B (year-end close), W481 (SEC filings), W95/W351 (external audit)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Completeness risk**: Related party identification is only as good as the Related Party Register; if key management personnel have undisclosed related entities or if new intercompany arrangements are established without updating the register, material transactions may be omitted from disclosure — a PFRS violation that triggers SEC scrutiny
- **Management compensation sensitivity**: PAS 24 requires disclosure of key management personnel compensation in aggregate by category; while individual amounts are not disclosed, the aggregate figure can be politically sensitive in a family-owned corporation and may require Board-level approval before inclusion in the AFS
- **Arm's-length documentation burden**: Each material IC transaction requires transfer pricing documentation (W235) to support the arm's-length assertion in the PAS 24 note; for 5 entities with diverse IC flows, the documentation volume is significant
- **Intercompany balance reconciliation**: PAS 24 requires disclosure of outstanding IC balances; if IC reconciliation (W14.4–5) is not completed before quarter-end, the disclosed balances may be inconsistent between entities

### Time Estimate
- Quarterly disclosure schedule update: 3–5 days/quarter
- Annual PAS 24 note preparation: 3–5 days
- External audit coordination: 2–3 days/annual
- **Total annual**: ~25–35 days/year for Controller

### Staffing Implication
- **Controller**: ~25–35 days/year for PAS 24 compliance. Significant portion of annual workload; absorbed within existing Controller duties with support from Tax Manager.
- **Tax Manager**: ~5–10 days/year for arm's-length pricing verification support. Absorbed.
- **Corporate Secretary**: ~2 days/year for Related Party Register updates. Absorbed within W124 duties.

---

## W487. Revenue Recognition Review (PFRS 15 Complex Scenarios)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Quarterly financial close (W9A); year-end close (W9B); new revenue stream launch |
| **Frequency** | Quarterly (review); annual (comprehensive assessment) |
| **Volume** | ~10–15 complex revenue scenarios reviewed per quarter across 5 entities |
| **Owner** | Controller |
| **Participants** | CFO, VP Merchandising, VP Store Ops, VP Services, Tax Manager, External Auditor |

### Background

PFRS 15 (Revenue from Contracts with Customers) introduces a five-step revenue recognition model that requires careful assessment of complex scenarios common in BuildRight's operations. While standard POS retail sales (cash-and-carry) have straightforward revenue recognition (point of sale), several revenue streams require periodic assessment: (a) gift card breakage (W28 — when can unredeemed gift card balances be recognized as revenue?); (b) loyalty points deferred revenue (W17 — loyalty points are a separate performance obligation requiring allocation of transaction price); (c) bundled goods and services (W138 + W5B — installation services bundled with product sales require allocation); (d) consignment revenue timing (W23 — revenue recognized at sell-through, not at receipt); (e) layaway revenue recognition (W75 — revenue at delivery vs. at deposit); (f) subscription billing for recurring services (W282 — revenue over service period); (g) vendor rebates and incentives (W27 — reduction of cost of goods sold vs. revenue?); (h) project-based sales with milestone billing (W165 — over time vs. at a point in time). Existing workflows cover the operational execution of these revenue streams but not the PFRS 15 accounting assessment.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Revenue Stream Inventory**: Controller maintains a register of all revenue streams requiring PFRS 15 assessment: (a) standard retail POS sales (straightforward — recognized at POS); (b) gift card sales and redemptions (W28); (c) loyalty points earning (W17); (d) bundled product + installation (W138); (e) consignment sell-through (W23); (f) layaway sales (W75); (g) subscription services (W282); (h) project-based sales with staged billing (W165); (i) vendor rebates and co-op advertising (W27, W153); (j) tool rental (W139); (k) marketplace commissions (W180) | Controller | CFO | 1 day/annual |
| 2 | **Performance Obligation Assessment**: For each complex revenue stream, Controller assesses: (a) identify distinct performance obligations in each arrangement; (b) determine standalone selling price allocation (SSP) for each obligation; (c) determine revenue recognition timing (at a point in time vs. over time); (d) document the accounting treatment and rationale per PFRS 15 | Controller | CFO | 2–3 days/annual |
| 3 | **Deferred Revenue Calculation**: Each quarter, Controller calculates deferred revenue balances: (a) **Gift cards**: outstanding unredeemed gift card balance (W28); estimate breakage rate based on historical redemption patterns; recognize breakage revenue for gift cards where likelihood of redemption is remote (per PFRS 15 paragraph B43); (b) **Loyalty points**: calculate total outstanding loyalty points liability (W104); allocate transaction price to points earned; adjust deferred revenue for points expiration and forfeiture; (c) **Layaway deposits**: review outstanding layaway agreements (W75) where customer has paid deposit but not taken delivery; assess whether revenue should be recognized; (d) **Subscription services**: review deferred revenue from pre-paid service contracts (W282); recognize revenue over service delivery period | Controller | CFO | 2–3 days/quarter |
| 4 | **Bundle Allocation Review**: Each quarter, Controller reviews bundled revenue allocations: (a) product + installation bundles: verify SSP allocation between product (recognized at delivery/POS) and installation service (recognized at installation completion); (b) promotional bundles (W13): verify that the bundle discount is allocated proportionally across all items; (c) tool rental + maintenance: separate rental revenue (over time) from maintenance revenue (as incurred) | Controller | VP Services | 1 day/quarter |
| 5 | **Consignment Revenue Timing Verification**: Each quarter, Controller verifies consignment revenue recognition: (a) confirm that consignment goods remain vendor-owned until sold (W23); (b) verify that revenue is recognized only at point of customer sale (not at store receipt); (c) reconcile consignment inventory balance (vendor-owned) with consignment revenue recognized; (d) verify consignment settlement timing matches sell-through reports | Controller | VP Merchandising | 1 day/quarter |
| 6 | **Journal Entry Posting**: Controller posts quarterly PFRS 15 adjustment journal entries: (a) gift card breakage revenue recognition; (b) loyalty points deferred revenue adjustment; (c) bundle SSP allocation corrections; (d) subscription deferred revenue amortization; (e) any revenue timing corrections identified during review; all entries posted during month-end close (W9A) with supporting schedules | Controller | CFO | 1 day/quarter |
| 7 | **Annual Comprehensive Review**: At year-end (W9B): (a) review all PFRS 15 accounting policies for continued appropriateness; (b) recalculate breakage rates, SSP allocations, and deferred revenue balances using full-year data; (c) coordinate with External Auditor on PFRS 15 audit approach and sampling; (d) prepare PFRS 15 disclosure note for AFS (including disaggregated revenue by nature, timing, and transfer point); (e) assess any new revenue streams launched during the year (e.g., new marketplace channels, new service offerings) for PFRS 15 treatment | Controller / External Auditor | CFO | 5–7 days/annual |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP Revenue Module: revenue stream tracking, deferred revenue ledger
- Gift Card Module (W28): outstanding balance, redemption history, breakage calculation
- Loyalty Engine (W17/W104): points liability, deferred revenue allocation
- POS System (W5B): transaction-level data for bundle analysis
- Consignment Module (W23): sell-through reporting
- Layaway Module (W75): outstanding agreements and deposit tracking
- Subscription Billing Module (W282): deferred revenue schedule
- Integration with W9A (month-end close), W9B (year-end close), W28 (gift cards), W17 (loyalty), W104 (loyalty financial governance), W23 (consignment), W75 (layaway), W138 (installation), W165 (project billing), W282 (subscriptions)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Loyalty points deferred revenue complexity**: With 600,000 loyalty members earning points on every transaction, the deferred revenue liability is significant and dynamically changing; calculating the standalone selling price of loyalty points (which have a nominal cash value but drive customer behavior) requires actuarial-style estimation that is difficult to audit
- **Gift card breakage estimation**: PFRS 15 requires that breakage revenue be recognized only when the likelihood of redemption is remote; estimating the breakage rate for a relatively new gift card program (BuildRight launched gift cards recently) is based on limited historical data, creating estimation uncertainty
- **Bundle allocation subjectivity**: Determining standalone selling prices for installation services (which are never sold separately) requires significant judgment; if the External Auditor disagrees with the SSP methodology, it could result in a prior-period adjustment
- **New revenue stream risk**: As BuildRight launches new services (marketplace integration W180, subscription services W282, retail media network W153), each new revenue stream requires a fresh PFRS 15 assessment before revenue can be recognized; delays in assessment can result in deferred revenue piling up or premature revenue recognition

### Time Estimate
- Quarterly review and journal entries: 4–7 days/quarter
- Annual comprehensive review: 5–7 days
- **Total annual**: ~25–35 days/year for Controller

### Staffing Implication
- **Controller**: ~25–35 days/year for PFRS 15 review. Absorbed within existing Controller duties with support from CFO.
- **External Auditor**: PFRS 15 review is a standard audit procedure. No incremental cost.

---

## W488. Credit Card Chargeback Dispute Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Chargeback notification from acquiring bank or card processor |
| **Frequency** | ~150–250 chargebacks/month (estimated ~0.005–0.01% of ~1M monthly card transactions) |
| **Volume** | ~150–250 disputes/month; average chargeback value: PHP 3,000–8,000 |
| **Owner** | Finance — Payment Settlement Specialist |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Cashier, LP Analyst, Customer Service, Card Processor/Acquiring Bank |

### Background

With approximately 36% of BuildRight's 2.8 million monthly POS transactions conducted via credit or debit card (~1 million card transactions/month), credit card chargebacks represent a significant operational and financial exposure. Cardholders can initiate chargebacks for various reasons: fraudulent transactions (unauthorized card use), disputed transactions (customer claims they did not make the purchase or did not receive goods), duplicate charges, incorrect amounts, or credit not processed (returns not refunded). The chargeback process follows card network rules (Visa, Mastercard, JCB) with strict response deadlines (typically 7–30 days depending on reason code). W99 covers payment settlement reconciliation broadly and W261 covers e-wallet settlement, but neither addresses the card chargeback dispute lifecycle — representment preparation, evidence gathering, and arbitration.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Chargeback Notification Receipt**: Payment Settlement Specialist receives chargeback notification from acquiring bank (BDO, BPI, or processor): (a) chargeback case number, card network (Visa/MC/JCB), reason code, transaction date, transaction amount, cardholder claim; (b) acquiring bank debits BuildRight's settlement account for the chargeback amount (provisional debit); (c) log chargeback in chargeback tracking system with response deadline | Payment Settlement Specialist | Finance Manager | 15 min/case |
| 2 | **Transaction Investigation**: Payment Settlement Specialist pulls transaction details: (a) POS transaction record (transaction number, store, terminal ID, cashier ID, date/time, items purchased, amount); (b) signed/EMV chip card receipt or contactless payment confirmation; (c) CCTV footage if fraud alleged (via W71 — coordinate with store security); (d) customer lookup in loyalty system if card is linked to loyalty account; (e) check for related return/exchange records (W12A/W12B) if credit-not-processed claim | Payment Settlement Specialist | Store Manager | 30 min/case |
| 3 | **Chargeback Assessment**: Payment Settlement Specialist determines response strategy: (a) **Accept**: if investigation confirms legitimate chargeback (e.g., proven duplicate charge, unauthorized use with no EMV/chip read); (b) **Represent (Fight)**: if evidence supports that transaction was legitimate (e.g., EMV chip read, signed receipt, CCTV shows cardholder, loyalty account match); (c) **Partial Accept**: for multi-item transactions where only some items are disputed | Payment Settlement Specialist | Finance Manager | 15 min/case |
| 4 | **Representment Package Preparation**: For chargebacks being contested, Payment Settlement Specialist compiles representment evidence: (a) transaction receipt (signed or EMV-verified); (b) POS system transaction detail; (c) delivery proof (for large items with home delivery W5D); (d) return/exchange history showing customer did not return the item; (e) loyalty account activity showing the cardholder's typical purchasing pattern; (f) CCTV screenshot showing cardholder at POS (if available and permitted by data privacy W271); (g) any customer communication acknowledging the purchase; (h) compile into card network-specific representment format | Payment Settlement Specialist | Finance Manager | 30–60 min/case |
| 5 | **Representment Submission**: Payment Settlement Specialist submits representment through acquiring bank's chargeback portal: (a) upload evidence package before deadline; (b) acquiring bank forwards to issuing bank; (c) issuing bank reviews and either: reverses the chargeback (BuildRight wins — credit back to account) or maintains the chargeback (cardholder wins); (d) complex cases may escalate to second chargeback or pre-arbitration | Payment Settlement Specialist | — | 15 min/case |
| 6 | **Resolution & Reconciliation**: Payment Settlement Specialist records the outcome: (a) **Chargeback Reversed**: credit received from acquiring bank; reconcile against original debit in settlement reconciliation (W99); (b) **Chargeback Upheld**: debit is finalized; write off as chargeback expense; no further action unless pattern detected; (c) **Pre-Arbitration/Loss**: for high-value disputes (>PHP 50,000), escalate to Finance Manager for arbitration decision; arbitration fees may exceed disputed amount for smaller cases | Payment Settlement Specialist | Finance Manager | 15 min/case |
| 7 | **Pattern Analysis & Prevention**: Monthly, Payment Settlement Specialist analyzes chargeback trends: (a) chargeback rate by store, by reason code, by card network; (b) identify stores with chargeback rates exceeding 0.01% (card network threshold for monitoring programs); (c) identify recurring fraud patterns (specific SKUs, specific terminal, specific cashier); (d) recommend preventive actions: enhanced cashier training (W51), terminal EMV enforcement, fraud alert rules; (e) escalate suspicious patterns to LP (W37, W466); (f) report chargeback metrics in monthly payment settlement reconciliation report (W99) | Payment Settlement Specialist | Finance Manager | 4 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints
- Card Acquiring Bank Portal: chargeback notification, representment submission, status tracking
- POS Transaction Lookup: pull transaction details by date/store/amount/card number (masked)
- CCTV System (W71): footage retrieval for fraud dispute evidence
- Loyalty System (W17): cardholder identification
- Returns/Exchange System (W12A/W12B): cross-reference for credit-not-processed claims
- Chargeback Tracking System: case management, deadline tracking, outcome recording
- Settlement Reconciliation Module (W99): chargeback debit/credit matching
- Integration with W99 (payment settlement reconciliation), W261 (e-wallet settlement), W37 (loss prevention), W466 (LPAP), W71 (CCTV), W5B (POS selling)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Tight representment deadlines**: Card networks allow 7–30 days to respond to chargebacks; gathering evidence (especially CCTV footage from provincial stores and cashier statements) within this window is operationally challenging, especially for stores in remote locations
- **CCTV retention limitation**: At 30 days online / 90 days archived (per W71), CCTV evidence may be unavailable for chargebacks initiated 60+ days after the transaction (common for "goods not received" or "credit not processed" reason codes)
- **Chargeback rate monitoring**: Card networks (Visa, Mastercard) monitor merchant chargeback rates; if BuildRight's rate exceeds the network threshold (typically 0.75–1.0% of transactions), the company may enter a monitoring program with escalating fines and potential loss of card acceptance privileges — catastrophic for a retailer where 36% of transactions are card-based
- **Provincial store evidence gaps**: Stores in remote areas may have poor CCTV quality, inconsistent receipt retention, or limited internet connectivity for evidence upload, reducing representment success rates
- **EMV liability shift**: If a counterfeit or stolen card is used at a terminal that is not EMV chip-enabled (or the chip is bypassed), BuildRight bears the fraud liability; ensuring all 600 POS terminals are chip-enabled and EMV enforcement is active is critical

### Time Estimate
- Per-chargeback processing: ~60–90 minutes average (investigation + representment + reconciliation)
- Monthly pattern analysis: ~4 hours
- **Total**: ~250–375 hours/month for 150–250 chargebacks; requires dedicated Payment Settlement Specialist

### Staffing Implication
- **Payment Settlement Specialist**: ~250–375 hours/month across 150–250 chargebacks. This is a full-time dedicated function within the Finance team.
- **Store Manager**: Provides cashier statements and facilitates CCTV retrieval. ~15–30 min/case when evidence needed. Absorbed with minor impact.
- **LP Analyst**: Supports fraud pattern investigation for suspicious chargeback clusters. ~4–8 hours/month.

---

## W489. Store-Level Operating Budget & Cost Control

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Annual budget preparation cycle (W26); monthly cost review; mid-year budget revision |
| **Frequency** | Annual (budget creation); monthly (actual vs. budget review); quarterly (forecast update) |
| **Volume** | 200 stores + 4 DCs; each location has an operating budget |
| **Owner** | Finance — FP&A Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Regional Manager, VP Store Ops, Controller, CFO, DC Manager |

### Background

W26 covers the enterprise-level annual budget preparation and monthly variance review. However, with 200 stores and 4 DCs, BuildRight needs a structured store-level operating budget workflow that empowers Store Managers with budget ownership and cost accountability. Store-level budgets cover: (a) store payroll and labor costs (W34 scheduling directly impacts this); (b) store utilities (electricity, water — W111); (c) store maintenance and repair (W47); (d) store supplies and consumables; (e) store-level petty cash (W25); (f) store security costs (W71); (g) shrinkage and loss provision (W248, W466); (h) store-specific marketing costs (local promotions, signage). Store Managers need budget visibility, cost control tools, and accountability for their location's P&L contribution. W67 covers monthly store performance review but does not include the budget creation and cost control framework.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Budget Template Distribution**: FP&A Manager distributes store-level budget templates to Regional Managers: (a) template includes: revenue targets (top-down from W26), cost categories (payroll, utilities, maintenance, supplies, security, shrinkage provision), and capital requests (minor store improvements < PHP 500K); (b) templates are pre-populated with prior-year actuals and known cost drivers (e.g., minimum wage increase per region, utility rate changes); (c) each Regional Manager cascades to their ~40 Store Managers | FP&A Manager | CFO | 1 week |
| 2 | **Store Manager Budget Input**: Store Manager reviews template and provides input: (a) validate revenue target against local market conditions (construction activity in area, competitor presence); (b) estimate labor hours needed based on sales forecast and shift schedule (W34); (c) flag known upcoming costs (lease escalation per W117, planned maintenance per W47, security upgrade); (d) submit minor capital requests with justification; (e) submit budget to Regional Manager for review | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 2–3 days/store |
| 3 | **Regional Consolidation & Review**: Regional Manager reviews budgets for their ~40 stores: (a) verify reasonableness of cost assumptions (labor ratios, utility benchmarks per sqm); (b) identify outliers (stores with cost growth exceeding revenue growth); (c) resolve conflicts between stores competing for shared resources; (d) consolidate regional budget and submit to FP&A | Regional Manager | VP Store Ops | 1 week/region |
| 4 | **FP&A Consolidation & Approval**: FP&A consolidates all 200 store and 4 DC operating budgets: (a) verify total store-level costs align with enterprise budget (W26); (b) identify stores requiring cost reduction targets; (c) approve or adjust individual store budgets; (d) obtain CFO approval for final store-level budgets; (e) load approved budgets into ERP budget module by cost center per location | FP&A Manager | CFO | 1–2 weeks |
| 5 | **Monthly Cost Review**: Store Manager reviews monthly actual vs. budget: (a) FP&A generates store-level variance report (actual vs. budget by cost category); (b) Store Manager reviews and provides explanations for material variances (>5% or >PHP 50K); (c) identify cost overruns requiring corrective action; (d) submit variance explanations to Regional Manager | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 2–4 hours/store/month |
| 6 | **Cost Control Action**: For stores with material budget overruns: (a) Regional Manager and Store Manager agree on corrective actions: reduce overtime (W34 scheduling adjustment), renegotiate local service contracts (W47 maintenance), implement utility conservation measures (W111), tighten shrinkage controls (W248, W466); (b) corrective actions documented with target completion dates; (c) FP&A tracks corrective action implementation in monthly review | Regional Manager / Store Manager | VP Store Ops | 1–2 days/store/issue |
| 7 | **Quarterly Forecast Update**: Each quarter, FP&A updates store-level forecasts: (a) adjust revenue forecasts based on actual performance; (b) update cost forecasts for known changes (new minimum wage rates, utility rate adjustments, lease escalations); (c) identify stores at risk of missing annual budget; (d) recommend mid-year corrective actions (cost reduction, schedule optimization, store closure evaluation per W45); (e) update enterprise forecast (W26) with consolidated store-level revisions | FP&A Manager | CFO | 1 week/quarter |
| 8 | **Annual Performance Assessment**: At year-end, FP&A prepares store-level budget performance summary: (a) actual vs. budget by store (revenue, gross margin, controllable expenses, store contribution margin); (b) rank stores by performance; (c) identify top-performing and bottom-performing stores; (d) feed into Store Manager performance review (W72) and monthly store performance review (W67); (e) inform next year's budget template with updated benchmarks | FP&A Manager | CFO | 1–2 weeks/annual |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP Budget Module: store-level budget entry, loading, variance reporting
- ERP Financial Reporting: store P&L by cost center (W35, W67)
- ERP Cost Center Accounting: actual cost tracking per location
- Payroll Module (W10/W34): labor cost tracking per store
- Facility Maintenance Module (W47): maintenance cost tracking per store
- Utility Management (W111): utility cost tracking per store
- LP Module (W248, W466): shrinkage cost tracking per store
- Integration with W26 (enterprise budget), W67 (store performance review), W72 (employee performance), W34 (shift scheduling), W47 (facility maintenance), W111 (utility management)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Store Manager financial literacy**: Many Store Managers in Philippine retail come from operations backgrounds and may lack the financial acumen to effectively manage a P&L budget; without adequate training (W51), budgets become a compliance exercise rather than a cost control tool
- **Uncontrollable cost allocation**: Some costs allocated to store budgets (e.g., regional overhead allocation, corporate cost allocation, lease charges from BuildRight Property Management) are beyond the Store Manager's control, creating frustration and gaming of the variance explanation process
- **Regional benchmark fairness**: Comparing store budgets across regions is complicated by vastly different operating environments — a Metro Manila store has higher labor costs and rent but higher revenue, while a Mindanao provincial store has lower costs but lower revenue; benchmarking must account for these structural differences
- **200-store consolidation complexity**: Consolidating, reviewing, and approving 200+ individual store budgets within the annual budget cycle (W26) creates a compressed timeline that limits the depth of review for each store

### Time Estimate
- Annual budget creation: ~4–6 weeks elapsed for full store-level budget cycle
- Monthly cost review: ~2–4 hours per store per month (Store Manager); ~8 hours/month consolidation (FP&A)
- Quarterly forecast update: ~1 week (FP&A)
- **Total annual**: ~200 hours for FP&A; ~50–80 hours/year per Store Manager

### Staffing Implication
- **FP&A Manager**: ~200 hours/year for store-level budget management. Significant but feasible within existing FP&A role.
- **Regional Managers**: ~40–60 hours/year for regional budget review and consolidation. Absorbed.
- **Store Managers**: ~50–80 hours/year for budget input and monthly review. Absorbed into store management duties.

## W498. Vendor Advance Payment & Prepayment Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Import vendor requires advance payment before shipment; or vendor agreement specifies prepayment terms |
| **Frequency** | ~30–50 advance payments/month (primarily for import vendors) |
| **Volume** | ~40 average/month; peaks during seasonal buy periods (+30%) |
| **Owner** | AP Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Buyer, Treasury Analyst, AP Clerk, Controller |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Buyer requests advance payment linked to approved import PO; specifies amount (percentage or fixed), vendor bank details, and expected shipment date | Buyer | AP Supervisor | 10 min |
| 2 | System validates: PO is approved, vendor is active, advance amount within PO value, vendor bank details verified against vendor master | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | System routes advance payment request through approval workflow: ≤PHP 500K → AP Supervisor; ≤PHP 2M → Controller; >PHP 2M → CFO | System | CFO (tiered) | 5 min review |
| 4 | AP Supervisor or Treasury Analyst executes bank transfer via electronic banking (W320); system records advance payment with link to PO | Treasury Analyst | AP Supervisor | 10 min |
| 5 | System posts journal entry: Dr. Vendor Advance (asset) / Cr. Bank; advance tracked separately from regular AP balance | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | System monitors advance aging: advance outstanding >30 days past expected shipment date → alert to Buyer; >60 days → escalation to AP Supervisor | System | AP Supervisor | Automated |
| 7 | At goods receipt against the PO: system automatically applies advance payment against vendor invoice (partial or full settlement) | System | — | Automated |
| 8 | If advance exceeds invoice: system creates vendor credit balance; AP Clerk decides on refund request or application to next PO | AP Clerk | AP Supervisor | 10 min |
| 9 | Monthly: AP Supervisor reviews advance aging report; follows up on unapplied advances >60 days; escalates to Controller for potential write-off | AP Supervisor | Controller | 1 hour/month |

### System Touchpoints
- Advance payment request linked to approved PO (W2, W2B)
- Tiered approval workflow with amount-based routing (FIN-041)
- Bank transfer execution via electronic banking module (W320)
- Vendor Advance asset account tracking separate from AP (FIN-001)
- Automatic advance application at goods receipt / invoice matching (W7)
- Advance aging dashboard with escalation alerts (FIN-041)
- Integration with LC management (W232) — advances are alternative to LC for trusted vendors

### Time Estimate
~30 minutes per advance payment (request + approval + execution). At ~40/month = ~20 staff-hours/month. Absorbed by AP Clerk and Treasury Analyst within existing FTE.

### Pain Points / Risks
- Vendor ships partial quantity after receiving full advance — advance overpayment risk requires manual reconciliation
- Vendor defaults after receiving advance — no goods received and advance must be recovered through legal channels; mitigated by vendor risk tiering (W491)
- Exchange rate fluctuation between advance payment date and invoice date for import advances in USD — FX gain/loss must be posted (FIN-014)

### Staffing Implication
- Absorbed within existing AP Clerk and Treasury Analyst FTEs. No incremental headcount.
- Monthly advance aging review adds ~1 hour to AP Supervisor's monthly workload.

## W499. BIR Percentage Tax Computation & Payment

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Quarterly percentage tax filing deadline (BIR Form 2551Q, due within 25 days after end of quarter) |
| **Frequency** | Quarterly per entity; annual monitoring |
| **Volume** | Up to 5 entities × 4 quarters = 20 filings/year; most entities are VAT-registered so percentage tax applies only to specific revenue streams |
| **Owner** | Tax Accountant |
| **Participants** | Controller, Treasury Analyst |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System computes quarterly gross receipts per entity; identifies revenue streams subject to percentage tax (3%) vs. VAT (12%) based on tax master configuration (W293) | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | Tax Accountant reviews percentage tax computation: validates revenue classification, confirms entity registration status (VAT vs. non-VAT), checks threshold monitoring | Tax Accountant | Controller | 30 min |
| 3 | System generates BIR Form 2551Q per entity with required fields: gross receipts, exempt amounts, tax base, percentage tax due, prior quarter payments | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | Tax Accountant reviews generated form; validates against GL revenue accounts and supporting schedules; identifies any misclassified transactions | Tax Accountant | Controller | 30 min |
| 5 | Controller approves percentage tax return | Controller | Controller | 15 min |
| 6 | Tax Accountant submits via BIR eFPS (W260); generates Payment Reference Number (PRN) | Tax Accountant | Controller | 15 min |
| 7 | Treasury Analyst processes payment via Authorized Agent Bank (AAB) or electronic payment | Treasury Analyst | Controller | 10 min |
| 8 | System posts payment: Dr. Percentage Tax Expense / Cr. Bank | System | — | Automated |
| 9 | Annual: Tax Accountant monitors VAT registration threshold per entity (currently PHP 3M annual gross receipts); if entity approaches threshold, escalates to Controller for VAT registration evaluation | Tax Accountant | Controller | 30 min/year |

### System Touchpoints
- Revenue stream tax classification in tax master (W293, FIN-042)
- Automated percentage tax computation per entity per quarter (FIN-042)
- BIR Form 2551Q generation with BIR-prescribed format (FIN-008)
- eFPS submission integration (W260)
- VAT threshold monitoring dashboard per entity (FIN-042)
- GL posting for percentage tax payment (FIN-001)

### Time Estimate
~2 hours per quarter for all entities combined (computation + review + filing + payment). Annual total: ~8 staff-hours. Absorbed by Tax Accountant within existing FTE.

### Pain Points / Risks
- Revenue misclassification between VAT and percentage tax — leads to underpayment penalties if not caught during review
- Entity threshold crossing mid-year — if a previously non-VAT entity exceeds PHP 3M threshold, mandatory VAT registration creates retroactive compliance requirements
- BIR eFPS downtime near filing deadline — manual filing with AAB as fallback

### Staffing Implication
- Fully absorbed within existing Tax Accountant and Controller FTEs. No incremental headcount.
- Adds ~2 hours per quarter to Tax Accountant's workload.

## W500. Transfer Order In-Transit Damage Claim & Resolution

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Receiving location (store or DC) discovers damaged goods during transfer order receipt |
| **Frequency** | ~80–120 damage incidents/month across all DC→Store and Store→Store transfers |
| **Volume** | ~100 average/month; ~1,200/year; higher during rainy season (+20%) |
| **Owner** | Logistics Supervisor (DC-originated) / Store Manager (store-originated) |
| **Participants** | Receiving Clerk, Logistics Supervisor, Carrier Account Manager, Insurance Coordinator, AP Clerk |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Receiving Clerk identifies damaged items during transfer order goods receipt; documents damage with photos (minimum 3: overview, close-up of damage, shipping label); records damage in system against transfer order line | Receiving Clerk | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 2 | System captures: damage quantity, damage severity (minor — saleable at discount; major — unsaleable but salvageable; total loss), transfer order number, originating location, carrier, driver name | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | System identifies responsible party based on carrier assignment on transfer order: owned fleet → Logistics Supervisor investigates; 3PL carrier → carrier claim process initiated; inter-DC → DC operations investigates | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | Logistics Supervisor reviews damage report and photos; confirms damage classification; determines if insurance claim applies (shipments above PHP 50K declared value or insured cargo) | Logistics Supervisor | Logistics Supervisor | 15 min |
| 5 | If carrier-responsible: system generates carrier damage claim notification with evidence package (photos, transfer order, delivery receipt); sends to carrier with 10-day response SLA | Logistics Supervisor | — | 15 min |
| 6 | If insurance-applicable: Insurance Coordinator initiates insurance claim (W59) with supporting documentation | Insurance Coordinator | Controller | 30 min |
| 7 | System adjusts inventory at receiving location: minor damage → inventory status changed to "damaged - pending disposition"; major/total loss → inventory written off with transfer order reference | System | — | Automated |
| 8 | AP Clerk processes carrier chargeback or credit: if carrier accepts liability → debit memo to carrier (reduces carrier AP); if carrier disputes → escalation to Logistics Supervisor for resolution | AP Clerk | AP Supervisor | 20 min |
| 9 | Monthly: Logistics Supervisor reviews damage trend dashboard — damage rate by carrier, route, product category, and severity; identifies chronic carriers or problem routes for corrective action | Logistics Supervisor | VP Supply Chain | 1 hour/month |

### System Touchpoints
- Damage capture during transfer order goods receipt with photo attachment (INV-021)
- Damage severity classification with automated inventory status update (INV-021, INV-001)
- Carrier damage claim generation with evidence package (INV-021)
- Insurance claim integration (W59, FIN-023)
- Inventory adjustment linked to damage claim reference (W92, INV-021)
- Carrier chargeback / debit memo processing (W7, FIN-004)
- Damage trend analytics dashboard by carrier, route, category (INV-021)

### Time Estimate
~30 minutes per damage incident (documentation + classification + claim initiation). At ~100/month = ~50 staff-hours/month. Absorbed by Receiving Clerks and Logistics Supervisors within existing FTE.

### Pain Points / Risks
- Damage not discovered at receiving — if store accepts goods without inspection and discovers damage later, carrier claim is difficult to prove
- Carrier disputes liability — delays resolution and inventory remains in damaged status; mitigated by photographic evidence at receiving
- High-value damage on uninsured shipments — company absorbs full loss; mitigated by carrier liability clauses in 3PL contracts (W62B)

### Staffing Implication
- Absorbed within existing Receiving Clerk and Logistics Supervisor FTEs across 4 DCs and 200 stores.
- Monthly damage review adds ~1 hour to Logistics Supervisor's monthly workload.
- Insurance Coordinator involvement for ~10% of incidents (insured shipments).

## W556. AP Payment Run & Batch Processing Execution

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Weekly/biweekly payment schedule (e.g., every Friday and last business day of month); ad hoc urgent payments |
| **Frequency** | 2 scheduled payment runs per week; ~1 ad hoc urgent run per week |
| **Volume** | ~400–600 vendor payments per scheduled run (~800–1,200/week); total monthly AP payments ~4,000–5,000 |
| **Owner** | AP Supervisor / Treasury Analyst |
| **Participants** | AP Specialist (R), AP Supervisor (A), Treasury Analyst (R for cash positioning), Finance Manager (A for large payments > PHP 1M), Bank (payment execution) |

### Background

W7 covers accounts payable vendor invoice processing including 3-way matching, approval, and posting. W7C covers non-PO/recurring expense invoice processing. W7D covers AP vendor statement reconciliation. W30 covers daily treasury and cash position management. However, no workflow covers the actual AP payment run execution — the weekly/biweekly process of selecting invoices for payment, optimizing payment timing against cash position and discount opportunities, generating payment files, transmitting to banks, and reconciling the payment batch. For a company with ~8,500–9,500 monthly AP invoices (merchandise + non-PO), ~1,000 vendors, and PHP 42–45B annual COGS, the payment run is a high-volume, high-value operation requiring precision in cash positioning, payment term optimization, and bank integration. Philippine banking conventions (PESONet, InstaPay, check issuance for certain vendors) add complexity.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | AP Supervisor initiates payment run in ERP: selects payment date, payment method filters (bank transfer, PESONet, check), vendor grouping options (by vendor, by due date, by payment terms); system presents payment proposal screen with all eligible invoices | AP Supervisor | — | 15 min/run |
| 2 | System auto-selects invoices meeting payment criteria: (a) due date within payment window (payment date ± 5 business days); (b) fully approved per W7 workflow (no pending approvals); (c) not on payment hold or dispute per W244; (d) vendor bank details validated and active; system calculates total payment proposal amount and displays summary by payment method | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | System applies payment term optimization: (a) early payment discount capture — invoices eligible for discount per W324 (dynamic discounting) are flagged with discount amount, discount deadline, and annualized ROI; (b) net due date calculation per W295 (payment terms master) — system groups invoices by payment term category; (c) discount priority ranking: discount invoices sorted by annualized ROI descending | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | Treasury Analyst reviews proposed payment total against cash position per W30: (a) total payment proposal amount vs. available bank balances; (b) upcoming cash inflows (AR collections, credit card settlements) vs. other cash outflows (payroll per W10, tax payments per W90, capex per W21); (c) FX payment requirements for import vendors (USD account balance); Treasury Analyst confirms sufficient liquidity or recommends payment prioritization | Treasury Analyst | Finance Manager | 30 min/run |
| 5 | If cash shortfall identified: Treasury Analyst recommends payment prioritization to AP Supervisor — (a) Tier 1: critical vendors (utilities, rent, tax, single-source suppliers) and discount opportunity invoices; (b) Tier 2: standard-term vendors within due date window; (c) Tier 3: vendors with extended terms (Net 60, Net 90) that can be deferred to next run; AP Supervisor adjusts payment proposal accordingly | Treasury Analyst / AP Supervisor | Finance Manager | 30 min/run (if shortfall) |
| 6 | AP Supervisor adjusts payment proposal: add/remove individual invoices, split payments across payment dates, apply partial payments for disputed invoices (pro-rata per W244 resolution), override payment method for specific vendors; system recalculates totals after each adjustment | AP Supervisor | — | 15–30 min/run |
| 7 | AP Supervisor submits final payment proposal for approval; system generates payment batch summary: total amount, number of invoices, number of vendors, payment method breakdown, discount capture summary | AP Supervisor | — | 5 min/run |
| 8 | Finance Manager reviews and approves payment batch: (a) mandatory approval for batch totals > PHP 10M or individual payments > PHP 1M; (b) reviews discount capture opportunities accepted/rejected; (c) verifies cash position concurrence from Treasury Analyst; (d) approves or returns batch with comments for adjustment | Finance Manager | Finance Manager | 15–30 min/run |
| 9 | System generates payment files per bank format requirements: (a) BDO — PESONet batch file format (XML); (b) BPI — PESONet batch file format (CSV); (c) Metrobank — PESONet batch file format (specific template); (d) Chinabank — PESONet batch file format; (e) check run — generates check printing file with MICR line data; system validates file format against bank schema before generation | System | — | Automated |
| 10 | AP Specialist transmits payment files to banks via secure channel per W320 (electronic banking security): (a) PESONet files uploaded to each bank's corporate banking portal; (b) transmission confirmation receipt captured and filed; (c) for check payments: print checks on pre-numbered security stock per W317; (d) verify PESONet batch acceptance/rejection status within 2 hours of submission | AP Specialist | AP Supervisor | 30–45 min/run |
| 11 | For check payments: AP Specialist prints checks, obtains dual signatures per W317 signatory management (signatory A: Finance Manager or above; signatory B: Controller or CFO — both required for amounts > PHP 500K); mails or arranges courier delivery per vendor instructions; tracks check delivery confirmation | AP Specialist | Finance Manager | 1–2 hours/run (if checks included) |
| 12 | System posts payment transactions: (a) clears AP invoices — Dr. Accounts Payable / Cr. Bank (or Cash in Transit for PESONet pending); (b) updates vendor balances; (c) updates cash position per W30; (d) captures early payment discounts taken — Dr. Accounts Payable / Cr. Purchase Discount; (e) generates payment confirmation report with check numbers or PESONet reference numbers | System | — | Automated |
| 13 | Payment confirmation reconciliation: within 2 business days, AP Specialist matches bank debit advices against payment batch — (a) PESONet: verify batch settlement amount matches payment total; (b) checks: verify cleared checks against issued check register; (c) discrepancies investigated and resolved per W89 bank reconciliation; (d) uncleared checks > 30 days flagged for follow-up | AP Specialist | AP Supervisor | 30 min/run |
| 14 | Exception handling: (a) returned payments (wrong account number, closed account) — AP Specialist contacts vendor for corrected bank details, reprocesses payment in next run; (b) rejected PESONet files — correct file format errors per bank rejection reason, resubmit same day; (c) stale-dated checks (uncashed after 6 months per Philippine law) — escheatment processing: notify vendor, if unclaimed after additional 30 days, reverse payment and credit to state per BIR and SEC guidelines | AP Specialist / AP Supervisor | Finance Manager | 1–2 hours/month (exception handling) |

### System Touchpoints

- AP payment run execution with proposal, approval, and batch generation (FIN-043)
- AP 3-way match — only approved invoices eligible for payment selection (FIN-004, W7)
- Multi-bank integration — payment file generation per bank format (FIN-009)
- Cash management — cash position verification before payment execution (FIN-010, W30)
- Non-PO invoice processing — non-PO payment inclusion in batch (W7C)
- AP vendor statement reconciliation — payment reconciliation (W7D)
- Bank reconciliation — payment confirmation matching (W89)
- Electronic banking security — secure file transmission (W320)
- Bank account lifecycle — signatory management for checks (W317)
- Supply chain finance — early payment discount optimization (W324)
- Payment terms master — net due date calculation (W295)

### Time Estimate

~2–3 hours per scheduled payment run (proposal review + cash position check + approval + file generation + transmission). 3 runs/week × 2.5 hours = ~7.5 person-hours/week. Monthly total: ~30 person-hours. Absorbed by AP Supervisor, AP Specialists, and Treasury Analyst within existing FTEs.

### Pain Points / Risks

- PESONet batch file format differences across 4 banks (BDO, BPI, Metrobank, Chinabank) require format-specific generation — each bank has slightly different field requirements, reference number formats, and validation rules; mitigated by ERP bank format templates and pre-submission validation
- Check payments require physical dual signatures which delays disbursement during signatory absence (leave, travel, illness) — critical during month-end when volume peaks; mitigated by alternate signatory delegation per W317 and electronic payment migration for repeat vendors
- Payment holds from pending disputes (W244) require manual intervention — AP Supervisor must identify and exclude disputed invoices from payment proposal; mitigated by system auto-exclusion of invoices with active dispute status
- Stale-dated checks (uncashed after 6 months) require escheatment processing per Philippine law — administrative burden and compliance risk if not tracked; mitigated by automated stale-dated check alerting at 5 months
- FX payments for import vendors require separate USD account management — PHP-to-USD conversion timing affects payment amount; mitigated by Treasury Analyst coordination per W30 and W80 (FX hedging)

### Staffing Implication

- Covered by existing AP Supervisor, AP Specialists, and Treasury Analyst; no incremental headcount needed.
- ~7.5 person-hours/week distributed across AP and Treasury teams.
- Finance Manager approval adds ~15–30 min per run; absorbed within existing duties.

---

## W572. Customer Credit Monitoring & Automated Alert Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Continuous monitoring via automated credit scoring engine; monthly credit portfolio review meeting |
| **Frequency** | Continuous automated monitoring (24/7); monthly portfolio review; quarterly credit policy review |
| **Volume** | ~5,200 trade accounts and ~200 corporate accounts monitored continuously; ~50–100 automated alerts per month requiring review |
| **Owner** | Credit Manager / AR Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Credit Analyst (R), AR Supervisor (R), Credit Manager (A), Finance Manager (I), Sales Manager (C for B2B relationship) |

### Background

W24 covers the initial trade/corporate credit application and approval process. W328 covers annual credit limit periodic review. W108 covers customer credit collection and escalation for overdue accounts. W8 covers accounts receivable processing. W229 covers B2B credit limit exception and escalation. However, no workflow covers ongoing continuous credit monitoring with automated alerts — the real-time surveillance of customer credit health indicators including: payment pattern changes, credit utilization spikes, overdue aging trends, external credit deterioration signals, and industry/market risk factors. For a company with ~5,400 B2B accounts generating ~30% of revenue (PHP ~18.7B annually), proactive credit monitoring is essential to minimize bad debt exposure while supporting sales growth.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Credit scoring engine continuously monitors credit portfolio using configured rules: (a) payment aging trends — rolling 6-month average days-to-pay vs. prior 6-month period; (b) credit utilization % — current outstanding balance / approved credit limit; (c) average days-to-pay trend — acceleration or deceleration; (d) NSF/bounced check history per W425 — any instance in last 12 months; (e) order-to-invoice ratio — significant drops may indicate customer financial distress (reducing purchases); (f) external signals — credit bureau updates (TransUnion, CIBI) if enrolled | System | — | Continuous (24/7) |
| 2 | Alert generation — configurable thresholds trigger tiered alerts: (a) **Amber alert** (watch list): credit utilization >75%, average days-to-pay increased by >10 days vs. rolling average, first bounced check, order volume drop >30% vs. rolling average; (b) **Red alert** (action required): credit utilization >90%, any invoice >60 days overdue, second bounced check within 12 months, external credit bureau deterioration, customer under litigation or insolvency filing; alerts queued in Credit Analyst dashboard with priority ranking | System | — | Automated (real-time) |
| 3 | Credit Analyst reviews daily alert dashboard: (a) new alerts generated in last 24 hours; (b) aging of unreviewed alerts (SLA: Amber reviewed within 3 business days, Red reviewed within 1 business day); (c) accounts with multiple concurrent alerts (indicates compounding risk); (d) accounts transitioning from Amber to Red (deterioration trajectory) | Credit Analyst | Credit Manager | 1–2 hours/day |
| 4 | For Amber alerts: Credit Analyst conducts preliminary review — (a) pull full payment history for last 12 months; (b) assess if seasonal pattern (construction industry seasonal slowdown June–September during rainy season) or genuine deterioration; (c) check recent payment notes per W108 collection records; (d) review Sales Manager relationship notes; (e) if seasonal or explainable anomaly → no action, add monitoring note, revisit if trend continues for 2 consecutive months; (f) if unexplained deterioration → upgrade to enhanced monitoring with weekly check-in | Credit Analyst | Credit Manager | 20–30 min/account |
| 5 | For Red alerts: Credit Analyst conducts immediate comprehensive review — (a) full account history: all invoices, payments, credits, disputes for last 24 months; (b) collection notes per W108 — last contact date, promised payment dates, dispute status; (c) bounced check details per W425 — amounts, dates, resolution status; (d) order book: open orders and unshipped SOs that would increase exposure; (e) B2B relationship context: discuss with Sales Manager — is customer experiencing project delay, market downturn, or one-time issue? | Credit Analyst / Sales Manager | Credit Manager | 45–60 min/account |
| 6 | Credit Analyst recommends action based on review: (a) **Temporary credit hold** — block new sales orders pending payment of overdue amount (system blocks SO creation per W24 authorization matrix); (b) **Credit limit reduction** — formal notification to customer, reduce limit to current outstanding + 20% buffer; (c) **Payment term tightening** — change from Net 60 to Net 30, or Net 30 to COD; (d) **Cash-on-delivery conversion** — remove credit terms entirely, all future transactions COD; (e) **No action** — false alarm, seasonal pattern, or customer has provided satisfactory explanation with committed payment plan | Credit Analyst | Credit Manager | 15 min/recommendation |
| 7 | Credit Manager approves recommended action: (a) credit hold and credit limit changes require Credit Manager approval per W24 authorization matrix; (b) payment term changes require Credit Manager approval with Finance Manager notification; (c) COD conversion requires Credit Manager approval with Sales Manager acknowledgment; (d) Credit Manager reviews recommendation, supporting analysis, and Sales Manager input before decision | Credit Manager | Finance Manager (for term changes) | 10–15 min/account |
| 8 | System implements approved credit action: (a) update customer master per W253 — credit status, credit limit, payment terms; (b) if credit hold applied: system blocks new sales orders for customer, notifies Sales Manager and assigned Sales Representative, generates hold notification for customer service; (c) generate formal customer notification letter (credit limit change, term change, or COD notification) — reviewed by AR Supervisor for tone and accuracy before sending; (d) all changes logged in credit action audit trail | System / AR Supervisor | Credit Manager | 10 min/account |
| 9 | Customer outreach: (a) Sales Manager contacts customer for relationship management — discusses business situation, conveys credit action as temporary measure, explores payment plan or partial payment arrangement; (b) AR Supervisor contacts customer for payment discussion — presents overdue balance details, negotiates payment commitment date, documents agreement in collection notes per W108; (c) if customer provides payment commitment: Credit Analyst monitors follow-through, escalates missed commitment to Credit Manager within 2 business days | Sales Manager / AR Supervisor | Credit Manager | 30–60 min/account |
| 10 | Monthly credit portfolio review meeting: Credit Manager, Finance Manager, and Sales Manager review — (a) top 20 exposures by outstanding balance with risk rating; (b) portfolio aging distribution (current, 1–30, 31–60, 61–90, 90+ days) vs. prior month; (c) provision adequacy per W81 — compare actual loss experience to provision rates; (d) alert effectiveness metrics — alerts generated vs. actions taken vs. false positives; (e) policy compliance — accounts exceeding credit limit without approval, credit holds overridden; (f) bad debt forecast for next quarter | Credit Manager / Finance Manager / Sales Manager | CFO | 2–3 hours/month |
| 11 | Quarterly credit policy review: Credit Manager assesses — (a) alert threshold effectiveness: review Amber-to-Red transition rate, false positive rate, missed deterioration (accounts that defaulted without triggering alert); (b) recommend threshold adjustments to optimize detection vs. false positive balance; (c) update scoring model weights based on latest 12 months of default data; (d) review industry/market risk factors — construction sector outlook, government infrastructure spending, interest rate environment | Credit Manager | Finance Manager | 4 hours/quarter |
| 12 | Annual credit scoring model calibration: Credit Analyst recalibrates scoring model using actual default data — (a) analyze defaulted accounts (written off per W81) vs. performing accounts to identify predictive factors; (b) update weighting of risk factors (utilization, aging, bounced checks, external scores); (c) back-test revised model against historical data; (d) present calibration results to Finance Manager for approval; (e) deploy updated model parameters to production | Credit Analyst / Credit Manager | Finance Manager | 1 week/year |

### System Touchpoints

- Credit monitoring and automated alert module with configurable thresholds (FIN-044)
- AR for B2B — account data source for credit health indicators (FIN-005, W8)
- Bad debt provisioning — monitoring feed for provision adequacy review (FIN-022, W81)
- Credit application — initial assessment baseline (W24)
- AR processing — transaction data source (W8)
- Credit collection — escalation partner for overdue accounts (W108)
- Credit limit exception — exception handling partner (W229)
- Credit limit periodic review — annual review partner (W328)
- Bounced check recovery — NSF signal source (W425)
- Customer master data — credit action update target (W253)

### Time Estimate

~1–2 hours daily for alert review (Credit Analyst). Monthly portfolio review: 2–3 hours. Quarterly policy review: 4 hours. Annual calibration: 1 week. Total: ~40–50 person-hours/month. Absorbed by Credit Analyst, Credit Manager, and AR Supervisor within existing FTEs.

### Pain Points / Risks

- Philippine construction industry is highly fragmented with many small contractors who have limited financial transparency — credit assessments rely heavily on trade references and payment history rather than formal financial statements; mitigated by conservative initial credit limits per W24 and proactive monitoring
- External credit bureau data (TransUnion, CIBI) may be incomplete for provincial B2B customers — many small contractors and developers outside Metro Manila do not have robust credit bureau profiles; mitigated by relying primarily on internal payment behavior data
- Construction project payment delays cascade from developer to contractor to supplier — customer's late payment may reflect upstream project delay rather than customer financial distress; mitigated by Sales Manager relationship context (step 9) before taking credit action
- Political connections may influence credit decisions creating governance risk — large B2B accounts with political affiliations may pressure for favorable credit terms; mitigated by formal credit action approval workflow (step 7) and monthly portfolio review with Finance Manager and CFO visibility

### Staffing Implication

- Covered by existing Credit Manager, Credit Analyst, and AR Supervisor; no incremental headcount needed.
- ~40–50 person-hours/month distributed across credit monitoring team.
- Sales Manager involvement (~30–60 min per Red alert) absorbed within existing B2B relationship management duties.
