## W589. Weekly Cash Flow Forecast & Treasury Planning

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Weekly treasury planning cycle (every Monday) |
| **Frequency** | Weekly (rolling 4-week forward forecast updated every Monday) |
| **Volume** | 5 legal entities consolidated; ~PHP 5.19B/month gross cash inflows across 200 stores (cash ~40%, card ~35%, e-wallet ~15%, online ~10%); ~PHP 4.8B/month outflows (vendor payments ~PHP 2.5B, payroll ~PHP 1.2B, import LC/TT ~PHP 600M, Capex ~PHP 200M, taxes ~PHP 300M); ~210 bank accounts across 4 banking partners (BDO, BPI, Metrobank, Chinabank) |
| **Owner** | Treasury Analyst |
| **Participants** | Treasury Analyst (R), Treasury Manager (A), CFO (A for surplus/deficit actions), AP Supervisor (C for payment schedule), Payroll Manager (C for payroll dates), Import Coordinator (C for LC calendar), Store Operations Finance (C for store collection data), Controller (I) |

### Background

W30 covers daily treasury and cash position management, including a 2-week rolling cash flow forecast (W30 step 8). However, W30's 2-week forecast window is insufficient for proactive treasury planning given BuildRight's complex cash flow dynamics: (a) 200 stores generating daily cash collections across multiple payment methods with varying settlement timelines (cash next-day, card T+2, e-wallet T+1, online T+3–5); (b) bi-monthly payroll on the 14th and 28th consuming ~PHP 600M per payroll run; (c) intercompany settlements on the 5th of each month across 5 legal entities per W14; (d) import LC payments with 30–60 day lead times and FX exposure per W232; (e) vendor payment runs typically on the 10th and 25th per W556; (f) BIR tax payments on prescribed deadlines per W90; (g) seasonal cash flow variability (Q4 holiday season sales surge, Q2 rainy season slowdown). A dedicated weekly 4-week rolling forecast enables the Treasury team to anticipate cash surpluses for short-term placement and cash deficits requiring credit line drawdowns, and to coordinate with AP on payment timing optimization.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Prior week actual vs. forecast variance analysis**: Treasury Analyst pulls prior week's actual cash inflows and outflows from banking platform; compares to prior week's forecast; documents material variances (>±5% of forecast per entity) with root cause — common causes include: delayed store deposits, unexpected vendor early-payment discounts taken, payroll overtime variance, import shipment timing changes | Treasury Analyst | Treasury Manager | 45 min |
| 2 | **Store cash collection aggregation**: System aggregates prior week's store-level collections by payment method — cash (PHP ~2.08B/month across 200 stores), card (settled via BDO/BPI acquiring, net of MDR 1.5–2.5%), e-wallet (GCash/Maya, settled T+1 net of 1.0–1.5% fee), online payments (via Digital Commerce Inc., settled T+3–5); Treasury Analyst reviews collection efficiency — cash deposit shortfall rate target <0.5%, card settlement completeness, e-wallet reconciliation exceptions per W261 | Treasury Analyst | Treasury Manager | 30 min |
| 3 | **AP payment forecast (vendor payment run schedule)**: AP Supervisor provides upcoming vendor payment run schedule — standard payment runs on 10th and 25th of month per W556; early-pay discount opportunities (2/10 net 30 terms from major suppliers); overdue/past-due invoices requiring priority payment per W7; one-time payments (insurance premiums, lease deposits, utilities); total AP payment forecast for 4-week window | AP Supervisor | Treasury Analyst | 20 min |
| 4 | **Payroll forecast**: Payroll Manager provides payroll forecast for 4-week window — semi-monthly payroll on 14th and 28th (~PHP 600M per run covering ~8,500 employees); variable components: overtime, commissions, 13th month accrual release (November–December), statutory contributions (SSS, PhilHealth, Pag-IBIG) due by last working day of month; special payroll runs (separation pay, backpay adjustments) | Payroll Manager | Treasury Analyst | 10 min |
| 5 | **Intercompany settlement forecast**: Treasury Analyst maps intercompany cash flows for 4-week window — monthly IC settlements on 5th per W14: Logistics Inc. → Depot Inc. (store replenishment freight), Digital Commerce Inc. → Depot Inc. (ecommerce payment collection remittance), Depot Inc. → Holdings Inc. (management fees), Property Mgmt Inc. → Holdings Inc. (lease income); IC loan interest payments per W137; IC dividend declarations (quarterly, if applicable); system auto-generates IC settlement calendar from open IC balances | Treasury Analyst | Treasury Manager | 20 min |
| 6 | **LC/import payment calendar**: Import Coordinator provides LC payment schedule — open LCs with maturity dates in 4-week window per W232; TT payments for non-LC imports; customs duties and taxes per W239/W464; FX conversion requirements (PHP → USD) for import payments; total import payment forecast for 4-week window with PHP equivalents at current spot rate | Import Coordinator | Treasury Analyst | 15 min |
| 7 | **FX exposure review**: Treasury Analyst updates FX exposure report — total USD-denominated payables outstanding (import POs confirmed per W2B), USD bank balances across 5 import accounts, open forward contracts per W80 with maturity dates and forward rates, net unhedged USD exposure; calculates potential FX gain/loss on unhedged exposure using current BDO/BPI USD/PHP spot rate; flags if unhedged exposure exceeds 30-day rolling average import payment threshold per W30.10a FX hedging policy | Treasury Analyst | Treasury Manager | 20 min |
| 8 | **4-week rolling forecast compilation**: Treasury Analyst compiles complete 4-week rolling cash flow forecast per entity and consolidated — Week 1 (high confidence, based on committed payables and confirmed receivables), Week 2 (medium confidence, based on scheduled payments and historical collection patterns), Weeks 3–4 (lower confidence, based on forecasted sales, historical payment patterns, and known commitments); forecast includes: opening balance per entity, scheduled inflows (store collections, AR collections per W8, IC receipts), scheduled outflows (AP payments, payroll, import payments, IC payments, tax payments per W90, capex per W21), projected closing balance per entity and consolidated | Treasury Analyst | Treasury Manager | 60 min |
| 9 | **Cash surplus/deficit action plan**: Treasury Analyst identifies projected surplus or deficit positions per entity — (a) **Surplus**: recommend short-term placement (BDO/BPI time deposit, 7–30 day tenor, current PHP interest rate environment); calculate opportunity cost of idle cash vs. placement yield; (b) **Deficit**: recommend credit line drawdown from existing PHP 3B revolving credit facility (RCF) with BDO/BPI syndicated; calculate interest cost of drawdown; identify payment deferral opportunities (AP terms negotiation with non-critical vendors); (c) **Inter-entity optimization**: if Entity A has surplus and Entity B has deficit in same week, recommend inter-entity cash transfer per W14 instead of external borrowing or placement | Treasury Analyst | CFO | 30 min |
| 10 | **Forecast review and approval**: Treasury Manager reviews complete forecast package (variance analysis, collection data, AP/payroll/import calendars, FX exposure, 4-week projection, action plan); validates assumptions; challenges optimistic collection estimates or conservative payment assumptions; approves action plan items; escalates to CFO for items requiring approval: credit line drawdown >PHP 100M, short-term placement >PHP 200M, inter-entity transfer >PHP 50M | Treasury Manager | CFO | 30 min |
| 11 | **Action plan execution**: Treasury Analyst executes approved actions — (a) initiates short-term time deposit placements via BDO/BPI corporate banking platform; (b) submits credit line drawdown request to bank; (c) executes inter-entity transfers per approved schedule; (d) coordinates with AP Supervisor on payment timing adjustments (defer non-critical vendor payments from Week 2 to Week 3 if deficit projected); (e) executes FX conversions for confirmed import payments at optimal timing within week; (f) all actions logged in treasury management system with audit trail | Treasury Analyst | Treasury Manager | 30 min |
| 12 | **Distribution and communication**: Treasury Analyst distributes approved forecast to: CFO (executive summary), Controller (for close process planning), AP Supervisor (payment schedule confirmation), Payroll Manager (payroll funding confirmation), Import Coordinator (FX conversion schedule); system auto-archives forecast version for historical tracking and forecast accuracy measurement | Treasury Analyst | Treasury Manager | 10 min |
| 13 | **Mid-week update (Thursday)**: If material events occur between Monday forecast and Thursday (large unexpected payment, significant collection shortfall, FX rate movement >2%, emergency Capex approval), Treasury Analyst issues abbreviated mid-week flash update to Treasury Manager and CFO with revised Week 1–2 projection | Treasury Analyst | Treasury Manager | 15 min (as needed) |

**Total time per weekly cycle**: ~5.5 hours (Treasury Analyst ~4.5 hours, Treasury Manager ~1 hour, CFO ~15 min for approval). Mid-week flash: ~15 min as needed.

### System Touchpoints (W589 — Weekly Cash Flow Forecast)

- Treasury management module: 4-week rolling forecast template with entity-level and consolidated views (W589.8)
- Multi-bank statement import: BDO, BPI, Metrobank, Chinabank — prior week actual cash position extraction (W589.1, cross-reference W30.2)
- Store cash collection aggregation dashboard: real-time collection data by store, payment method, and settlement status (W589.2, cross-reference W99, W261)
- AP payment schedule integration: upcoming payment runs from AP module per W556 with vendor payment calendar (W589.3)
- Payroll module integration: payroll dates and amounts from HR/Payroll system per W10 (W589.4)
- Intercompany settlement calendar: auto-generated from open IC balances per W14 with scheduled settlement dates (W589.5)
- LC/import payment calendar: open LC maturity schedule from trade finance module per W232 with customs duty estimates per W239 (W589.6)
- FX exposure dashboard: USD-denominated payables, USD bank balances, open forward contracts per W80, net unhedged position with mark-to-market (W589.7)
- Forecast variance tracking: system stores prior forecasts alongside actuals for accuracy measurement; auto-calculates MAPE (Mean Absolute Percentage Error) per entity per week; target forecast accuracy: Week 1 ±3%, Week 2 ±8%, Weeks 3–4 ±15% (W589.1, W589.8)
- Credit facility utilization tracking: PHP 3B RCF drawdown and repayment schedule with interest accrual (W589.9)
- Short-term placement management: time deposit creation, maturity tracking, interest accrual (W589.9)
- Executive treasury dashboard: consolidated 4-week forecast summary for CFO mobile review (W589.12)

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Store collection forecast accuracy**: with 200 stores generating ~PHP 5.19B/month in collections, even a 2% forecast error equals ~PHP 104M/month in unexpected surplus or deficit; provincial stores with irregular banking hours and weather-related deposit delays (typhoon season June–November) introduce significant collection volatility that degrades forecast accuracy
- **Card and e-wallet settlement timing uncertainty**: card acquiring settlement (BDO/BPI) is typically T+2 but can extend to T+3 during banking holidays; e-wallet settlement (GCash/Maya) is T+1 but may be delayed by platform outages; with ~50% of collections via card/e-wallet (~PHP 2.6B/month), settlement timing uncertainty creates ~PHP 85M/day of ambiguity in the near-term cash position
- **Import payment lumpy cash outflows**: import LC payments are large, infrequent, and FX-sensitive — a single container shipment of power tools from China may require USD 200K (~PHP 11.6M); if multiple LCs mature in the same week, the outflow spike can create a temporary cash deficit even if the monthly position is healthy
- **Intercompany settlement coordination**: IC settlements on the 5th require all 5 entities to have accurate payable/receivable balances agreed by day 3 of the month; if any entity's close process is delayed, the IC settlement amounts are uncertain, creating ambiguity in the first week's forecast
- **Payroll timing rigidity**: payroll on the 14th and 28th is a non-negotiable PHP 600M outflow per run per RA 6727 (Wage Rationalization Act) and labor regulations; if a payroll date falls in a week with other large outflows (vendor payment run + import payment), the cumulative outflow can temporarily exceed available cash, requiring credit line drawdown
- **Seasonal cash flow volatility**: Q4 holiday season (October–December) generates ~35% of annual revenue with corresponding cash inflow surge, while Q2 rainy season (June–September) sees ~15% revenue decline with fixed costs unchanged; the 4-week forecast must dynamically adjust for seasonal patterns that the 2-week W30 forecast cannot adequately capture

### Staffing Implication

- **Treasury Analyst (dedicated)**: ~4.5 hours/week on forecast cycle + ~15 min/week mid-week flash = ~5 hours/week. This is a dedicated role within the existing Treasury team of 2–3 analysts. The weekly forecast cycle is the primary responsibility of one analyst; the other analysts cover daily cash position management (W30) and import payment execution.
- **Treasury Manager**: ~1 hour/week on forecast review and approval. Absorbed within existing role.
- **CFO**: ~15 min/week on action plan approval. Absorbed within existing role.
- **AP Supervisor**: ~20 min/week providing payment schedule. Absorbed within existing AP duties.
- **Payroll Manager**: ~10 min/week providing payroll forecast. Absorbed within existing payroll duties.
- **Import Coordinator**: ~15 min/week providing LC calendar. Absorbed within existing import duties.
- **No incremental headcount required**. The weekly forecast absorbs approximately 25% of one Treasury Analyst's capacity, complementing their daily W30 duties.

---

## W590. Monthly Tax Provision & Compliance Review

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Month-end close process initiation (day 1 of following month), feeds into W9 financial close |
| **Frequency** | Monthly (by day 3 of following month, to meet 5-day month-end close target) |
| **Volume** | 5 legal entities; monthly tax provisions covering: VAT (12% on ~PHP 5.19B monthly revenue), EWT (1–10% on ~PHP 2.5B monthly AP), CWT collection (~PHP 300M/month in customer CWT), percentage tax (3% on VAT-exempt revenue streams), corporate income tax (RCIT 25% / FCIT 20% per CREATE law per PAS 12), local taxes (LGU business tax quarterly); BIR eFPS filing deadlines per tax type |
| **Owner** | Tax Accountant |
| **Participants** | Tax Accountant (R), Chief Accountant (A), Controller (A for provision approval), AP Supervisor (C for EWT data), AR Supervisor (C for CWT data), Treasury Analyst (C for tax payment funding), External Tax Advisor (C for complex positions) |

### Background

W90 covers monthly BIR tax filing and statutory remittance execution. W260 covers BIR eFPS filing and electronic payment submission. W407 covers corporate income tax computation and deferred tax (PAS 12). W473 covers BIR EIS API transmission. W475 covers CWT 2307 certificate collection. W499 covers percentage tax computation. However, no single workflow covers the end-to-end monthly tax provision process — the computation, review, journal entry posting, and compliance calendar verification that must occur before the financial close is completed on day 5. This workflow fills that gap by ensuring that: (a) tax provisions are computed accurately before financial statements are finalized; (b) VAT input/output reconciliation identifies any unclaimed input VAT or under-remitted output VAT before BIR 2550M filing; (c) EWT remittance per BIR 1601-E is reconciled to actual AP withholding; (d) CWT certificates (BIR 2307) are collected from customers for credit against income tax; (e) deferred tax movements are computed per PAS 12; (f) tax provision journal entries are posted before close completion; (g) the BIR compliance calendar is reviewed for upcoming deadlines within the next 30 days. The Philippine tax environment is particularly complex due to: multiple withholding tax rates depending on payee type (corporations 1%, professionals 10%, suppliers of goods 1%, casual employees 5–10%); VAT on importations handled separately from domestic VAT; quarterly income tax returns (1702Q) requiring cumulative provisions; and LGU business tax payments (quarterly) requiring separate accruals.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Revenue-based tax provision computation**: System auto-computes tax provisions based on month's posted revenue per entity — (a) **Output VAT** (12%): gross revenue per entity from POS (Depot Inc.), ecommerce (Digital Commerce Inc.), services (Logistics Inc., Property Mgmt Inc.), and management fees (Holdings Inc.); separately identify VAT-exempt sales (senior citizen/PWD per W217, agricultural products, residential lease below threshold) which are subject to percentage tax (3%) instead; (b) **Percentage tax** (3%): computed on VAT-exempt revenue streams per W499; (c) **Gross receipts tax**: applicable to financial income and certain service revenues per entity; (d) **LGU business tax accrual**: quarterly LGU payments accrued monthly based on prior year's assessed business tax per LGU per entity | System / Tax Accountant | Chief Accountant | 30 min (review automated computation) |
| 2 | **VAT input/output reconciliation**: Tax Accountant reconciles VAT position per entity — (a) **Output VAT**: total output VAT from sales invoices and official receipts per BIR EIS transmission log (W473); include output VAT on deemed sales (IC transfers between VAT-registered entities per W14); verify output VAT on importations (customs-assessed VAT per W239); (b) **Input VAT**: total input VAT from purchase invoices (domestic), import customs declarations, and other creditable input VAT; verify input VAT claims are supported by valid VAT invoices/official receipts per BIR requirements; identify deferred input VAT (purchases recorded but VAT invoice not yet received from vendor); (c) **Net VAT payable/receivable**: output VAT minus input VAT per entity; if net payable, this is the amount due for BIR 2550M filing; if net receivable (excess input VAT from imports or capital goods), determine whether to claim refund (BIR processing time 12–24 months) or carry forward to next month per W90; (d) **VAT reconciliation exceptions**: unmatched input VAT claims (vendor VAT invoice received but purchase not yet posted), missing VAT invoices from vendors, VAT rate discrepancies (10% vs. 12% transition period issues for older invoices) | Tax Accountant | Chief Accountant | 60 min |
| 3 | **EWT remittance reconciliation**: Tax Accountant reconciles EWT per entity — (a) pull EWT amounts withheld from AP module: EWT rates applied per vendor/payee type — 1% (corporate suppliers of goods per RR 2-98 as amended), 2% (corporate suppliers of services), 5–10% (individual payees per tax table), 10% (professionals); (b) compare to EWT amounts remitted via BIR eFPS per BIR Form 1601-E; (c) identify discrepancies: under-remittance (withholding deducted from vendor but not remitted to BIR = company liability), over-remittance (remitted more than withheld = requires reconciliation with vendor); (d) verify EWT certificate (BIR 2306/2307) generation per W475 — certificates must be issued to vendors within 20 days of month-end per BIR requirement | Tax Accountant | Chief Accountant | 45 min |
| 4 | **CWT certificate collection status review**: Tax Accountant reviews CWT certificates collected from customers — (a) pull list of customers who withheld tax on payments to BuildRight during the month (primarily B2B corporate customers and government entities); (b) verify BIR Form 2307 certificates received from customers — required to credit CWT against quarterly income tax per BIR 1702Q; (c) follow up on missing certificates with AR Supervisor — customer contact per W8 for certificate collection; (d) compute total CWT available as tax credit for current quarter; (e) flag material uncollected CWT (>PHP 1M per customer) for escalation to Credit Manager | Tax Accountant / AR Supervisor | Chief Accountant | 30 min |
| 5 | **Deferred tax movement computation**: Tax Accountant computes deferred tax movements per PAS 12 per entity — (a) **Temporary differences**: depreciation timing (tax vs. book per W407 — accelerated depreciation for tax vs. straight-line for book), accrued expenses not yet deductible (provisions requiring cash basis recognition per BIR), unrealized FX gains/losses; (b) **Deferred tax liability (DTL)**: taxable temporary differences × applicable rate (25% RCIT for domestic corporations per CREATE Law, 20% FCIT for Regional Operating Headquarters if applicable); (c) **Deferred tax asset (DTA)**: deductible temporary differences × applicable rate, including net operating loss carryover (NOLCO) per CREATE — NOLCO carryforward period is 3 years for small taxpayers, unlimited for large taxpayers; (d) **DTL/DTA movement**: current month movement = ending balance minus beginning balance; movement posted to deferred tax provision journal entry | Tax Accountant | Controller | 45 min |
| 6 | **Tax provision journal entry preparation and posting**: Tax Accountant prepares composite tax provision journal entry per entity — (a) **Output VAT provision**: debit VAT output (liability), credit VAT payable; (b) **Input VAT claim**: debit VAT input (asset), credit VAT expense/VAT payable; (c) **EWT payable**: debit EWT expense (embedded in purchase cost), credit EWT payable; (d) **CWT receivable**: debit CWT receivable (asset), credit income tax provision; (e) **Current income tax provision**: debit income tax expense, credit current income tax payable (estimated based on quarterly-to-date taxable income); (f) **Deferred tax provision**: debit/credit deferred tax expense, credit/debit DTL/DTA per step 5; (g) **Percentage tax payable**: debit percentage tax expense, credit percentage tax payable; (h) **LGU business tax accrual**: debit LGU tax expense, credit LGU tax payable (accrual); Tax Accountant posts journal entries in ERP; Chief Accountant reviews and approves before close completion | Tax Accountant | Chief Accountant | 45 min |
| 7 | **Tax compliance calendar review**: Tax Accountant reviews 30-day forward compliance calendar — (a) **BIR 2550M** (Monthly VAT Return): due 20th of following month or 25th via eFPS per W90; (b) **BIR 1601-E** (Monthly EWT Remittance Return): due 10th of following month via eFPS per W260; (c) **BIR 1601-C** (Monthly CWT Remittance Return): due 10th of following month via eFPS; (d) **BIR 1702Q** (Quarterly Income Tax Return): due 60 days after quarter-end (if current month is quarter-end); (e) **BIR 1604-E/1604-C** (Annual Information Returns): due January 31 of following year (if applicable); (f) **LGU business tax**: quarterly payments per LGU schedule (varies by city/municipality where 5 entities are registered); (g) **BIR EIS transmission**: daily sales invoice transmission per W473 — verify no gaps in transmission log; (h) Tax Accountant confirms all deadlines are tracked in compliance calendar with responsible person assigned and preparation start date flagged | Tax Accountant | Chief Accountant | 15 min |
| 8 | **BIR filing preparation**: Tax Accountant prepares upcoming month BIR filings — (a) pre-populate BIR 2550M from system VAT reconciliation (step 2); (b) pre-populate BIR 1601-E from system EWT reconciliation (step 3); (c) pre-populate BIR 1601-C from system CWT reconciliation (step 4); (d) verify tax amounts match provision journal entries (step 6); (e) generate draft returns for Chief Accountant review; (f) Chief Accountant reviews and approves draft returns; (g) Tax Accountant submits via BIR eFPS per W260 | Tax Accountant | Chief Accountant | 30 min |
| 9 | **LGU tax payments review**: Tax Accountant reviews LGU tax obligations — (a) verify quarterly LGU business tax payments are accrued correctly per entity per LGU jurisdiction; (b) confirm payments to LGUs where entities hold business permits (Quezon City, Manila, Laguna, Clark, Cebu — per entity registration); (c) verify real property tax (RPT) semi-annual payments per W184 for owned properties; (d) confirm LGU tax payments are scheduled in cash flow forecast per W589 | Tax Accountant | Chief Accountant | 15 min |
| 10 | **Quarterly income tax provision review (if quarter-end month)**: If current month is quarter-end (March, June, September, December), Tax Accountant conducts comprehensive quarterly review — (a) compute cumulative quarterly taxable income per entity per BIR 1702Q; (b) reconcile cumulative tax provision (current + deferred) to estimated annual tax liability; (c) adjust quarterly provision if material variance between estimated annual tax and year-to-date provision; (d) verify NOLCO utilization per CREATE Law provisions; (e) review tax incentives per W472 (BOI/PEZA registered activities if applicable); (f) External Tax Advisor reviews complex positions (cross-border, transfer pricing per W486, related party transactions per PAS 24) | Tax Accountant / External Tax Advisor | Controller | 2–3 hours (quarterly) |

**Total time per monthly cycle**: ~5 hours (Tax Accountant ~4 hours, Chief Accountant ~1 hour review). Quarterly addendum: +2–3 hours. Target completion: day 3 of following month (2 days before W9 close target of day 5).

### System Touchpoints (W590 — Monthly Tax Provision)

- Tax provision module: automated computation of output VAT, input VAT, EWT, CWT, percentage tax, and current/deferred income tax per entity (W590.1, W590.6)
- VAT input/output reconciliation tool: matches output VAT from sales invoices/EIS transmission to input VAT from purchase invoices and import customs declarations (W590.2, cross-reference W473)
- EWT reconciliation report: compares AP module withholding amounts to BIR eFPS remittance per Form 1601-E (W590.3, cross-reference W260)
- CWT certificate tracking: lists CWT collected from customers with BIR 2307 certificate status (W590.4, cross-reference W475)
- Deferred tax computation worksheet: temporary difference identification, DTL/DTA calculation, movement tracking per PAS 12 (W590.5, cross-reference W407)
- Tax provision journal entry template: pre-formatted journal entry per entity with auto-populated tax amounts (W590.6)
- BIR compliance calendar: configurable calendar with all BIR filing deadlines per tax type per entity (W590.7)
- BIR eFPS integration: pre-populated BIR returns (2550M, 1601-E, 1601-C) ready for submission (W590.8, cross-reference W260)
- LGU tax tracking: per-entity per-LGU business tax and RPT obligations with payment schedule (W590.9)
- Senior citizen/PWD VAT-exemption data: feeds from W217 to compute VAT-exempt revenue for percentage tax calculation (W590.1)
- AP module: EWT rate assignment per vendor, automatic EWT computation at invoice posting (W590.3)
- AR module: CWT deduction at customer payment application, BIR 2307 tracking (W590.4)
- Import module: customs-assessed VAT and duties from BOC E2M integration for input VAT claims (W590.2, cross-reference W239)

### Pain Points / Risks

- **VAT input/output reconciliation complexity**: with ~PHP 5.19B/month in revenue (output VAT ~PHP 623M) and ~PHP 2.5B/month in purchases (input VAT ~PHP 300M), the monthly VAT reconciliation involves matching thousands of invoices across 5 entities; any vendor invoice with incorrect TIN, VAT amount, or invoice format per BIR requirements invalidates the input VAT claim, creating permanent tax leakage
- **EWT rate misapplication**: Philippine EWT rates are complex and depend on the payee's tax status (corporation vs. individual, goods vs. services, VAT-registered vs. non-VAT) — a 1% vs. 2% misapplication on a PHP 50M vendor payment equals PHP 500K in either under-withholding (company liable for deficiency + penalties) or over-withholding (vendor disputes, relationship damage)
- **CWT certificate collection from B2B customers**: BuildRight's ~5,400 B2B customers are required to withhold tax and issue BIR 2307 certificates, but collection rates are historically low (~60–70%); uncollected CWT means BuildRight cannot credit the withholding against its income tax liability, effectively paying tax twice on the same income until certificates are obtained
- **CREATE Law income tax rate changes**: the Corporate Recovery and Tax Incentives for Enterprises (CREATE) Law reduced RCIT from 30% to 25% (20% for micro/small enterprises) and introduced NOLCO carryforward changes; deferred tax computations must reflect the correct applicable rate per entity size classification, and rate changes require remeasurement of existing DTL/DTA balances
- **BIR eFPS downtime**: BIR's electronic filing system (eFPS) experiences frequent downtime, especially near filing deadlines (10th and 20th of month); late filing penalties are 25% surcharge + 20% interest per annum + compromise penalty per RMC 7-2015, which can materially increase tax costs for a company with BuildRight's scale
- **Multi-entity LGU compliance**: each of the 5 legal entities may be registered in multiple LGU jurisdictions for business tax purposes; LGU tax rules, rates, and payment schedules vary by city/municipality, creating compliance complexity and risk of missed payments in smaller LGU jurisdictions

### Staffing Implication

- **Tax Accountant (dedicated)**: ~4 hours/month on monthly provision cycle + ~2–3 hours/quarter on quarterly review = ~50–55 hours/year on this workflow. This is a dedicated role within the Finance team; the Tax Accountant also supports W90 (monthly filing execution) and W260 (eFPS submission).
- **Chief Accountant**: ~1 hour/month on provision review. Absorbed within existing close responsibilities.
- **Controller**: ~15 min/month on provision approval; ~1 hour/quarter on quarterly income tax review. Absorbed within existing role.
- **AR Supervisor**: ~15 min/month on CWT certificate follow-up coordination. Absorbed within existing AR duties.
- **External Tax Advisor**: ~2 hours/quarter on complex position review (transfer pricing, cross-border, tax incentive). Engaged on retainer.
- **No incremental headcount required**. The monthly tax provision cycle is the primary responsibility of the existing Tax Accountant, complementing their W90 filing duties.

## W610. Insurance Claims Processing & Recovery Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Insurable incident occurs: property damage, theft, vehicle accident, cargo damage, customer injury, business interruption |
| **Frequency** | As needed; estimated ~40-60 claims/month across 200 stores, 4 DCs, fleet |
| **Volume** | ~500-700 incidents/year; ~30% become formal insurance claims |
| **Owner** | Insurance & Risk Manager |
| **Participants** | Store/DC Manager, Insurance & Risk Manager, insurance adjuster, legal counsel, Treasury Analyst, affected department head |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Incident occurs: location Manager documents incident with date, time, location, description, photos, estimated damage value, and witness statements; system creates insurance incident record linked to location master and policy registry (per W59) | Location Manager | Insurance & Risk Manager | 30 min |
| 2 | Insurance & Risk Manager reviews incident report; determines if incident falls within policy coverage; verifies deductible amount and coverage limits; classifies claim type: property, liability, cargo, vehicle, business interruption | Insurance & Risk Manager | — | 15 min |
| 3 | For incidents below deductible or non-covered: Insurance & Risk Manager advises location Manager to process as operational loss; GL posting to appropriate expense account; no formal claim filed | Insurance & Risk Manager | Controller | 10 min |
| 4 | For covered incidents above deductible: Insurance & Risk Manager prepares claim documentation package: incident report, photos, police report (if theft/vandalism per W471), repair estimates, replacement quotes, loss of revenue calculation (for business interruption) | Insurance & Risk Manager | — | 60 min |
| 5 | Claim filed with insurance provider via broker portal or direct submission; system tracks claim number, submission date, estimated recovery amount, and expected settlement timeline | Insurance & Risk Manager | — | 15 min |
| 6 | Insurance adjuster inspection scheduled and conducted; Insurance & Risk Manager coordinates site access, documentation, and witness availability; adjuster provides assessment report | Insurance & Risk Manager | — | 2-4 hours |
| 7 | Claim settlement negotiation: Insurance & Risk Manager reviews adjuster assessment vs. actual loss; negotiates settlement amount; involves legal counsel for disputed claims > PHP 500K | Insurance & Risk Manager | CFO | 1-2 hours |
| 8 | Settlement received: Treasury posts recovery to insurance recovery GL account; system reconciles claim record; GL posting: DR Cash / CR Insurance Recovery (gain) or DR Insurance Recovery / CR Asset/Expense (reimbursement) | Treasury Analyst | Controller | 15 min |
| 9 | Monthly: Insurance & Risk Manager generates claims dashboard — open claims by type, aging, recovery rate, claim frequency trend by location; identifies locations with above-average claim frequency for risk mitigation review | Insurance & Risk Manager | CFO | 2 hours |
| 10 | Annual: Insurance & Risk Manager conducts policy renewal review with claim history analysis; presents claim frequency, severity, and recovery data to CFO; recommends policy adjustments, coverage changes, or new coverage requirements | Insurance & Risk Manager | CFO | 8 hours |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP Insurance module: policy registry, claim case management, settlement tracking
- ERP Finance module: GL posting for losses and recoveries, deductible expense allocation
- ERP Fixed Asset module: asset damage/loss recording (per W39)
- Document management: claim documentation storage and retrieval (per W255)
- BI dashboard: claims analytics, recovery rate, trend analysis

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Underinsurance**: store assets or inventory may exceed policy coverage limits, especially for newer stores with higher inventory investment; mitigated by annual coverage adequacy review in Step 10
- **Slow settlement**: Philippine insurance claims can take 3-12 months for complex cases; mitigated by proactive adjuster follow-up and escalation to broker for delayed claims > 90 days
- **Documentation gaps**: incomplete incident documentation at store level reduces claim success rate; mitigated by incident report template in mobile app and Store Manager training
- **Claim fraud suspicion**: insurers may dispute legitimate claims; mitigated by thorough documentation, CCTV evidence (per W207), and independent adjuster engagement

### Staffing Implication
- **Insurance & Risk Manager**: ~15-30 hours/month on claims processing + ~8 hours/year on policy review. This is a dedicated role within Finance/Compliance team.
- **Store/DC Managers**: ~30 min per incident for documentation. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Treasury Analyst**: ~15 min per settlement posting. Absorbed within existing role.

## W611. Payment Gateway Daily Operations & Settlement Monitoring

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Start of business day; continuous throughout operating hours |
| **Frequency** | Daily |
| **Volume** | ~2.8M POS transactions/month; ~42,900 ecommerce orders/month; ~36% card, ~15% e-wallet |
| **Owner** | Treasury Analyst |
| **Participants** | Treasury Analyst, IT Integration Specialist, POS Administrator, Finance Analyst |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Morning: Treasury Analyst reviews payment gateway health dashboard — connectivity status for all active gateways (card acquirer, PayMongo, Dragonpay, GCash, Maya), transaction success rate (target ≥ 99.5%), average response time, and any overnight settlement files received | Treasury Analyst | — | 15 min |
| 2 | System auto-processes overnight settlement files: card acquirer settlement file (previous day's batch close per W537), e-wallet settlement files (GCash, Maya, GrabPay — T+1 settlement), ecommerce payment gateway settlement (PayMongo, Dragonpay — T+1 to T+2) | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | Treasury Analyst reviews auto-matching results: settlement amount vs. POS/ecommerce recorded transactions; system flags unmatched items (missing transactions, amount discrepancies, MDR fee variance > 0.1%) | Treasury Analyst | — | 30 min |
| 4 | For unmatched items: Treasury Analyst investigates — (a) timing differences (settlement file covers different cutoff than POS batch close), (b) MDR rate discrepancy (new rate not configured), (c) failed transactions not captured, (d) duplicate settlement entries | Treasury Analyst | — | 15 min/item |
| 5 | If MDR rate discrepancy: Treasury Analyst creates configuration change request to POS Administrator for rate update in payment gateway master; verifies contracted rate vs. billed rate | Treasury Analyst | POS Administrator | 10 min |
| 6 | Mid-day: system monitors real-time payment gateway transaction success rate; if success rate drops below 98%, auto-alerts IT Integration Specialist and Treasury Analyst for immediate investigation (connectivity issue, gateway outage, API rate limiting) | System / IT Specialist | Treasury Analyst | As needed |
| 7 | End of day: Treasury Analyst reviews daily settlement reconciliation summary — total processed, matched, unmatched, and aging of unresolved items; unresolved items > 5 business days escalated to Controller | Treasury Analyst | Controller | 15 min |
| 8 | Weekly: Treasury Analyst generates payment gateway performance report — transaction volumes by gateway, success rates, settlement timeliness, MDR costs, chargeback rates (per W488); presents to Finance Manager | Treasury Analyst | Finance Manager | 2 hours |
| 9 | Monthly: Finance Manager reviews total payment processing costs (MDR, gateway fees, chargeback losses) vs. budget; evaluates new payment method requests (e.g., new e-wallet, BNPL provider); coordinates onboarding with IT Integration Specialist | Finance Manager | CFO | 2 hours |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP Treasury module: settlement file processing, reconciliation, GL posting
- Payment gateway APIs: real-time transaction monitoring, settlement file retrieval
- POS system: transaction batch close, card terminal management (per W537)
- BI dashboard: payment gateway health, settlement analytics, cost tracking

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Gateway outage during peak hours**: payment gateway downtime blocks card/e-wallet payments, reducing revenue and causing customer frustration; mitigated by multi-gateway redundancy and automatic failover
- **Settlement delays**: e-wallet and card settlements can be delayed 1-3 days, creating reconciliation backlogs; mitigated by automated matching and tolerance-based auto-reconciliation
- **MDR rate creep**: payment providers may increase rates without notice; mitigated by monthly rate verification in Step 9
- **New payment method proliferation**: Philippine market has many e-wallets and payment apps; onboarding each requires IT configuration and testing; mitigated by standardized onboarding checklist and quarterly evaluation

### Staffing Implication
- **Treasury Analyst**: ~60 min/day on gateway operations + ~2 hours/week on reporting = ~8 hours/week. Absorbed within existing Treasury role.
- **IT Integration Specialist**: ~2-3 hours/month on gateway configuration and troubleshooting. Absorbed within existing role.

## W612. Intercompany Rate Setting & Quarterly Transfer Pricing Review

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Quarterly calendar; annual review; or change in market conditions |
| **Frequency** | Quarterly formal review; annual comprehensive update |
| **Volume** | 5 legal entities; ~8-12 intercompany service/transaction types |
| **Owner** | Controller |
| **Participants** | Controller, CFO, Tax Accountant, Chief Accountant, external transfer pricing advisor |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Controller prepares quarterly IC rate review package: current IC pricing for all transaction types (warehousing fee per pallet, distribution fee per delivery, ecommerce fulfillment fee per order, property lease per sqm, management fees), actual costs incurred, comparison to prior period rates, and market benchmark data | Controller | CFO | 4 hours |
| 2 | Controller validates arm's-length compliance: compares IC rates to external market data (3PL warehousing rates, delivery service rates, commercial lease rates in same LGU); documents benchmark methodology per BIR Transfer Pricing guidelines (RR 02-2013) and PAS 24 disclosure requirements (per W486) | Controller | External TP Advisor | 4 hours |
| 3 | Tax Accountant reviews IC rate implications for tax compliance: (a) BIR scrutiny risk for IC rates significantly above or below market, (b) VAT treatment of IC services, (c) withholding tax obligations on cross-entity payments, (d) BOI/PEZA entity implications (per W472) | Tax Accountant | Controller | 2 hours |
| 4 | Quarterly meeting: Controller presents rate review to CFO; CFO approves or adjusts IC rates; for rate changes > 10%, external transfer pricing advisor provides written opinion; CFO approves with advisor concurrence | Controller | CFO | 1 hour |
| 5 | Chief Accountant updates IC transfer pricing rule master in ERP (per W305); configures new rates with effective date; system validates rate change against defined tolerance thresholds (±15% from prior rate requires Controller re-approval) | Chief Accountant | Controller | 30 min |
| 6 | System auto-generates IC invoices at new rates from effective date; IC settlement process (per W14) applies updated rates; consolidation module (per W9A) uses updated rates for elimination entries | System | — | Automated |
| 7 | Annual: Controller engages external transfer pricing advisor for comprehensive TP documentation preparation — functional analysis, comparability analysis, benchmark study, and TP report per BIR requirements; estimated cost: PHP 500K-1M/year | Controller | CFO | 20 hours |
| 8 | Annual: Chief Accountant ensures PAS 24 related party disclosure in annual financial statements includes all IC transaction types, amounts, and pricing methods (per W486) | Chief Accountant | Controller | 4 hours |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP Intercompany module: IC pricing rules, auto-invoice generation, IC settlement
- ERP Consolidation module: IC elimination using current transfer pricing rules
- ERP Financial Reporting: PAS 24 disclosure templates
- Document management: TP documentation and benchmark study storage (per W255)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **BIR transfer pricing audit**: BIR increasingly scrutinizes IC transactions; insufficient documentation can result in tax adjustments and penalties; mitigated by annual TP documentation in Step 7
- **Rate lag vs. cost changes**: IC rates reviewed quarterly but actual costs may change between reviews; mitigated by quarterly review cadence and automatic escalation trigger if cost variance > 10%
- **Entity-level P&L distortion**: if IC rates are set too high/low, individual entity profitability is distorted, affecting management decisions; mitigated by arm's-length benchmarking in Step 2
- **Complexity with 5 entities and multiple IC flows**: tracking all IC transaction types across 5 entities creates administrative complexity; mitigated by centralized IC pricing rule master (W305)

### Staffing Implication
- **Controller**: ~4 hours/quarter on rate review + ~20 hours/year on annual documentation = ~36 hours/year. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Tax Accountant**: ~2 hours/quarter on tax review = ~8 hours/year. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Chief Accountant**: ~30 min/quarter on system update + ~4 hours/year on PAS 24 = ~6 hours/year. Absorbed within existing role.
- **External Transfer Pricing Advisor**: ~20 hours/year for annual TP documentation (engaged on retainer).

## W613. Store-Level Weekly Payroll Accrual & Labor Cost Flash Report

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | End of each work week (Sunday); semi-monthly cutoff dates (15th and 30th) |
| **Frequency** | Weekly flash; semi-monthly reconciliation |
| **Volume** | 200 stores; ~5,800 store employees; semi-monthly payroll of ~PHP 90M |
| **Owner** | Finance Analyst (Payroll) |
| **Participants** | Finance Analyst, Payroll Officer, Controller, Store Managers |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System accumulates daily payroll data from attendance system: regular hours, overtime hours (with premium), holiday hours, night differential hours, leave hours — per employee per store | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | Weekly (Monday): Finance Analyst runs weekly payroll accrual flash report for all 200 stores — estimated gross payroll by store based on hours worked × applicable rates; includes overtime, holiday, night differential, and projected statutory deductions | Finance Analyst | — | 30 min |
| 3 | Finance Analyst compares weekly flash to pro-rated weekly payroll budget (monthly budget ÷ 4.33); flags stores exceeding weekly budget by > 10%; investigates causes (overtime surge, holiday week, new hires) | Finance Analyst | Controller | 30 min |
| 4 | System auto-generates GL accrual entry: DR Payroll Expense (by store cost center) / CR Accrued Payroll Liability; accrual based on hours worked to date × average hourly rate; adjusting entry posted at semi-monthly payroll actual (per W10) | System | Controller | Automated |
| 5 | Semi-monthly (after payroll run per W10): Finance Analyst reconciles accrued payroll (from weekly flashes) to actual payroll posted; posts adjusting entry for variance (typically < 3%); investigates variances > 5% by store | Finance Analyst | Controller | 2 hours |
| 6 | Finance Analyst distributes weekly flash to Store Managers via mobile dashboard: their store's week-to-date labor cost, budget comparison, and department breakdown; Store Managers use for overtime decisions (per W602) | Finance Analyst | — | 15 min |
| 7 | Monthly: Controller reviews payroll accrual accuracy — compares total accrued vs. actual for trailing 3 months; adjusts accrual methodology if systematic bias detected (consistent over/under accrual) | Controller | CFO | 1 hour |
| 8 | Quarterly: Finance Analyst prepares labor cost trend analysis for CFO — store-level labor cost as % of revenue trend, overtime trend, headcount efficiency (revenue per employee per store), seasonal pattern analysis | Finance Analyst | CFO | 4 hours |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP Payroll module: hours accumulation, rate calculation, accrual generation
- ERP Finance module: GL accrual posting, budget comparison, variance analysis
- ERP HR module: attendance data, leave data, overtime data
- BI dashboard: weekly payroll flash per store, labor cost trends, accrual accuracy

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Accrual accuracy**: weekly accrual based on estimated rates may differ from actual payroll by 2-5% due to variable deductions, retroactive adjustments, and new hire/departure timing; mitigated by semi-monthly true-up in Step 5
- **Holiday week distortion**: Philippine holidays with premium pay create significant accrual spikes; mitigated by holiday calendar awareness and separate holiday accrual line item
- **Data latency**: attendance data from biometric system may have 1-day lag for stores with intermittent connectivity; mitigated by W366 network monitoring and offline attendance buffering

### Staffing Implication
- **Finance Analyst (Payroll)**: ~30 min/week on flash report + ~2 hours semi-monthly reconciliation + ~4 hours/quarter trend analysis = ~6 hours/month. Absorbed within existing FP&A role.
- **Controller**: ~15 min/week review + ~1 hour/month accrual review = ~2 hours/month. Absorbed within existing role.

## W634. Revenue Assurance & POS Revenue Reconciliation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Daily (automated); weekly comprehensive review |
| **Frequency** | Daily automated checks; weekly manual review; monthly comprehensive reconciliation |
| **Volume** | ~2.8M POS transactions/month across 200 stores; ~130,000 daily |
| **Owner** | Revenue Assurance Analyst |
| **Participants** | Store Manager (per store), LP Analyst, Finance Supervisor |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System auto-generates daily revenue reconciliation: POS gross revenue (from POS system) vs. ERP posted revenue (from GL) vs. bank-settled revenue (from card/e-wallet settlement files + cash deposit confirmations) | System | Revenue Assurance Analyst | Automated |
| 2 | Revenue Assurance Analyst reviews daily exceptions: POS-to-GL variances > PHP 1,000 per store, unbanked revenue (cash deposit shortfall vs. POS cash tender), settlement shortfalls from card/e-wallet processors | Revenue Assurance Analyst | Finance Supervisor | 2 hours/day |
| 3 | For POS-to-GL variances: analyst investigates posting errors, duplicate entries, missing transactions from offline POS recovery (W5G), or system interface failures | Revenue Assurance Analyst | Finance Supervisor | 30 min/exception |
| 4 | For pricing compliance: system flags transactions where applied price deviates > 5% from system SRP or promotional price; Revenue Assurance Analyst investigates unauthorized overrides, sweethearting, or pricing master errors | Revenue Assurance Analyst | LP Analyst | 1 hour/day |
| 5 | LP Analyst cross-references revenue anomalies with LP exception alerts (W538) for fraud indicators: high void rates, excessive overrides, unusual discount patterns | LP Analyst | Revenue Assurance Analyst | 1 hour/day |
| 6 | Weekly comprehensive review: Revenue Assurance Analyst produces store-level revenue assurance scorecard with variance trends, exception aging, and confirmed loss by category | Revenue Assurance Analyst | Finance Supervisor | 4 hours/week |
| 7 | Finance Supervisor escalates material issues (> PHP 10,000 recurring variance or confirmed fraud) to LP investigation (W248) and Store Manager accountability review | Finance Supervisor | Finance Manager | As needed |

### System Touchpoints
- POS transaction log, ERP GL, bank settlement files (W99/W261), LP analytics (W538), pricing master (W289)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Volume of exceptions at 2.8M transactions/month can overwhelm analyst capacity**: mitigated by automated variance detection and threshold-based filtering
- **Offline POS transactions (W535) create reconciliation complexity**: mitigated by offline recovery workflow and time-bound reconciliation windows
- **Distinguishing genuine errors from fraud requires cross-functional investigation**: mitigated by LP Analyst cross-reference in Step 5

### Staffing Implication
- **Revenue Assurance Analyst**: ~2–3 hours/day + 4 hours/week + 1 day/month = ~25 hours/month. Requires dedicated headcount.
- **LP Analyst**: ~1 hour/day cross-referencing revenue anomalies = ~5 hours/week. Absorbed within existing LP role.

## W635. PFRS 16 Lease Accounting Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | New lease commencement (W117); lease modification; monthly recurring journal entries; lease termination |
| **Frequency** | Ongoing for new/modified leases; monthly for recurring entries |
| **Volume** | ~200 store leases + 4 DC leases + equipment leases + ~10 new leases/year (new stores) |
| **Owner** | Accounting Manager |
| **Participants** | Property Manager, Senior Accountant, External Auditor (annual review) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | For new lease: Property Manager provides lease agreement details (term, payment schedule, escalation clauses, renewal options, incentives) to Accounting Manager | Property Manager | Accounting Manager | 1 day |
| 2 | Senior Accountant calculates initial right-of-use (ROU) asset and lease liability using incremental borrowing rate per entity; inputs into lease accounting module | Senior Accountant | Accounting Manager | 1 day/lease |
| 3 | System auto-generates monthly journal entries: ROU asset depreciation, lease liability interest accretion, lease payment allocation (principal vs. interest) | System | Senior Accountant | Automated |
| 4 | Senior Accountant reviews auto-generated entries for accuracy; verifies against lease payment schedule; posts to GL | Senior Accountant | Accounting Manager | 0.5 day/month for all leases |
| 5 | For lease modifications (rent escalation, extension, early termination per W45): Senior Accountant remeasures ROU asset and lease liability; adjusts remaining lease term and payment schedule | Senior Accountant | Accounting Manager | 0.5 day/modification |
| 6 | For IC leases (Depot Inc. leasing from Property Mgmt Inc.): Accounting Manager verifies IC lease elimination matches during consolidation (W234); confirms transfer pricing compliance (W235) | Accounting Manager | Finance Manager | Monthly check |
| 7 | Quarterly: Senior Accountant prepares PFRS 16 disclosure schedule (lease liabilities maturity analysis, ROU asset reconciliation, lease expense breakdown, variable lease payments, sale-and-leaseback disclosures) | Senior Accountant | Accounting Manager | 1 day/quarter |
| 8 | Annual: External Auditor reviews lease accounting policies, sample of ROU calculations, and disclosure completeness | Accounting Manager | Finance Manager | During annual audit |

### System Touchpoints
- Lease accounting module (IFRS 16), GL, AP (lease payments), intercompany module (IC leases), consolidation module

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Complex lease terms (escalation, options, incentives) require manual interpretation**: mitigated by standardized lease abstraction template and lease accounting module
- **IC lease accounting doubles the workload for 200+ leases**: mitigated by automated IC elimination in consolidation module
- **Lease modification frequency with 200 stores**: mitigated by standard modification workflow and system-driven remeasurement
- **Keeping incremental borrowing rate current per entity**: mitigated by quarterly rate review aligned with treasury
- **PFRS 16 disclosure requirements are extensive**: mitigated by system-generated disclosure schedules in Step 7

### Staffing Implication
- **Senior Accountant**: 1 day/lease for initial setup + 0.5 day/month recurring + 0.5 day/modification + 1 day/quarter disclosure = ~8–10 days/month. Requires dedicated lease accounting capacity.
- **Accounting Manager**: Monthly IC review + quarterly review = ~4 hours/month. Absorbed within existing role.

## W636. Standardized Balance Sheet Account Reconciliation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Monthly close calendar (day 1–3 of month-end close per W9A) |
| **Frequency** | Monthly for all accounts; daily for bank accounts (per W89) |
| **Volume** | ~50–80 balance sheet accounts per entity × 5 entities = ~250–400 reconciliations/month |
| **Owner** | Accounting Manager |
| **Participants** | Senior Accountant (per entity), Finance Manager (reviewer) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System generates reconciliation templates for all balance sheet accounts with opening balance, period activity, closing balance, and supporting sub-ledger detail | System | Accounting Manager | Automated |
| 2 | Senior Accountant completes reconciliation per account: verifies balance to supporting documentation (sub-ledger, third-party statements, schedules), identifies reconciling items, classifies items (timing difference, error, omission, unknown) | Senior Accountant | Accounting Manager | 3–5 days |
| 3 | For reconciling items: Senior Accountant creates resolution task with owner, root cause, and target resolution date; items > 90 days auto-escalate to Finance Manager | Senior Accountant | Accounting Manager | Ongoing |
| 4 | Accounting Manager reviews all reconciliations for completeness and accuracy; verifies no accounts are unreconciled; signs off per entity | Accounting Manager | Finance Manager | 1 day/entity |
| 5 | Finance Manager reviews consolidated reconciliation dashboard: completion rate, reconciling item aging (current, 30, 60, 90+ days), high-risk accounts (cash, IC, VAT, inventory reserves) | Finance Manager | CFO | 0.5 day |
| 6 | System auto-generates reconciliation completion tracker and aging report for month-end close package (W9A) | System | Accounting Manager | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- GL, sub-ledgers (AP, AR, inventory, fixed assets), bank statements, intercompany module, reconciliation tool

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Volume of reconciliations (250–400/month) strains team capacity**: mitigated by system-generated templates and auto-matching for standard account types
- **Complex accounts (IC, deferred revenue, VAT) require multi-source verification**: mitigated by dedicated reconciliation procedures for high-risk accounts
- **Reconciling items accumulate if not resolved within 30 days**: mitigated by auto-escalation at 90 days and monthly aging review
- **High-volume entities may fall behind during peak periods**: mitigated by staggered close calendar and resource sharing across entities

### Staffing Implication
- **Senior Accountant**: 3–5 days/month per entity on reconciliations. With 5 entities, requires team of 3–4 Senior Accountants. Absorbed within existing team.
- **Accounting Manager**: 1 day/entity for review = 5 days/month. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Finance Manager**: 0.5 day/month for consolidated review. Absorbed within existing role.

## W637. Financial Controls Testing & Monitoring

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Quarterly control testing cycle; triggered by control exception alerts |
| **Frequency** | Quarterly comprehensive testing; continuous monitoring for automated controls |
| **Volume** | 67 controls per Internal Controls Matrix; ~25 key controls requiring quarterly testing |
| **Owner** | Accounting Manager |
| **Participants** | Senior Accountant (tester), Finance Manager (reviewer), Internal Audit (independent validation per W331) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Accounting Manager prepares quarterly testing plan: selects controls for testing per risk-based rotation (all key controls tested annually; high-risk controls tested quarterly) | Accounting Manager | Finance Manager | 0.5 day |
| 2 | Senior Accountant executes testing per control: selects sample of transactions (statistically valid sample size per population), verifies control operated as designed, documents test results with evidence | Senior Accountant | Accounting Manager | 3–5 days |
| 3 | For automated controls: system generates exception report for control overrides, late approvals, skipped authorizations, and tolerance threshold breaches; Senior Accountant investigates exceptions | Senior Accountant | Accounting Manager | 1 day |
| 4 | Accounting Manager classifies findings: operating effectively, deficiency (design or operating), significant deficiency, material weakness; documents root cause for any deficiency | Accounting Manager | Finance Manager | 1 day |
| 5 | For identified deficiencies: Accounting Manager creates remediation plan with responsible owner, corrective action, and target date; tracks remediation to completion | Accounting Manager | Finance Manager | Ongoing |
| 6 | Finance Manager reviews testing results and remediation status; escalates significant deficiencies to CFO and Audit Committee | Finance Manager | CFO | 0.5 day |
| 7 | Quarterly results shared with Internal Audit (W331) for independent validation and external audit coordination (W95) | Accounting Manager | Finance Manager | 0.5 day |

### System Touchpoints
- Internal controls matrix, approval workflow logs, exception reports, GL audit trail, remediation tracker

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Testing resource constraints in a lean finance team**: mitigated by risk-based rotation and automated control monitoring
- **Sample-based testing may miss infrequent control failures**: mitigated by continuous monitoring of automated controls in Step 3
- **Remediation tracking can fall behind without dedicated follow-up**: mitigated by system-tracked remediation with escalation at 30/60/90 days
- **External auditor reliance on management testing requires rigorous documentation**: mitigated by standardized testing templates and evidence repository

### Staffing Implication
- **Senior Accountant**: 3–5 days/quarter on testing + 1 day/quarter on automated control review = ~4–6 days/quarter. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Accounting Manager**: 0.5 day/quarter planning + 1 day/quarter classification = ~1.5 days/quarter. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Finance Manager**: 0.5 day/quarter review + 0.5 day/quarter escalation = ~1 day/quarter. Absorbed within existing role.

## W638. Period-End Journal Entry Review & Approval

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Manual journal entry posted during period-end close (W9A) |
| **Frequency** | Monthly (~50–100 manual JEs per entity per month-end) |
| **Volume** | ~250–500 manual JEs across 5 entities per month-end |
| **Owner** | Accounting Manager |
| **Participants** | Senior Accountant (preparer), Finance Manager (reviewer/approver), CFO (material entry approver) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Senior Accountant prepares manual journal entry with: debit/credit accounts, amounts, business justification, supporting documentation attachment, and JE classification (standard, correcting, estimate, overriding) | Senior Accountant | Accounting Manager | 15 min/JE |
| 2 | System auto-routes JE for approval based on approval matrix: routine entries < PHP 500K → Accounting Manager; entries PHP 500K–5M → Finance Manager; entries > PHP 5M or top-side consolidation entries → CFO | System | Accounting Manager | Automated |
| 3 | For correcting and overriding JEs: system flags for enhanced review; requires original transaction reference and root cause documentation | System | Accounting Manager | Automated flag |
| 4 | Approver reviews JE: validates account coding, verifies supporting documentation, assesses business rationale, confirms amount accuracy, checks for proper period | Approver | Finance Manager | 10 min/JE |
| 5 | Approver approves or rejects JE; rejected JEs return to preparer with comments; system prevents period-close until all pending JEs are resolved | Approver | Finance Manager | 5 min/JE |
| 6 | Post-close: Finance Manager reviews complete manual JE register for the period; flags unusual patterns (late entries, high-value estimates, reversing entries that didn't reverse) | Finance Manager | CFO | 2 hours |
| 7 | System generates monthly JE analytics: manual JE count and value by type, preparer, and entity; auto-reversal tracking; override frequency | System | Accounting Manager | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- GL journal entry module, approval workflow, period-lock controls, supporting document repository

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Manual JEs are highest risk for financial misstatement**: mitigated by tiered approval matrix, enhanced review for correcting/overriding entries, and post-close analytics
- **Time pressure during 5-day close incentivizes rubber-stamp approvals**: mitigated by system-enforced documentation requirements and post-close review in Step 6
- **Complex correcting entries require deep investigation that close timeline may not allow**: mitigated by root cause documentation requirement and escalation to Finance Manager
- **Auto-reversal failures create balance sheet noise**: mitigated by system tracking of auto-reversal status and monthly analytics in Step 7

### Staffing Implication
- **Senior Accountant**: 15 min/JE × 50–100 JEs = ~12–25 hours/month-end per entity. Absorbed across existing accounting team.
- **Accounting Manager**: Review of all JEs per entity = ~8–16 hours/month-end. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Finance Manager**: Post-close review + CFO-level approvals = ~4–6 hours/month-end. Absorbed within existing role.

## W639. Rolling Forecast & Financial Scenario Planning

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Monthly forecast update cycle; triggered by material business change (new store delay, major vendor disruption, FX shock) |
| **Frequency** | Monthly 12-month rolling forecast update; quarterly scenario analysis |
| **Volume** | 1 consolidated forecast per month; 3 scenarios (base, upside, downside) per quarter |
| **Owner** | FP&A Manager |
| **Participants** | FP&A Analyst, Category Managers (revenue input), HR Manager (labor cost input), VP Supply Chain (COGS input), CFO (review) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | FP&A Analyst extracts actual month results and compares against prior forecast; identifies forecast vs. actual variances by major line item (revenue, gross margin, operating expenses, capex) | FP&A Analyst | FP&A Manager | 1 day |
| 2 | Category Managers provide updated revenue outlook by category considering: current sell-through trends, promotional calendar impact, new store ramp curves, competitive landscape | Category Manager | VP Merchandising | 1 day |
| 3 | VP Supply Chain provides updated COGS outlook: commodity price trends, FX forecast impact on imports, vendor price negotiations, and logistics cost changes | VP Supply Chain | COO | 0.5 day |
| 4 | HR Manager provides updated labor cost outlook: hiring plan changes, wage rate adjustments, new store staffing | HR Manager | CHRO | 0.5 day |
| 5 | FP&A Analyst updates 12-month rolling forecast model incorporating all inputs; calculates net income, EBITDA, and cash flow implications | FP&A Analyst | FP&A Manager | 2 days |
| 6 | Quarterly: FP&A Manager develops three scenarios — base (most likely), upside (accelerated growth, favorable FX), downside (consumer spending decline, adverse FX, supply disruption) with probability weighting | FP&A Manager | CFO | 2 days |
| 7 | FP&A Manager presents rolling forecast and scenario analysis to CFO and executive team with key risks, sensitivities, and recommended management actions | FP&A Manager | CFO | 0.5 day |
| 8 | CFO approves forecast for Board reporting; updates cash flow projections for treasury planning (W589/W233) and covenant compliance monitoring (W319) | CFO | CEO | 0.5 day |

### System Touchpoints
- FP&A planning module, GL actuals, budget module (W26), treasury forecast (W589), demand planning (W31)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Input quality depends on functional team engagement and timeliness**: mitigated by structured input templates and deadline enforcement
- **Forecast accuracy degrades rapidly for months 9–12**: mitigated by monthly re-forecasting and probability-weighted scenarios
- **Scenario analysis requires assumptions that may prove incorrect**: mitigated by quarterly refresh and sensitivity analysis
- **Organizational resistance to downside scenario presentation**: mitigated by framing scenarios as risk management tools, not performance evaluation

### Staffing Implication
- **FP&A Analyst**: 1 day variance analysis + 2 days model update = ~3 days/month. Absorbed within existing FP&A role.
- **FP&A Manager**: 2 days/quarter scenario analysis + 0.5 day presentation + 0.5 day review = ~1 day/month + 3 days/quarter. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Category Managers / VP Supply Chain / HR Manager**: 0.5–1 day/month each providing inputs. Absorbed within existing roles.

## W640. Merchant Fee Analysis & Payment Cost Optimization

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Monthly settlement data availability; quarterly negotiation cycle |
| **Frequency** | Monthly analysis; quarterly optimization review; annual contract renegotiation |
| **Volume** | ~2.8M POS card/e-wallet transactions/month + ~42,900 ecommerce payment transactions/month |
| **Owner** | Finance Analyst |
| **Participants** | Treasury Analyst, IT Payments Lead, Finance Manager |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System aggregates total payment processing cost by payment method (Visa/MC/JCB, GCash, Maya, GrabPay, bank transfer, COD), by channel (POS vs. ecommerce), and by store | System | Finance Analyst | Automated |
| 2 | Finance Analyst calculates effective merchant fee rate (total fees ÷ total transaction value) per payment method, per channel, and per store; compares against contracted rates | Finance Analyst | Finance Manager | 1 day |
| 3 | Finance Analyst calculates total cost of acceptance per payment method including: MDR, per-transaction fees, settlement fees, chargeback costs, FX conversion fees, and cash handling costs (CIT per W174, deposit processing, reconciliation labor) | Finance Analyst | Finance Manager | 1 day |
| 4 | Treasury Analyst tracks payment method mix shift (cash → card → e-wallet) month-over-month; models financial impact of mix changes on total payment cost | Treasury Analyst | Finance Manager | 0.5 day |
| 5 | Quarterly: Finance Analyst benchmarks BuildRight's effective rates against Philippine market rates and peer retailers; identifies overpriced payment methods | Finance Analyst | Finance Manager | 1 day |
| 6 | Finance Manager develops optimization recommendations: volume-based rate renegotiation targets, customer steering strategies (e-wallet incentives over card), surcharging feasibility assessment per payment method, new payment method evaluation (QR PH, BNPL) | Finance Manager | CFO | 1 day |
| 7 | IT Payments Lead evaluates technical feasibility of recommended changes; estimates implementation cost and timeline | IT Payments Lead | Finance Manager | 1 day |
| 8 | CFO approves optimization plan; Finance Manager executes rate renegotiations with acquirers and e-wallet providers | CFO | CEO | 2–4 weeks |

### System Touchpoints
- Payment settlement module (W99/W261/W611), merchant fee schedule master, bank statements, POS transaction data

### Pain Points / Risks
- **PHP 500M–1B annual merchant fees make this a material cost center**: mitigated by dedicated monthly analysis and quarterly optimization reviews
- **Philippine payment landscape shifting rapidly with new methods**: mitigated by quarterly evaluation of new payment methods in Step 6
- **Rate negotiations favor high-volume merchants but BuildRight may lack leverage vs. major banks**: mitigated by multi-provider strategy and competitive bidding
- **Customer resistance to steering tactics**: mitigated by incentive-based steering (discounts for preferred methods) rather than punitive surcharges

### Staffing Implication
- **Finance Analyst**: 2–3 days/month on analysis + 1 day/quarter benchmarking = ~3–4 days/month. Absorbed within existing Finance Analyst role.
- **Treasury Analyst**: 0.5 day/month on mix shift tracking. Absorbed within existing Treasury role.
- **Finance Manager**: 1 day/quarter on optimization recommendations. Absorbed within existing role.
- **IT Payments Lead**: 1 day/quarter on feasibility assessment. Absorbed within existing role.

| **Participants** | Cost Accountant, Finance Manager, VP Store Operations (for store assets), Engineering Manager |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System auto-generates depreciation schedule for all asset classes per entity using configured depreciation methods (straight-line for buildings/furniture, declining balance for IT equipment, units-of-production for fleet) | Fixed Asset Accountant | Fixed Asset Accountant | 2 hrs |
| 2 | Fixed Asset Accountant reviews depreciation run exceptions: new assets without class assignment, disposed assets with remaining NBV, transferred assets between locations | Fixed Asset Accountant | Finance Manager | 3 hrs |
| 3 | For 200 store build-outs: verify component depreciation for significant components (HVAC, electrical, flooring, racking, signage) with different useful lives per PFRS/PAS 16 component approach | Fixed Asset Accountant | Engineering Manager | 4 hrs |
| 4 | Fixed Asset Accountant reconciles depreciation expense to fixed asset register — total accumulated depreciation per entity | Fixed Asset Accountant | Finance Manager | 2 hrs |
| 5 | Cost Accountant allocates depreciation to cost centers (store-level P&L per W489, DC, HQ) | Cost Accountant | Finance Manager | 2 hrs |
| 6 | Monthly: post depreciation journal entries per entity; quarterly: review component split for accuracy; annually: perform impairment testing per PAS 36 on assets with indicators of impairment | Fixed Asset Accountant | Finance Manager | 2 hrs |
| 7 | Fixed Asset Accountant generates depreciation reports per entity per class for tax return support (BIR 1702) | Fixed Asset Accountant | Finance Manager | 2 hrs |
| 8 | Finance Manager reviews and approves depreciation entries | Finance Manager | VP Finance | 1 hr |

- Cost center allocation

### Pain Points / Risks
- Component depreciation complexity for 200 stores; PAS 36 impairment judgment; tax vs. book depreciation differences; asset transfers between locations causing duplicate/missed depreciation

## W664. Cash Flow Variance Analysis & Liquidity Stress Testing

| **Frequency** | Monthly variance analysis; quarterly stress testing |

| **Participants** | FP&A Analyst, Finance Manager, CFO |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Treasury Manager compares actual cash flow for prior month against forecast per W589: cash receipts by channel (POS, ecommerce, trade AR, wholesale), cash disbursements by category (vendor payments, payroll, capex, taxes, IC settlements) | Treasury Manager | Treasury Manager | 2 hrs |
| 2 | FP&A Analyst analyzes material variances (> 5% deviation): revenue shortfall/surprise, payment timing shifts, unexpected capex, tax payment timing, FX impact on import payments | FP&A Analyst | Finance Manager | 2 hrs |
| 3 | Treasury Manager updates rolling 4-week forecast per W589 with variance-informed adjustments | Treasury Manager | Finance Manager | 1 hr |
| 4 | Quarterly: Treasury Manager conducts liquidity stress test with three scenarios: Base (current forecast), Moderate Stress (15% revenue decline + 30-day AR extension + 15% vendor payment acceleration), Severe Stress (25% revenue decline + 60-day AR extension + supply chain disruption requiring emergency procurement) | Treasury Manager | CFO | 4 hrs |
| 5 | For each scenario, Treasury Manager models: minimum cash balance compliance per entity (target ≥ 2 weeks operating expenses), debt covenant compliance per W319, ability to fund planned capex, dividend capacity per W327 | Treasury Manager | Finance Manager | 3 hrs |
| 6 | Treasury Manager presents stress test results to CFO with recommended contingency actions (draw on debt facility per W319, defer discretionary capex, accelerate AR collection per W108, extend vendor terms negotiation) | Treasury Manager | CFO | 2 hrs |
| 7 | CFO approves contingency plan if Moderate Stress scenario shows covenant breach | CFO | Board | 1 hr |

### Time Estimate

- Forecast accuracy for a seasonal retail business with typhoon disruption risk; multi-entity cash pooling complexity; covenant headroom declining during expansion periods
