## W711. BIR Withholding Tax (EWT) Certificate Form 2307 Issuance to Vendors

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Monthly EWT payment run to BIR; or vendor requests BIR Form 2307 for their own tax credit claim |
| **Frequency** | Monthly (batch issuance); ad-hoc per vendor request |
| **Volume** | ~800-1,000 vendor EWT certificates per month; ~10,000-12,000 per year |
| **Owner** | Tax Accountant |
| **Participants** | AP Clerk, Finance Manager, Vendor, BIR |

### Background

Under Philippine tax law, BuildRight Depot is required to issue BIR Form 2307 (Certificate of Creditable Tax Withheld at Source) to every vendor from whom Expanded Withholding Tax (EWT) has been deducted. Vendors use Form 2307 as a tax credit against their own income tax liability. Failure to issue 2307 on time exposes BuildRight to BIR penalties and damages vendor relationships. With ~800-1,000 active vendors subject to varying EWT rates (1-10% depending on service/goods category), monthly batch issuance is essential. This workflow complements W475 (which covers COLLECTION of Form 2307 from B2B customers) and W90 (monthly tax filing).

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Monthly EWT extraction**: after AP payment run per W556, system extracts all EWT transactions for the month grouped by vendor: vendor TIN, vendor name, ATC (Alphanumeric Tax Code), gross amount, EWT rate, EWT amount withheld, payment date, PO/invoice reference | System | Tax Accountant | Automated |
| 2 | **2307 batch generation**: system generates BIR Form 2307 for each vendor with EWT transactions during the period: (a) Part I — Payor info (BuildRight legal name, TIN, address, registered address per BIR registration); (b) Part II — Payee info (vendor name, TIN, registered address); (c) Part III — Details of monthly income payments and taxes withheld: date of payment, ATC, nature of income payment, gross amount, EWT rate, EWT amount; (d) Part IV — total amount withheld; (e) system auto-calculates totals and validates against AP EWT ledger | System | Tax Accountant | Batch: 2-3 hours for ~1,000 forms |
| 3 | **Validation and review**: Tax Accountant reviews batch: (a) verify vendor TIN format matches BIR registration (XXX-XXX-XXX-000); (b) verify ATC codes are correct per income nature (e.g., WI 010 for professional services, WI 028 for rental, WI 160 for goods); (c) verify EWT rates match ATC code requirements; (d) check for duplicate vendor entries or missing vendor data; (e) flag exceptions for manual correction | Tax Accountant | Finance Manager | 2-3 hours/month |
| 4 | **Approval and signing**: Finance Manager reviews summary report: total EWT certificates generated, total EWT amounts per ATC category, reconciliation against BIR Form 1601-E monthly remittance per W260; approves batch for issuance; authorized signatory (Finance Manager or Controller) signs certificates | Finance Manager | Controller | 30-60 min/month |
| 5 | **Distribution to vendors**: system distributes Form 2307 to vendors: (a) primary: email PDF to vendor's registered email address from vendor master; (b) secondary: upload to vendor self-service portal per W705 for vendor download; (c) physical mail for vendors without email (rare); (d) system logs distribution date and method per certificate | System / AP Clerk | Tax Accountant | Automated + 1 hour/month for physical mail exceptions |
| 6 | **Quarterly reconciliation**: at quarter-end, Tax Accountant reconciles: (a) total EWT certificates issued (quarterly sum of Forms 2307) vs. total EWT remitted to BIR per Form 1601-E quarterly filing; (b) any unreconciled difference investigated and corrected; (c) vendor-level reconciliation: verify every vendor with EWT deduction received their 2307; (d) BIR Form 1604-E (Annual Information Return of Income Payments Subject to WHT) preparation uses 2307 data as primary source | Tax Accountant | Finance Manager | 4-6 hours/quarter |
| 7 | **Ad-hoc vendor request handling**: when a vendor requests a copy or re-issuance of their Form 2307: (a) AP Clerk verifies vendor identity (TIN + registered business name); (b) system retrieves historical 2307 from document archive per W255; (c) re-issue via email or portal download; (d) if vendor disputes EWT amount: Tax Accountant investigates against AP transaction history and resolves per W244 (vendor invoice dispute) | AP Clerk / Tax Accountant | Tax Accountant | 15-30 min per request; ~20-30 ad-hoc requests/month |

### System Touchpoints

- AP module EWT transaction extraction linked to payment run per W556
- BIR Form 2307 generation engine with ATC code mapping and auto-calculation
- Vendor master data (TIN, registered name, address, email) per W287
- BIR eFPS integration for Form 1601-E cross-reference per W260
- Vendor self-service portal for 2307 download per W705
- Document archive for historical 2307 retrieval per W255
- Tax & regulatory master data per W293 (ATC codes, EWT rates, BIR form mapping)

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Vendor TIN discrepancies**: vendor TIN on file may not match their BIR-registered TIN (vendors sometimes have multiple TINs or registration updates), causing Form 2307 to be rejected by the vendor's own tax preparer and requiring re-issuance
- **ATC code misclassification**: incorrect assignment of ATC codes (e.g., WI 010 for services that should be WI 011) results in wrong EWT rates on the 2307, creating tax credit issues for vendors and potential BIR penalties for BuildRight
- **Late issuance penalties**: BIR requires 2307 issuance within 20 days after the end of each quarter (for quarterly filers); failure to issue on time results in compromise penalties and vendor complaints
- **High volume of ad-hoc requests**: vendors frequently lose their 2307 copies and request re-issuance, consuming AP Clerk time disproportionate to the task's complexity
- **Multi-entity complexity**: BuildRight's 5 legal entities each issue separate 2307s; vendors receiving payments from multiple entities (e.g., Depot Inc. for goods + Logistics Inc. for services) receive multiple certificates that must be correctly attributed

### Staffing Implication

Tax Accountant: ~8-12 hours/month on 2307 management (3 hours batch generation + 3 hours review + 2 hours quarterly reconciliation + ad-hoc). AP Clerk: ~3-4 hours/month on distribution and ad-hoc requests. Absorbed within existing headcount. No incremental FTE.

### Time Estimate

**Total**: ~12-16 hours/month (2-3 hours batch generation + 2-3 hours review + 30-60 min approval + 1 hour distribution + 3-6 hours/quarter reconciliation + 5-10 hours/month ad-hoc). Peak at quarter-end due to reconciliation.

---

## W712. Financial Restatement & Prior-Year Adjustment Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Discovery of material error in posted financial statements; external auditor adjustment during annual audit per W351; BIR assessment requiring correction of prior-period tax filing; management-initiated correction of accounting policy misapplication |
| **Frequency** | Ad-hoc (rare) |
| **Volume** | ~5-10 material restatements per year |
| **Owner** | Controller |
| **Participants** | Finance Manager, Tax Accountant, External Auditor, CFO, VP Legal |

### Background

When a material error is discovered in previously issued financial statements — whether from an accounting policy misapplication, calculation error, omission, or fraud — PFRS/PAS 8 requires correction with retrospective application. For a PHP 62.3B revenue company with 5 legal entities and BIR/SEC reporting obligations, restatements require careful documentation, multi-entity impact assessment, and coordinated regulatory communication. This workflow complements W9A (month-end close) and W9B (year-end close) by addressing the rare but critical scenario of correcting already-reported financials.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Error identification and assessment**: (a) source identifies error: internal discovery (month-end review, internal audit per W121, reconciliation per W636), external auditor per W351, or BIR assessment per W77; (b) Controller performs materiality assessment: quantify financial impact on each affected entity and consolidated group; (c) determine if error is material (requires restatement) or immaterial (prospectively corrected per W638); (d) if material: escalate to CFO with written assessment | Controller | CFO | 2-4 hours |
| 2 | **Restatement scoping**: Controller and Finance Manager define scope: (a) identify all affected periods (current year, prior years); (b) identify all affected entities (1-5 entities depending on error); (c) map impact to: income statement lines, balance sheet accounts, cash flow items, equity changes; (d) assess tax impact: VAT, income tax, withholding tax, local business tax; (e) identify downstream impacts: budget variance per W26, management reporting per W35, KPI calculations per W665; (f) prepare restatement memo documenting error, impact, and proposed correction | Controller / Finance Manager | CFO | 4-8 hours |
| 3 | **CFO and Audit Committee approval**: (a) CFO reviews restatement memo; (b) if error affects consolidated financial statements: present to Audit Committee for approval before execution; (c) if error affects BIR filings: engage VP Legal for assessment of penalty exposure and voluntary disclosure strategy; (d) approved restatement plan documented with authorization signatures | CFO | Audit Committee | 1-2 hours |
| 4 | **Journal entry preparation**: Tax Accountant / Finance Manager prepares correcting entries: (a) prior-year adjustments posted to retained earnings (per PFRS/PAS 8 retrospective application); (b) current-year corrections posted to affected accounts with detailed supporting schedules; (c) multi-entity entries: IC adjustments if error affected intercompany transactions per W14; (d) tax adjustments: correcting entries for VAT, income tax, withholding tax as needed; (e) all entries reviewed by Controller before posting | Finance Manager / Tax Accountant | Controller | 4-8 hours per entity |
| 5 | **Period-end journal entry review per W638**: correcting entries submitted through W638 review and approval process with enhanced scrutiny: (a) Controller reviews all restatement journal entries; (b) dual approval required: Controller + CFO; (c) entries posted with special "Restatement" flag in audit trail; (d) system generates restatement-specific audit log | Controller / CFO | CFO | 2-4 hours |
| 6 | **Financial statement revision and re-issuance**: (a) revise affected financial statements for all impacted periods; (b) update comparative figures (prior year columns) per PFRS/PAS 8; (c) prepare revised notes to financial statements with restatement disclosure; (d) if previously filed with SEC: prepare and file amended AFS per W481; (e) if previously filed with BIR: prepare and file amended tax returns per W260; (f) re-issue internal management reports for affected periods per W35 | Controller / Finance Manager | CFO | 1-2 days |
| 7 | **External auditor communication**: (a) notify external auditor of restatement per W351 coordination; (b) provide restatement memo, supporting schedules, and corrected financial statements; (c) external auditor assesses restatement impact on their audit opinion; (d) if restatement affects current-year audit: adjust audit plan and timeline; (e) obtain external auditor sign-off on restated figures | Controller | CFO | 2-4 hours |
| 8 | **Root cause analysis and controls remediation**: (a) Controller conducts RCA to determine how the error occurred and why it was not detected; (b) identify control failures: preventive, detective, or monitoring; (c) implement remediation: process changes, system validations, additional review steps; (d) update financial controls testing per W637; (e) document lessons learned and communicate to finance team | Controller | CFO | 4-8 hours |

### System Touchpoints

- GL module with prior-period adjustment posting capability and retroactive effect
- Multi-entity posting with automated IC adjustment per W14
- Audit trail with "Restatement" flag for enhanced traceability
- Period-end journal entry review workflow per W638
- Consolidated financial statement re-generation per W9A
- BIR eFPS amended return filing per W260
- SEC reportorial requirements filing per W481
- Financial controls testing module per W637

### Pain Points / Risks

- **BIR penalty exposure**: restatements affecting tax filings trigger BIR penalties (compromise penalty of 25% of deficiency tax per NIRC); voluntary disclosure before BIR discovery mitigates penalty severity
- **SEC filing deadlines**: amended AFS must be filed within SEC-prescribed periods; late filing penalties and potential SEC scrutiny of the company's financial reporting controls
- **Audit opinion impact**: material restatements may result in external auditor issuing qualified opinion or emphasis of matter paragraph, affecting lender confidence and debt covenant compliance per W319
- **Multi-entity cascading effects**: an error in one entity may cascade through IC eliminations per W234, transfer pricing per W235, and consolidation per W9A, requiring corrections across all 5 entities
- **Internal morale and credibility**: financial restatements erode management's credibility with the board and external stakeholders; transparent communication is essential but uncomfortable

### Staffing Implication

Ad-hoc (rare). Each material restatement requires 2-4 days of concentrated effort from Controller, Finance Manager, and Tax Accountant. No incremental headcount; absorbed by existing finance team with reprioritization of routine tasks. External auditor involvement per W351 adds 2-4 hours of coordination per restatement.

### Time Estimate

**Total**: 3-5 business days per material restatement (2-4 hours identification + 4-8 hours scoping + 1-2 hours approval + 4-8 hours journal entries + 2-4 hours review + 1-2 days re-issuance + 2-4 hours auditor communication + 4-8 hours RCA). Timeline extends if multiple entities or periods are affected.

## W713. Corporate Credit Card Program Management & Expense Reconciliation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Monthly corporate credit card statement receipt; or employee expense report submission |
| **Frequency** | Monthly (statement reconciliation); daily (transaction monitoring) |
| **Volume** | ~150-200 corporate cardholders; ~3,000-4,000 transactions/month; ~PHP 15-20M monthly spend |
| **Owner** | Finance Manager |
| **Participants** | Cardholder (Employee), Approving Manager, AP Clerk, Finance Manager, Bank |

### Background

BuildRight Depot issues corporate credit cards to ~150-200 authorized employees (senior management, regional managers, category managers, procurement team, and traveling staff) for business expenses including travel, entertainment, office supplies, and incidental purchases. With ~PHP 15-20M in monthly card spend across 5 legal entities, proper reconciliation with the general ledger, policy compliance enforcement, and fraud monitoring are essential. This workflow complements W74 (employee expense reimbursement) by handling the corporate card lifecycle.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Transaction monitoring (daily)**: system imports corporate card transactions daily via bank feed per FIN-009: (a) automated transaction categorization based on merchant category code (MCC); (b) policy violation alerts: transactions exceeding per-transaction limit, restricted merchant categories (casinos, personal items), weekend/holiday transactions without travel approval; (c) fraud pattern detection: transactions in unusual locations, rapid sequential charges, split transactions to avoid limits; (d) alerts routed to cardholder's approving manager for review | System / AP Clerk | Finance Manager | 30-60 min/day |
| 2 | **Monthly statement reconciliation**: upon monthly statement receipt: (a) system matches card transactions to submitted expense reports (where applicable); (b) unmatched transactions flagged: cardholder must submit expense report within 5 business days; (c) system reconciles statement total vs. GL accrual for outstanding card transactions; (d) verify all transactions have supporting receipts per retention policy W256; (e) bank charges, interest, and FX markups verified and coded | AP Clerk | Finance Manager | 4-6 hours/month |
| 3 | **Expense report review and approval**: cardholder submits expense report per W74 process with corporate card charges pre-populated: (a) each line item requires: business purpose, cost center allocation, project code (if applicable), receipt attachment; (b) approving manager reviews for policy compliance: per-meal limits, hotel rate caps, airfare class restrictions; (c) exceptions require Finance Manager approval with justification; (d) approved expenses coded to correct GL accounts and cost centers | Cardholder / Approving Manager | Finance Manager | 10-15 min per report |
| 4 | **Payment processing**: (a) system generates corporate card payment file per W556; (b) full balance payment (no revolving credit on corporate cards); (c) payment funded from appropriate entity bank account based on cardholder's employing entity; (d) split payment if cardholder's expenses span multiple entities; (e) payment confirmed via bank statement reconciliation per W89 | AP Clerk / Finance Manager | Controller | 1-2 hours/month |
| 5 | **Card lifecycle management**: (a) new card issuance: employee request with manager approval → Finance Manager reviews credit limit request → bank application → card activation; (b) credit limit review: annual review of all cardholder limits based on role, travel frequency, and historical spend; (c) card suspension/cancellation: employee separation triggers immediate card suspension per W43 offboarding checklist; policy violations trigger temporary suspension; (d) lost/stolen card: 24-hour reporting requirement → bank hotline → emergency replacement | AP Clerk / Finance Manager | Finance Manager | Ongoing |
| 6 | **Quarterly program review**: (a) spend analytics: total spend by category, department, entity, and cardholder; (b) policy compliance rate: % of transactions with timely expense reports, % with receipts, % of flagged transactions; (c) cost optimization: negotiate bank fee reductions based on volume, evaluate reward programs, assess card program vs. petty cash usage per W25; (d) fraud summary: confirmed fraud incidents, recovery rate, control improvements; (e) report to CFO with recommendations | Finance Manager | CFO | 4-6 hours/quarter |

### System Touchpoints

- Corporate card bank feed integration per FIN-009
- Expense management module with receipt OCR and auto-categorization
- GL coding and cost center allocation automation
- Approval workflow per W74 (employee expense reimbursement)
- AP payment run integration per W556
- Bank reconciliation per W89
- Employee master data per W292 for cardholder management
- Policy violation detection engine with configurable rules

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Receipt collection compliance**: employees frequently fail to attach receipts for card transactions, requiring follow-up and creating reconciliation delays; estimated 15-20% of transactions initially lack receipts
- **Personal expense commingling**: employees occasionally use corporate cards for personal expenses, requiring manual identification, reclassification, and employee reimbursement to the company
- **Fraud exposure**: lost or stolen corporate cards can generate significant fraudulent charges before cancellation; BuildRight's liability depends on bank notification speed
- **Multi-entity complexity**: corporate card program spans 5 entities; cardholders may incur expenses on behalf of different entities than their employer, requiring intercompany allocation per W435
- **Policy exception overload**: senior executives frequently request policy exceptions (higher meal limits, premium hotel rates) that undermine program standardization

### Staffing Implication

AP Clerk: ~1-2 hours/day on transaction monitoring and follow-up. Finance Manager: ~6-8 hours/month on reconciliation, approval escalation, and program review. No incremental headcount; absorbed within existing AP and finance team.

### Time Estimate

**Total**: ~20-30 hours/month (30-60 min/day monitoring + 4-6 hours statement reconciliation + 2-3 hours payment processing + ongoing card management + 4-6 hours/quarter program review). Peak at month-end when statements arrive.

---

## W714. Store-Level Daily Financial Summary Reporting & Flash P&L

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | End of each store operating day |
| **Frequency** | Daily (all 200 stores) |
| **Volume** | 200 daily flash P&L reports; consolidated into regional and national summary |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Cashier (data input), Store Manager (review), Regional Manager (consolidation), FP&A Analyst |

### Background

BuildRight Depot's 200 stores generate ~PHP 5.04B in monthly in-store revenue. Daily financial visibility at the store level is critical for operational decision-making, trend identification, and early warning of revenue or cost anomalies. While W489 covers the store-level operating budget and monthly cost control, and W67 covers monthly store performance review, there is no workflow for the daily financial flash that bridges the gap between real-time POS data (W533) and monthly reporting. This workflow generates a daily flash P&L for each store, consolidated into regional and national summaries for executive review.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Automated data extraction (post store close)**: after store close per W574, system automatically extracts: (a) gross revenue by department (Lumber, Plumbing, Electrical, Tiles, Tools, Hardware, Paint, Appliances, Garden, Services); (b) transaction count and average transaction value (ATV); (c) returns and voids count and value; (d) payment method mix (cash, card, e-wallet, trade account); (e) loyalty points earned and redeemed; (f) labor hours worked and overtime; (g) discount and promotion impact (markdown, employee discount, senior/PWD); (h) key service revenue (installation, cutting, rental) | System | Store Manager | Automated |
| 2 | **Flash P&L generation**: system generates daily store flash P&L: (a) Revenue: gross sales − returns − discounts = net revenue; (b) Estimated COGS: using category-level average margin from W85; (c) Estimated gross profit and margin %; (d) Estimated labor cost: hours worked × blended hourly rate from W613; (e) Other estimated variable costs: utilities allocation, supplies; (f) Estimated store contribution margin; (g) comparison vs. same day last year, vs. budget per W489, vs. last 4-week average; (h) variance flags: revenue ±10% vs. budget, margin ±2% vs. target, ATV ±15% vs. average | System | Store Manager | Automated |
| 3 | **Store Manager review**: Store Manager reviews daily flash by 09:00 next morning: (a) verify revenue total matches Z-report per W5F; (b) investigate significant variances: revenue drop (stockout? weather? competitor promo?); margin decline (excessive discounting? low-margin product mix shift?); (c) annotate variances with explanations in system; (d) flag items requiring Regional Manager attention | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 15-20 min/store/day |
| 4 | **Regional consolidation**: Regional Manager reviews daily flash for all stores in region (~30-40 stores): (a) system consolidates regional P&L summary; (b) identify bottom 5 and top 5 stores by revenue and margin performance; (c) follow up on Store Manager variance annotations; (d) escalate systemic issues (e.g., regional weather event, supply disruption) to VP Store Operations | Regional Manager | VP Store Ops | 30-45 min/day |
| 5 | **National summary generation**: FP&A generates national daily financial summary by 10:00: (a) consolidated flash P&L for all 200 stores; (b) revenue vs. budget tracking (MTD and YTD); (c) regional performance heat map; (d) top 10 positive and negative store variance narratives; (e) trend alerts: stores declining 3+ consecutive days; (f) summary distributed to C-suite via executive dashboard per RPT-001 | FP&A Analyst | CFO | 30-60 min/day |
| 6 | **Weekly trend analysis**: FP&A compiles 7-day rolling analysis: (a) week-over-week trend by region and store cluster; (b) category-level revenue trend (which departments are driving/declining); (c) labor cost efficiency: revenue per labor hour; (d) promotion effectiveness: promotional vs. non-promotional revenue days; (e) weekly flash report submitted to CFO and VP Store Operations for W575 review | FP&A Analyst | CFO | 2-3 hours/week |

### System Touchpoints

- POS transaction data per W533 (real-time event streaming)
- Store close Z-report data per W5F
- Budget and target data from W489 (store-level operating budget)
- Category margin data from W85 (product costing & margin analysis)
- Labor hours from time & attendance system per W561
- Executive dashboard per RPT-001
- Regional consolidation and reporting engine

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Estimated COGS inaccuracy**: flash P&L uses category-level average margins rather than actual item-level COGS; during periods of significant mix shift (e.g., a sale event pushing low-margin items), the flash margin can be materially misleading
- **Data latency for labor costs**: payroll hours from biometric/attendance system per W561 may not reflect real-time schedule changes (sick calls, overtime approvals), making daily labor cost estimates imprecise
- **Store Manager review fatigue**: requiring 200 Store Managers to review and annotate daily flash reports creates 200 × 20 min = ~67 hours/day of management time across the chain; risk of rubber-stamping without genuine analysis
- **Variance explanation quality**: Store Manager annotations are often generic ("weather," "low foot traffic") rather than actionable root causes, reducing the value of the daily flash for regional and national decision-making
- **Information overload at national level**: 200 stores generating daily exceptions can overwhelm FP&A; need intelligent filtering to surface only materially significant variances

### Staffing Implication

Store Managers: ~15-20 min/day each = ~3,000-4,000 hours/month across 200 stores (absorbed within existing store management duties). Regional Managers: ~30-45 min/day = ~15-20 hours/month per regional manager. FP&A Analyst: ~2-3 hours/day on daily flash + weekly analysis. No incremental headcount.

### Time Estimate

**Total per store**: 15-20 min/day for Store Manager review. **Regional consolidation**: 30-45 min/day. **National summary**: 30-60 min/day. **Weekly analysis**: 2-3 hours. System generation is automated.

---

## W750. Credit Card Installment Sales Reconciliation & Bank Settlement Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Daily bank settlement file receipt; monthly bank statement arrival |
| **Frequency** | Daily reconciliation; monthly bank statement reconciliation |
| **Volume** | ~4,000 installment transactions/day chain-wide across 5+ acquiring banks |
| **Owner** | Finance Analyst (Card Settlements) |
| **Participants** | Finance Analyst, Finance Manager, Bank Acquiring Operations |

### Background
Credit card installment sales generate complex settlement flows: the acquiring bank remits transaction amounts net of merchant discount rates (MDR), with separate handling for 0% interest subsidy deductions. With 5+ bank partners and ~4,000 daily installment transactions, systematic reconciliation ensures BuildRight Depot receives correct settlement amounts and interest subsidies are properly accounted.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Daily settlement file download**: Finance Analyst downloads daily settlement files from each acquiring bank (BDO, BPI, Metrobank, Chinabank, HSBC); files contain transaction-level detail: card number (masked), transaction amount, installment term, MDR, and net settlement amount | Finance Analyst | — | 30 min |
| 2 | **Automated matching**: System auto-matches bank settlement transactions to POS installment transaction records (W747) by transaction reference, amount, and date; flags unmatched items for manual review | System | Finance Analyst | Automated + 15 min |
| 3 | **MDR verification**: System verifies MDR per bank per transaction against contracted rates in W309 bank master; flags any MDR discrepancies (> 0.1% variance) | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | **Interest subsidy reconciliation**: For 0% promo transactions: system calculates expected interest subsidy deduction and reconciles against actual bank deduction; flags variances | System | Finance Analyst | Automated + 15 min |
| 5 | **GL posting**: System posts daily settlement: DR Bank (net amount), DR Interest Subsidy Expense (0% promo cost), DR MDR Expense, CR Installment Sales Receivable | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Unmatched item resolution**: Finance Analyst investigates unmatched items: (a) timing differences (post to next day), (b) bank errors (initiate dispute), (c) POS errors (coordinate with store) | Finance Analyst | Finance Manager | 30 min |
| 7 | **Monthly bank statement reconciliation**: Monthly: Finance Analyst reconciles accumulated daily settlements against bank statement; investigates and resolves any remaining discrepancies per W89 bank reconciliation | Finance Analyst | Finance Manager | 2 hours |
| 8 | **Bank performance reporting**: Quarterly: Finance reports MDR trends, interest subsidy costs, settlement timeliness, and chargeback rates per bank; feeds to W149 bank partnership review | Finance Analyst | Finance Manager | 4 hours/quarter |

### System Touchpoints

- Bank settlement file import module with auto-parsing per bank format
- Automated matching engine: bank settlement ↔ POS installment transaction
- MDR rate verification against W309 bank & banking partner master
- Interest subsidy calculation engine based on W747 installment terms
- GL posting automation for settlement entries
- W89 bank reconciliation integration — installment settlement as sub-ledger
- W640 merchant fee analysis — installment MDR and subsidy cost feed
- W149 bank partnership management — performance data feed

### Pain Points / Risks

- Multi-bank format complexity — each acquiring bank uses different settlement file formats and reporting periods; manual consolidation is error-prone without standardized parsing
- Interest subsidy disputes — banks may deduct incorrect subsidy amounts; resolution requires bank coordination and may take weeks
- Settlement timing delays — installment settlements may take T+2 to T+5 depending on bank, creating cash flow forecasting challenges for W589
- Chargeback risk on installment sales — installment chargebacks are more complex than straightcharge chargebacks and may involve partially-paid terms
- MDR negotiation leverage — without transaction-level MDR analysis, BuildRight lacks data to negotiate better rates with banks

### Time Estimate

Daily reconciliation: 1–2 hours. Monthly statement reconciliation: 2 hours. Quarterly bank performance report: 4 hours. Total monthly: ~40–50 hours.

### Staffing Implication

1 dedicated Finance Analyst for card settlement reconciliation, handling installment and straightcharge card settlements across all bank partners. Absorbed partially by existing payment reconciliation team. 0.5 FTE incremental for installment-specific reconciliation at current ~4,000 transactions/day volume.

---

## W751. Store-Level Emergency Cash Float Request & Expedited Replenishment

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store cash position falls below minimum operational threshold; unexpected high-volume cash transaction day; till shortage requiring emergency replenishment |
| **Frequency** | 2–3 times per store per month (ad-hoc) |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~2.5 emergency requests/month = ~500 emergency requests/month chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Finance (Treasury), CIT Service (W174), Bank |

### Background
Despite daily cash management procedures (W541), stores occasionally face emergency cash situations: higher-than-expected cash transactions (payday weekends), till shortages from cashier errors (W272), or delayed CIT pickup (W174). Emergency cash float replenishment ensures stores can continue serving cash-paying customers without disruption.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Emergency trigger**: Store Manager identifies cash shortage: (a) till float below PHP 5,000 per terminal, (b) no change available for high-denomination bills, (c) anticipated high-cash-volume day (payday, promotion) | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 2 | **Emergency request submission**: Store Manager submits emergency cash request in system: amount needed, reason, urgency level (Standard: next-day, Urgent: same-day, Critical: within 4 hours); system validates against store cash budget (W489) | Store Manager | Finance | 5 min |
| 3 | **Finance approval**: Treasury Analyst reviews request: (a) validates against store cash budget, (b) checks if standard CIT schedule can accommodate, (c) approves/rejects with documentation | Treasury Analyst | Finance Manager | 15 min |
| 4 | **Cash sourcing**: Finance determines fulfillment method: (a) expedited CIT delivery (W174) for urgent/critical, (b) bank branch emergency withdrawal by authorized store personnel, (c) inter-store cash transfer from nearby store | Treasury Analyst | Finance Manager | 15 min |
| 5 | **Cash receipt at store**: Store Manager or designated cash handler receives emergency cash; counts and verifies amount against request; signs receipt; system records emergency float addition | Store Manager | — | 15 min |
| 6 | **Reconciliation**: Emergency float tracked separately in system; reconciled in next EOD procedure (W541) and next bank reconciliation (W89) | System | Store Manager | Automated |
| 7 | **Root cause documentation**: For recurring emergency requests (> 3 per month for same store), Finance requires root cause documentation: (a) inadequate standard float allocation, (b) poor cash management practices, (c) seasonal volume shift | Finance Analyst | Store Manager | 30 min |
| 8 | **Float adjustment**: Based on root cause analysis, Finance adjusts store's standard daily float allocation in W489 store-level budget to prevent recurrence | Finance Analyst | Finance Manager | 15 min |

### System Touchpoints

- Emergency cash request module with tiered approval workflow
- W489 store-level budget integration — cash float budget validation
- W174 CIT service integration — expedited delivery coordination
- W541 cash office operations — emergency float receipt and reconciliation
- W89 bank reconciliation — emergency float tracking
- Recurring request analytics — store-level emergency frequency dashboard

### Pain Points / Risks

- Cash-in-transit security risk — emergency CIT deliveries are less predictable and potentially less secure than scheduled pickups
- Approval bottleneck — Finance Manager approval required for urgent requests may delay critical replenishment during peak hours
- Store manager unauthorized cash handling — if emergency procedures are too cumbersome, Store Managers may use personal funds or unapproved cash sources
- Seasonal pattern failure — standard float allocations based on average daily volumes are inadequate during Ber months and payday weekends; system should auto-adjust

### Time Estimate

Request submission: 5 min. Finance approval: 15 min. Cash sourcing: 15 min. Receipt at store: 15 min. Total: 50 min from request to receipt (urgent/same-day). Critical (4-hour): depends on CIT proximity.

### Staffing Implication

No incremental headcount. Emergency requests handled by existing Store Manager and Treasury Analyst. 500 requests/month × 50 min average = ~417 staff-hours/month distributed across 200 stores and Finance team.

---

## W752. Intercompany Management Fee Allocation & Monthly Billing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Monthly IC billing cycle (1st business day of month) |
| **Frequency** | Monthly |
| **Volume** | 5 legal entities × management fee allocations |
| **Owner** | Finance Manager (Consolidation) |
| **Participants** | FP&A Analyst, Finance Manager, Entity Controllers, Treasury (W30) |

### Background
BuildRight Depot Corp. operates 5 legal entities (Holdings, Depot Inc., Logistics Inc., Digital Commerce Inc., Property Management Inc.). Holdings provides shared corporate services (executive management, finance, HR, IT, legal) to the operating entities. These shared services must be allocated and billed as management fees at arm's length per transfer pricing requirements (W235) and PAS 24 related party disclosure (W486). This workflow manages the monthly allocation, billing, and settlement of management fees.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Cost pool compilation**: FP&A Analyst compiles monthly shared services cost pool: HQ salaries, corporate overhead, shared IT systems, shared services (finance, HR, legal), and corporate insurance | FP&A Analyst | Finance Manager | 4 hours |
| 2 | **Allocation methodology application**: System applies approved allocation methodology: (a) headcount-based for HR/admin costs, (b) revenue-based for executive/corporate costs, (c) transaction-volume-based for IT/finance shared services, (d) square-footage-based for property-related shared costs | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | **Entity allocation calculation**: System generates entity-level management fee allocation schedule with supporting calculations; each entity's allocation shows: cost pool, allocation basis, calculated amount, and month-over-month variance | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | **Review and adjustment**: Finance Manager reviews allocation schedule; adjusts for one-time items (e.g., corporate restructuring, ERP implementation costs); documents adjustments with transfer pricing justification (W235) | Finance Manager | — | 2 hours |
| 5 | **IC invoice generation**: System auto-generates intercompany management fee invoices from Holdings to each operating entity; invoices include allocation methodology, calculation detail, and supporting documentation | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | **IC recording and settlement**: Each entity records IC payable (Holdings records IC receivable); settled via IC net settlement process (W14) along with other IC transactions | Entity Controllers | Finance Manager | 1 hour |
| 7 | **Transfer pricing documentation**: Monthly allocation added to annual transfer pricing documentation file (W235); supports PAS 24 related party disclosure (W486) | System | FP&A Analyst | Automated |
| 8 | **Quarterly benchmarking review**: Quarterly: Finance reviews management fee rates against external benchmarks; adjusts if rates fall outside arm's-length range per transfer pricing guidelines | Finance Manager | CFO | 4 hours/quarter |

### System Touchpoints

- Cost pool compilation module with GL account grouping and classification
- Allocation engine with configurable methodologies (headcount, revenue, volume, square footage)
- IC invoice auto-generation linked to W14 IC transaction processing
- W235 transfer pricing documentation — monthly allocation data feed
- W486 related party disclosure — management fee disclosure data
- W234 IC profit elimination — management fee elimination on consolidation
- W26 budget — management fee budget vs. actual tracking

### Pain Points / Risks

- Transfer pricing risk — if management fee allocation methodology is not defensible, BIR may challenge the deductions claimed by operating entities
- Allocation disputes — operating entity controllers may contest their allocation share, especially if their entity's profitability is under pressure
- Cost pool definition changes — new shared services (e.g., ERP implementation team) need to be added to the cost pool and allocation methodology updated
- Timing mismatch — management fees are billed monthly but actual HQ costs are finalized during month-end close (W9A), creating a 1–2 day lag

### Time Estimate

Monthly: Cost pool compilation (4 hours) + review (2 hours) + IC recording (1 hour) = 7 hours. Quarterly: +4 hours benchmarking. Annual: +8 hours methodology review.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing FP&A and consolidation team. Monthly time investment: ~7 hours by FP&A Analyst and Finance Manager. No incremental headcount.

---

## W764. Store-Level Daily Opening Safe Count & Cash Float Preparation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Start of each store business day |
| **Frequency** | Daily; 200 stores |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × 1 opening safe count + float preparation per day |
| **Owner** | Assistant Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Assistant Store Manager, Cashier (opening shift) |

### Background

Every BuildRight Depot store begins the day with a verified safe count and cashier float preparation. While W5A covers the broader store opening procedure and W541 covers cash office operations at end-of-day, this workflow specifically addresses the morning safe count verification, float preparation for 3 POS terminals, and petty cash verification — the critical first cash control point of each business day. With 200 stores handling ~PHP 5.04B in monthly in-store revenue, opening cash control is essential for shrinkage prevention and cash accuracy.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Safe access and dual-control open**: Assistant Store Manager and opening Cashier open safe together per dual-control policy; both present during entire count | ASM | ASM | 5 min |
| 2 | **Overnight safe count verification**: Count all denominations in safe; compare to previous night's closing safe count from W541; document variance; any variance > PHP 50 triggers immediate investigation per W272 | ASM | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 3 | **POS terminal float preparation**: Prepare 3 cash floats per standard denomination mix: (a) PHP 20,000 per terminal (50 × PHP 100, 50 × PHP 50, 100 × PHP 20, 200 × PHP 10, 200 × PHP 5, coins); total float: PHP 60,000 per store | Cashier | ASM | 10 min |
| 4 | **Petty cash fund verification**: Verify petty cash float of PHP 20,000 per W25; count and record; reconcile against petty cash register | ASM | — | 5 min |
| 5 | **System opening entry**: ASM records safe count and float disbursement in ERP cash management module; system logs opening balance per terminal; system links to W541 cash office close for overnight reconciliation | ASM | — | 5 min |
| 6 | **Float disbursement to cashiers**: Each cashier signs for terminal float at shift start per W517; tamper-evident bag opened in cashier's presence; both ASM and Cashier verify count | ASM + Cashier | — | 5 min/terminal |
| 7 | **Exception handling**: If safe count variance detected: (a) document variance, (b) review CCTV footage from overnight per W71, (c) escalate to Store Manager, (d) if variance > PHP 500, escalate to Regional LP per W248 | ASM | Store Manager | 15-30 min if exception |

### System Touchpoints

- Cash management module — safe balance tracking and float disbursement recording
- W541 cash office operations — opening balance feeds into daily cash reconciliation
- W517 cashier shift handover — float receipt and acknowledgment
- W272 cashier over/short — variance investigation integration
- W25 petty cash management — petty cash fund balance
- W71 store physical security — safe access log and CCTV integration
- W248 inventory variance & LP investigation — cash variance escalation

### Pain Points / Risks

- Dual-control bypass — if ASM opens safe alone (e.g., cashier late), cash control is compromised; must wait for second person per policy
- Float preparation errors — incorrect denomination mix delays checkout when cashiers need change; recurring errors suggest training need per W518
- Overnight variance — unexplained safe count variances may indicate internal theft; must be investigated consistently per W755
- Safe mechanical failure — combination lock or electronic safe malfunction delays store opening; IT and security vendor escalation per W48

### Time Estimate

Daily per store: Safe count (10 min) + float preparation (10 min) + petty cash (5 min) + system entry (5 min) + disbursement (15 min for 3 terminals) = ~45 minutes total. Exception handling adds 15-30 minutes.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing Assistant Store Manager and opening cashier duties. No incremental headcount. Daily time: ~45 minutes of ASM time.

## W765. Multi-Entity Consolidation Monthly Execution & Elimination Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Month-end close cycle (W9A); after all entities complete individual month-end close |
| **Frequency** | Monthly |
| **Volume** | 1 consolidated close per month covering 5 legal entities |
| **Owner** | Finance Manager (Consolidation) |
| **Participants** | Entity Controllers (5), FP&A Analyst, Finance Manager, CFO |

### Background

BuildRight Depot operates through 5 legal entities (Holdings, Depot Inc., Logistics Inc., Digital Commerce Inc., Property Management Inc.) that require consolidated financial reporting per PFRS/IFRS. While W9 covers the overall financial close and W14 handles intercompany transactions, this workflow manages the specific monthly consolidation execution — the elimination of intercompany balances, revenue, and expenses, and the generation of consolidated financial statements for the group. This is a critical compliance requirement for SEC filing (W481) and external audit (W351).

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Entity close completion verification**: Finance Manager verifies all 5 entities have completed month-end close per W9A: all subledgers posted, all JEs approved per W638, bank reconciliations completed per W89, intercompany balances matched per IC-005 | Finance Manager | — | 1 hour |
| 2 | **Intercompany balance reconciliation**: System performs automated IC balance matching across all entity pairs: (a) Depot Inc. ↔ Logistics Inc. (warehousing fees, IC inventory), (b) Depot Inc. ↔ Digital Commerce Inc. (ecommerce fees, payment remittance), (c) Depot Inc. ↔ Property Management Inc. (lease payments per W275), (d) Holdings ↔ all operating entities (management fees per W752); flag unmatched IC entries | System | Entity Controllers | 30 min automated + 2 hours review |
| 3 | **IC discrepancy resolution**: For any unmatched IC balances: Entity Controllers investigate and resolve; common causes: timing differences (goods in transit), FX rounding, posting errors; corrections posted per W638 approval matrix | Entity Controllers | Finance Manager | 1-4 hours |
| 4 | **Consolidation elimination entries**: System auto-generates elimination JEs: (a) IC AP/AR elimination, (b) IC revenue/expense elimination, (c) IC profit in inventory elimination per W234, (d) IC dividend elimination per W137, (e) IC management fee elimination per W752, (f) lease elimination (Depot Inc. ↔ Property Management Inc. per W635 PFRS 16) | System | Finance Manager | Automated + 1 hour review |
| 5 | **Minority interest calculation**: If any entity has non-controlling interest (applicable if external shareholders exist per W327): system calculates NCI share of profit and equity | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Consolidated trial balance generation**: System generates consolidated trial balance with eliminations; Finance Manager reviews for unusual balances, orphan eliminations, or missing entries | Finance Manager | — | 1 hour |
| 7 | **Consolidated financial statements**: System generates consolidated BS, IS, CF, and Equity Changes per PFRS 1; Finance Manager reviews presentation, disclosures, and classification | System + Finance Manager | CFO | 2 hours |
| 8 | **Intercompany profit in inventory**: System calculates unrealized IC profit in inventory remaining at month-end: (a) Logistics Inc. markup on goods held in Depot Inc. stores, (b) Digital Commerce Inc. markup on ecommerce inventory; elimination JE generated | System | Finance Manager | Automated + 30 min review |
| 9 | **Consolidation workpaper documentation**: Finance Manager prepares consolidation workpaper documenting: all elimination entries, IC reconciliation, non-controlling interest calculation, and consolidation adjustments; filed for external audit per W351 | Finance Manager | — | 2 hours |
| 10 | **CFO review and sign-off**: CFO reviews consolidated financial statements and workpaper; approves for management reporting per W35 and external filing per W481 | CFO | — | 1 hour |

### System Touchpoints

- Consolidation module — automated elimination engine with multi-entity GL aggregation
- IC matching engine — automated intercompany balance reconciliation per IC-005
- W14 intercompany transactions — IC transaction feed for elimination
- W234 IC profit elimination — IC profit in inventory calculation
- W635 PFRS 16 lease accounting — IC lease elimination
- W752 IC management fee allocation — management fee elimination
- W137 IC dividend management — dividend elimination
- W638 period-end JE review — elimination JE approval
- W9A month-end close — entity close completion trigger
- W481 SEC reportorial requirements — consolidated FS for filing
- W351 external audit coordination — consolidation workpaper support

### Pain Points / Risks

- Entity close timing dependency — consolidation cannot begin until ALL 5 entities complete close; a single entity delay blocks the entire process; SLA: all entities closed by day 3
- IC mismatch investigation — intercompany balance discrepancies can take hours to resolve, especially for IC inventory in transit where goods have left one entity but not been received by another
- IC profit in inventory complexity — calculating unrealized IC profit on 35,000 SKUs across multiple entity transfers requires precise system configuration
- FX consolidation — if any entity has USD transactions (imports per W2B), FX translation of IC balances adds complexity
- Consolidation adjustments — manual consolidation adjustments require additional CFO review per W638 and create audit risk

### Time Estimate

Monthly: Verification (1 hour) + IC reconciliation (2.5 hours) + discrepancy resolution (1-4 hours) + elimination review (1 hour) + FS review (2 hours) + IC profit review (30 min) + workpaper (2 hours) + CFO review (1 hour) = 11-15 hours. Quarterly: +4 hours for QBR consolidation. Annual: +8 hours for year-end close per W9B.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing Finance Manager (Consolidation) and Entity Controllers. Dedicated consolidation time: 11-15 hours monthly. No incremental headcount.

## W766. Customer Credit Note Aging Management & Unredeemed Credit Write-Off

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Monthly credit note aging review cycle |
| **Frequency** | Monthly review; quarterly write-off batch |
| **Volume** | ~2,000-3,000 active credit notes at any time (~PHP 15-20M outstanding) |
| **Owner** | AR Accountant |
| **Participants** | AR Accountant, Finance Manager, Customer Service Manager |

### Background

When customers receive credit notes for returns (W12), pricing corrections (W540), or dispute resolutions (W41), the credit notes remain as open liabilities until redeemed against future purchases or refunded in cash. Over time, credit notes age unredeemed — customers forget, lose the credit, or the amounts are too small to use. Aging credit notes create accounting complexity (liability on balance sheet), customer dissatisfaction (forgotten credits), and fraud risk (employee exploitation of orphan credits). This workflow manages the lifecycle of credit notes from issuance through redemption or write-off.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Monthly credit note aging report generation**: System generates aging report: (a) 0-30 days (active), (b) 31-90 days (aging), (c) 91-180 days (stale), (d) 181-365 days (dormant), (e) > 365 days (write-off candidate); categorized by customer type (B2C, trade, corporate), amount band, and store | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | **Stale credit note customer notification**: For credit notes in 91-180 day bucket: system generates automated customer notification (SMS, email, app push per W708) reminding customer of outstanding credit with balance and redemption instructions | System | AR Accountant | Automated + 1 hour review |
| 3 | **Dormant credit note escalation**: For credit notes in 181-365 day bucket: (a) AR Accountant reviews for patterns (same store generating excessive credits, same employee processing); (b) suspicious patterns escalated to LP per W37; (c) customer outreach for high-value dormant credits (> PHP 5,000) | AR Accountant | Finance Manager | 2 hours |
| 4 | **Quarterly write-off batch preparation**: For credit notes > 365 days: AR Accountant prepares write-off batch with: (a) total amount by customer type, (b) write-off financial impact (revenue reduction per PFRS 15 vs. liability release), (c) BIR compliance considerations (credit notes referenced to VAT-registered invoices require BIR documentation per W540) | AR Accountant | Finance Manager | 4 hours/quarter |
| 5 | **Write-off approval**: Finance Manager reviews and approves write-off batch: (a) individual credit notes > PHP 10,000 require CFO approval, (b) aggregate batch per quarter requires Finance Manager approval; system posts write-off: DR Customer Credit Liability / CR Revenue (breakage recognition per FIN-039 PFRS 15) | Finance Manager | CFO (> PHP 10K) | 2 hours/quarter |
| 6 | **Redemption analytics**: Monthly: AR Accountant reports: (a) credit note issuance volume by reason, (b) redemption rate by aging bucket, (c) average time to redemption, (d) write-off rate, (e) total credit note liability on balance sheet | AR Accountant | — | 2 hours |
| 7 | **Policy review**: Annual: Finance Manager reviews credit note policy: (a) expiration period (currently 365 days), (b) minimum redemption threshold (currently PHP 0 — no minimum), (c) proactive notification frequency; recommends adjustments based on analytics | Finance Manager | CFO | 2 hours/year |

### System Touchpoints

- Credit note module — issuance, tracking, aging, and redemption per W540 BIR compliance
- AR aging management — integration with W108 customer credit collection
- W708 customer communication management — proactive credit note notification
- W37 LP investigation — suspicious credit note pattern escalation
- W540 BIR invoice/credit note management — BIR-compliant credit note lifecycle
- FIN-039 PFRS 15 revenue recognition — credit note breakage accounting
- W101 customer refund & credit processing — credit note issuance integration

### Pain Points / Risks

- Breakage recognition complexity — PFRS 15 requires systematic approach to credit note breakage recognition; inconsistent write-off timing creates audit risk
- Customer disputes — customers who discover expired credit notes may demand reinstatement, requiring manual override and policy exception
- Employee fraud — orphan credits may be exploited by employees processing fictitious returns to generate credit notes for personal use; LP monitoring per W755 essential
- BIR documentation — credit notes referenced to VAT-registered sales invoices must maintain proper documentation per W540; write-off does not eliminate BIR record retention requirement
- Customer experience — unredeemed credits represent poor customer experience; proactive notification and easy redemption channels critical

### Time Estimate

Monthly: Report review (1 hour) + stale notification (1 hour) + dormant escalation (2 hours) + analytics (2 hours) = 6 hours. Quarterly: +6 hours for write-off batch. Annual: +2 hours policy review.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing AR Accountant role. Monthly time investment: ~6 hours. No incremental headcount.

## W767. Vendor Rebate Claim Filing & Settlement Documentation Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Rebate qualifying period end (monthly/quarterly/annual per vendor agreement) |
| **Frequency** | Monthly for top vendors; quarterly for standard vendors |
| **Volume** | ~20-30 rebate claims per month representing ~PHP 30-50M in rebate accruals |
| **Owner** | Procurement Analyst |
| **Participants** | Category Manager, Procurement Analyst, AP Accountant, Vendor |

### Background

BuildRight Depot's vendor agreements (W62) include rebate provisions — volume-based incentives, promotional support, and market development funds that represent ~PHP 300-500M annually. While W27 covers rebate accrual and settlement at a high level and MER-009 covers vendor trade fund management, this workflow addresses the detailed operational process of filing rebate claims with vendors, compiling supporting documentation, resolving disputes, and processing settlement — the critical execution step that converts accrued rebates into actual cash recovery.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Rebate eligibility calculation**: System calculates qualifying purchases per vendor agreement: (a) volume-based rebates: total purchases vs. tier thresholds, (b) growth rebates: current period vs. same period prior year, (c) promotional rebates: qualifying SKU sell-through per W513, (d) new product launch support per W761 | System | Procurement Analyst | Automated |
| 2 | **Documentation package compilation**: Procurement Analyst compiles claim documentation: (a) purchase history report (PO numbers, dates, quantities, values), (b) goods receipt confirmations, (c) qualifying sell-through data for promotional rebates, (d) price list verification for price protection rebates per W161 | Procurement Analyst | Category Manager | 2-4 hours/claim |
| 3 | **Claim submission**: Procurement Analyst submits claim through vendor portal (W705) or direct communication: (a) claim form per vendor requirements, (b) supporting documentation, (c) claim amount with calculation detail, (d) settlement preference (credit note against AP, wire transfer, or deduction from next payment) | Procurement Analyst | — | 1 hour/claim |
| 4 | **Vendor dispute resolution**: If vendor disputes claim: (a) Category Manager engages vendor for resolution meeting, (b) review disputed line items against contract terms per W669, (c) agree on revised claim amount or provide additional documentation, (d) document resolution for audit trail per W351 | Category Manager | Procurement Manager | 2-8 hours |
| 5 | **Settlement processing**: Upon vendor approval: (a) AP Accountant records settlement per agreed method: credit note reduces AP balance, wire transfer recorded per W89, or payment deduction applied per W556; (b) system reconciles against original accrual per W27; (c) variance between accrual and settlement posted to P&L | AP Accountant | Finance Manager | 1 hour/claim |
| 6 | **Rebate tracking dashboard update**: System updates rebate tracking: (a) total accruals vs. settlements by vendor, (b) outstanding claims aging, (c) disputed claims, (d) settlement rate (target: 95% of accruals settled within 60 days) | System | Procurement Analyst | Automated |
| 7 | **Quarterly rebate analytics**: Procurement Analyst reports: (a) total rebate income by category and vendor, (b) settlement rate and average time-to-settlement, (c) disputed claim analysis, (d) rebate income as % of COGS, (e) accrual accuracy (accrued vs. settled variance) | Procurement Analyst | Category Manager | 4 hours/quarter |

### System Touchpoints

- Vendor rebate module — accrual calculation, claim tracking, settlement processing
- W27 vendor rebate accrual & settlement — rebate agreement and accrual management
- W705 vendor self-service portal — claim submission and tracking
- W7 AP processing — settlement credit note application
- W556 AP payment run — settlement payment processing
- W669 vendor contract compliance — contract term verification
- W161 vendor price protection — price protection rebate claim integration
- W513 vendor-funded promotional activity — promotional rebate sell-through data
- W680 supply chain cost analysis — rebate income impact on cost metrics

### Pain Points / Risks

- Documentation burden — vendors require extensive supporting documentation; incomplete packages delay settlement; procurement must maintain meticulous records
- Vendor dispute frequency — vendors frequently dispute claim amounts citing contract interpretation differences; disputes can take months to resolve
- Accrual vs. settlement variance — significant variance between accrued and settled amounts distorts gross margin reporting; must be analyzed and explained per FIN-026
- Settlement timing — vendors may delay settlement beyond contract terms; aging tracking and escalation essential
- Contract complexity — rebate structures vary significantly by vendor (tiered, flat rate, growth-based, promotional); each requires different calculation logic and documentation

### Time Estimate

Monthly: Eligibility calculation (automated) + documentation (2-4 hours/claim × 20-30 claims = 40-120 hours distributed across Category Managers) + submission (1 hour/claim) + settlement (1 hour/claim) = significant but distributed. Quarterly: +4 hours analytics. Focused Procurement Analyst time: ~15-20 hours/month.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing Procurement Analyst and Category Manager roles. Procurement Analyst: ~15-20 hours/month on rebate processing. No incremental headcount.

## W768. BIR VAT Refund Claim Processing & Input VAT Recovery

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Quarterly VAT review identifies excess input VAT over output VAT; or annual VAT reconciliation per W590 |
| **Frequency** | Quarterly review; annual or ad-hoc claim filing |
| **Volume** | 1-2 VAT refund claims per year per entity (primarily entities with significant imports but low domestic revenue — e.g., Logistics Inc. during early years) |
| **Owner** | Tax Accountant |
| **Participants** | Tax Accountant, Finance Manager, CFO, External Tax Counsel, BIR |

### Background

Under Philippine tax law, when a taxpayer's input VAT (VAT on purchases) exceeds output VAT (VAT on sales) in a given quarter, the excess may be carried forward or claimed as a tax credit/refund per BIR Revenue Regulations. For BuildRight's import-heavy operations (40% of COGS from imports per W2B, with 12% VAT on customs value including duty and freight), certain entities — particularly BuildRight Logistics Inc. during years when warehouse fee revenue is lower than import-related input VAT — may generate excess input VAT. This workflow manages the BIR VAT refund claim process, which is known for lengthy processing times (6-24 months) and strict documentation requirements.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Excess input VAT identification**: Tax Accountant reviews quarterly VAT reconciliation per W590: (a) input VAT from purchases (domestic and import per FIN-006), (b) output VAT from sales, (c) net VAT position; if excess input VAT > PHP 1M, evaluates claim feasibility | Tax Accountant | Finance Manager | 4 hours/quarter |
| 2 | **Claim feasibility assessment**: Tax Accountant assesses: (a) expected BIR processing time (historical 6-24 months), (b) probability of approval (BIR audit history, documentation strength), (c) alternative: carry forward to offset future output VAT vs. claim cash refund, (d) cost of external tax counsel for claim preparation (~PHP 200-500K per claim) | Tax Accountant | CFO | 2 hours |
| 3 | **Documentation package preparation**: If proceeding with claim: Tax Accountant compiles BIR-required documentation: (a) BIR Form 2550M/Q showing excess input VAT, (b) schedule of input VAT claimed with supporting invoices/customs declarations, (c) proof of payment (bank receipts, customs payment receipts), (d) certificate of registration per REG-001, (e) sworn statement declaring no pending tax liabilities | Tax Accountant | External Tax Counsel | 20-40 hours |
| 4 | **External tax counsel review**: External tax counsel reviews documentation package for completeness and legal sufficiency; advises on claim strategy; prepares cover letter and legal memorandum supporting the refund claim | External Tax Counsel | CFO | 8-16 hours |
| 5 | **BIR claim submission**: Tax Accountant files claim with BIR Revenue District Office (RDO) where entity is registered per REG-001: (a) submits complete documentation package, (b) obtains BIR acknowledgment receipt with tracking number, (c) enters claim in BIR tracking log | Tax Accountant | — | 4 hours |
| 6 | **BIR follow-up and audit coordination**: During BIR processing: (a) monthly follow-up with BIR RDO on claim status, (b) prepare for BIR audit of claimed input VAT — provide original invoices, customs declarations, and payment proofs on demand, (c) coordinate with External Tax Counsel for BIR queries | Tax Accountant | Finance Manager | 2-4 hours/month during processing |
| 7 | **Claim resolution**: Upon BIR decision: (a) if approved: Tax Accountant records refund (DR Cash / CR Input VAT) or tax credit memo; (b) if partially approved: analyze disallowed amounts, adjust, and record net refund; (c) if denied: External Tax Counsel evaluates appeal via Court of Tax Appeals within 30 days | Tax Accountant | CFO | 4 hours |
| 8 | **Quarterly VAT refund tracking**: Tax Accountant maintains VAT refund tracking dashboard: (a) outstanding claims by entity and amount, (b) days since filing, (c) BIR audit status, (d) historical approval rate, (e) total input VAT carried forward vs. claimed | Tax Accountant | Finance Manager | 1 hour/quarter |

### System Touchpoints

- Tax module — VAT input/output tracking per FIN-006, quarterly reconciliation per W590
- W260 BIR eFPS filing — VAT return filing and input VAT reporting
- W473 BIR EIS e-invoicing — sales invoice VAT documentation
- REG-001 BIR branch registration — entity RDO tracking
- W351 external audit coordination — VAT refund claim audit support
- AP module — input VAT from vendor invoices per W7
- Import module — customs VAT per W144/W239
- Treasury module — refund receipt and bank reconciliation per W89

### Pain Points / Risks

- BIR processing time — Philippine BIR VAT refund claims historically take 6-24 months; cash is tied up during processing, impacting working capital per W30
- Documentation stringency — BIR requires original documents; any missing invoice or customs declaration can result in partial or full denial
- BIR audit risk — filing a VAT refund claim increases probability of BIR tax audit per W77; entity must ensure all tax positions are defensible
- Carry-forward vs. refund decision — carrying forward excess input VAT is administratively simpler but delays cash recovery; refund claim provides cash but requires significant documentation effort
- Entity-level complexity — each of 5 entities has separate VAT registration and may have different refund positions; cross-entity analysis needed to optimize group-level VAT strategy

### Time Estimate

Quarterly review: 4 hours. Per claim: Feasibility (2 hours) + documentation (20-40 hours) + counsel review (8-16 hours) + filing (4 hours) + monthly follow-up (2-4 hours/month × 6-18 months) = significant. Annual tracking: 4 hours.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing Tax Accountant role. Per-claim time: 30-60 hours plus ongoing follow-up. External tax counsel engaged on retainer. No incremental headcount.

## W769. Customer Overpayment Detection & Refund Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Automated detection during payment reconciliation; or customer inquiry |
| **Frequency** | Weekly review; daily automated detection |
| **Volume** | ~100-200 overpayments per month (~PHP 1-3M) |
| **Owner** | AR Accountant |
| **Participants** | AR Accountant, Finance Manager, Customer (B2B trade/corporate) |

### Background

With ~3,500 AR invoices per month (W8) and ~5,200 trade + ~200 corporate accounts, payment reconciliation occasionally identifies overpayments — where a customer has paid more than the invoice amount. Overpayments arise from: (a) duplicate payments, (b) payment applied to wrong invoice, (c) rounding differences, (d) credit memo not deducted before payment, or (e) advance payment exceeding actual billing. Unresolved overpayments create customer dissatisfaction, distort AR aging, and represent a liability on the balance sheet. This workflow systematically detects, communicates, and resolves overpayments.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Automated overpayment detection**: During daily payment reconciliation per W99 and cash application: system flags payments where: (a) payment amount exceeds invoice balance by > PHP 100, (b) payment applied to already-paid invoice, (c) payment with no matching invoice (on-account payment) | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | **Overpayment classification**: AR Accountant reviews each flagged overpayment and classifies: (a) Duplicate payment — same invoice paid twice, (b) Misapplication — payment intended for different invoice, (c) Credit memo not applied — customer paid gross without deducting issued credit note per W766, (d) Genuine overpayment — customer paid more than owed | AR Accountant | — | 5-10 min/case |
| 3 | **Misapplication correction**: For misapplied payments: AR Accountant re-applies payment to correct invoice; no customer communication needed; system updates AR aging per W108 | AR Accountant | — | 5 min/case |
| 4 | **Customer notification**: For genuine overpayments and unapplied credits > PHP 500: AR Accountant initiates customer notification per W708: (a) details of overpayment (invoice number, payment date, excess amount), (b) refund options: (i) credit note for future purchases per W101, (ii) wire transfer refund to original payment account, (iii) offset against other open invoices | AR Accountant | — | 10 min/case |
| 5 | **Refund processing**: Based on customer preference: (a) Credit note: system generates credit note per W540 with BIR compliance, (b) Wire refund: AR Accountant prepares refund request per W101 approval matrix, Finance Manager approves, payment processed per W556, (c) Offset: system applies overpayment to customer's oldest open invoice | AR Accountant | Finance Manager | 10-15 min/case |
| 6 | **Aging and escalation**: Monthly: unresolved overpayments > 60 days escalated: (a) second customer notification, (b) B2B accounts: Sales Manager outreach per W617 customer success, (c) overpayments > 180 days assessed for breakage recognition per PFRS 15 | AR Accountant | Finance Manager | 2 hours/month |
| 7 | **Overpayment analytics**: Monthly: AR Accountant reports: (a) overpayment frequency by cause, (b) average resolution time, (c) total outstanding overpayment balance, (d) recurring customer patterns (indicating process improvement opportunity) | AR Accountant | — | 1 hour/month |

### System Touchpoints

- Cash application module — automated payment matching and overpayment detection
- W99 payment settlement reconciliation — payment reconciliation trigger
- W108 customer credit collection — AR aging integration
- W101 customer refund & credit processing — refund workflow
- W540 BIR credit note issuance — BIR-compliant credit note generation
- W556 AP payment run — refund payment processing
- W708 customer communication management — customer notification
- W617 B2B customer success — account outreach for unresolved overpayments

### Pain Points / Risks

- Manual cash application errors — incorrect payment application creates false overpayments and false underpayments simultaneously; root cause is often manual data entry
- Customer credit risk — if overpayment is refunded via credit note but customer never redeems, it becomes aged credit note per W766
- Duplicate payment detection — system may not catch all duplicates if invoice reference format differs between customer payment advice and ERP invoice number
- Customer relationship impact — unresolved overpayments frustrate B2B customers who expect prompt handling; impacts W617 customer success metrics

### Time Estimate

Weekly: detection review (2 hours) + processing (5-10 min/case × ~50/week = 4-8 hours) + notification (10 min/case = 8 hours) = ~14-18 hours/week. Monthly analytics: 1 hour.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing AR Accountant role. Weekly time: ~14-18 hours across AR team. No incremental headcount.

---

## W770. AP Vendor Debit Memo Processing & Account Deduction Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Vendor quality issue, pricing discrepancy, or contract compliance violation identified |
| **Frequency** | Weekly; ~30-50 debit memos per month |
| **Volume** | ~30-50 debit memos per month (~PHP 5-15M in deductions) |
| **Owner** | AP Accountant |
| **Participants** | AP Accountant, Procurement Analyst, Finance Manager, Vendor |

### Background

When BuildRight Depot identifies vendor-caused issues — quality defects requiring CAPA per W110, pricing overcharges exceeding contract rates per W669, delivery short-ships, or damage claims per W500 — the company may issue a debit memo to reduce the vendor's AP balance instead of waiting for the vendor to issue a credit note. This is faster for cash flow and ensures deductions are not forgotten. However, debit memos create reconciliation complexity and can strain vendor relationships if not managed properly. This workflow manages the debit memo lifecycle from issuance through vendor agreement and settlement.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Debit memo trigger identification**: AP Accountant or Procurement Analyst identifies debit memo trigger: (a) quality hold — vendor defect per W110 requiring chargeback, (b) pricing discrepancy — vendor invoice exceeds contract price per W669, (c) short-shipment — goods received quantity < invoiced quantity per W7, (d) transit damage — carrier damage claim with vendor responsibility per W500, (e) promotional rebate shortfall — vendor did not fund agreed promotional support per W513 | AP Accountant / Procurement | — | 5-10 min/case |
| 2 | **Documentation and evidence compilation**: AP Accountant compiles supporting documentation: (a) original invoice and PO reference, (b) goods receipt discrepancy report, (c) quality inspection report per W681, (d) photo evidence of damage, (e) contract pricing schedule per W669, (f) communication log with vendor | AP Accountant | — | 15-30 min/case |
| 3 | **Debit memo creation**: AP Accountant creates debit memo in AP module: (a) references original invoice, (b) specifies deduction amount with calculation detail, (c) links supporting documentation, (d) selects deduction type (quality, pricing, shortage, damage, promotional), (e) applies against vendor AP balance | AP Accountant | Finance Manager | 10 min/case |
| 4 | **Vendor notification**: System notifies vendor of debit memo via vendor portal (W705): (a) debit memo details and supporting documentation, (b) vendor response deadline (14 business days), (c) dispute mechanism if vendor disagrees | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | **Vendor response management**: (a) If vendor accepts: debit memo finalized; AP balance reduced; deduction reflected in next payment run per W556. (b) If vendor disputes: Procurement Analyst engages vendor per W244 invoice dispute resolution; negotiate resolution; adjust debit memo if warranted; document agreed resolution. (c) If no response within 14 days: debit memo auto-finalized per terms | Procurement Analyst / AP Accountant | Finance Manager | 1-4 hours/dispute |
| 6 | **Settlement and reconciliation**: Debit memo settled through: (a) deduction from next payment to vendor per W556, (b) offset against vendor credit memo if vendor issues one, (c) separate wire transfer if vendor disputes and partial resolution reached | AP Accountant | — | 5 min/case |
| 7 | **Monthly debit memo analytics**: AP Accountant reports: (a) debit memo volume by type, vendor, and category, (b) vendor acceptance vs. dispute rate, (c) average time to resolution, (d) total deductions as % of total AP, (e) top 10 vendors by debit memo frequency (indicating systemic issues requiring W110 CAPA) | AP Accountant | Finance Manager | 2 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints

- AP module — debit memo creation, application, and settlement
- W7 AP invoice processing — invoice reference and 3-way match data
- W244 vendor invoice dispute — dispute management workflow
- W669 vendor contract compliance — contract pricing verification
- W110 supplier quality CAPA — quality issue chargeback trigger
- W500 transfer order damage claim — damage documentation integration
- W705 vendor self-service portal — vendor notification and response
- W556 AP payment run — debit memo deduction from payment
- W100 vendor statement reconciliation — debit memo reconciliation on vendor statement

### Pain Points / Risks

- Vendor relationship strain — excessive or poorly documented debit memos damage vendor relationships; vendors may refuse to supply or reduce service levels
- Documentation quality — insufficient documentation weakens BuildRight's position in vendor disputes and may result in debit memo reversal
- Unresolved debit memos — vendor disputes that remain unresolved create aging items on vendor reconciliation per W100, complicating month-end close per W9A
- Double-deduction risk — if AP Accountant issues debit memo AND vendor issues credit note for same issue, double-deduction occurs; system must enforce one-to-one matching
- Contract interpretation — pricing disputes often arise from ambiguous contract terms; clear contract language per W688 CLM system is preventive

### Time Estimate

Weekly: identification and creation (5-10 min/case × 10-12/week = 1-2 hours) + documentation (15-30 min/case = 2.5-6 hours) + vendor management (varies) = ~6-10 hours/week. Monthly analytics: 2 hours.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing AP Accountant and Procurement Analyst roles. Weekly time: ~6-10 hours. No incremental headcount.
