## W812. Customer Credit Field Collection Operations & Legal Escalation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer AR aging exceeds 90 days; phone/email collection unsuccessful per W108 |
| **Frequency** | Weekly; ~150-250 field visits per month across chain |
| **Volume** | ~5,200 trade accounts + ~200 corporate accounts; ~3% reach field collection stage |
| **Owner** | Collection Officer |
| **Participants** | Collection Officer, Sales Manager, Legal Counsel, Finance Manager, Customer |

### Background

While W108 covers phone and email-based collection for BuildRight Depot's ~5,400 B2B accounts, approximately 3% of trade and corporate accounts progress to requiring physical field visits. These are accounts where the customer has been unreachable by phone and email, has repeatedly broken payment commitments, or has communicated a dispute that cannot be resolved remotely. Philippine collection law imposes specific requirements before legal action can be pursued: Republic Act 8484 (Access Devices Regulation Act) governs credit card-related collections, while the Civil Code of the Philippines (Articles 1156-1304) governs obligations and contracts, requiring documented demand before filing a collection case. Field collection serves as the critical bridge between W108 phone/email escalation and W81 bad debt write-off, providing the documented demand trail necessary for legal proceedings. Given BuildRight's 200-store footprint across Luzon, Visayas, and Mindanao, field collection operations require route optimization, safety protocols, and coordination with the Legal department for accounts that ultimately require court filing.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **90+ day aging review and account selection**: Collection Officer reviews W108 aging report to identify accounts exceeding 90 days overdue where phone/email collection attempts have been exhausted (minimum 5 documented contact attempts per W108). Accounts ranked by: (a) outstanding balance (prioritize > PHP 100K), (b) collectibility score based on customer payment history, business viability, and asset visibility, (c) geographic proximity to other field visits for route efficiency per W196 | Collection Officer | — | 2-3 hours/week |
| 2 | **Pre-visit preparation**: For each selected account, Collection Officer prepares: (a) complete account ledger with invoice detail, payment history, and previous collection notes from W108, (b) signed demand letter on BuildRight Depot letterhead citing specific invoices and amounts due, reviewed by Legal Counsel, (c) statement of account printout with running balance, (d) customer location map and contact information, (e) visit safety assessment (area risk, customer demeanor history) | Collection Officer | Legal Counsel | 15-20 min/account |
| 3 | **Field visit scheduling and route optimization**: Collection Officer schedules field visits using route optimization per W196 logistics planning: (a) group accounts by geographic cluster (NCR, Central Luzon, Calabarzon, Visayas, Mindanao), (b) optimize visit sequence to maximize accounts per trip, (c) coordinate with Sales Manager for introductions to key B2B trade accounts where relationship exists, (d) file field visit itinerary with Finance Manager for safety tracking | Collection Officer | Finance Manager | 1-2 hours/week |
| 4 | **Conduct field visit with demand letter**: Collection Officer visits customer premises: (a) present BuildRight Depot credentials and purpose of visit, (b) hand-deliver signed demand letter requesting payment within 10 business days, (c) obtain customer acknowledgment signature on demand letter copy (or document refusal to sign with witness notation), (d) discuss account status, understand customer's financial situation and payment capacity, (e) document visit outcome: customer present/absent, responsiveness, stated payment intent, key observations about business operations | Collection Officer | — | 2-4 hours/visit including travel |
| 5 | **Negotiate payment plan or partial payment**: If customer is willing to engage: (a) negotiate payment terms based on customer capacity — options include: (i) immediate full payment, (ii) partial immediate payment with post-dated checks (PDCs) for balance per Philippine banking practice, (iii) structured payment plan over 30-90 days with specific payment dates, (iv) offset arrangement where customer returns unused inventory for credit per W101, (b) Collection Officer authority: up to PHP 50K payment plan approval; above PHP 50K requires Finance Manager approval, (c) document agreed terms in payment commitment letter signed by both parties | Collection Officer | Finance Manager (> PHP 50K) | 30-60 min/negotiation |
| 6 | **Payment plan formalization and deposit management**: If payment plan agreed: (a) system creates payment schedule with automatic reminders per W94 deposit management, (b) if PDCs collected: secure in collection officer's custody, log in PDC register, present to bank on scheduled dates, (c) if partial cash payment received on-site: issue provisional receipt, deposit to nearest BuildRight store's collection account within 24 hours per W340 store cash audit procedures, (d) update customer account with payment commitment in W8 AR module | Collection Officer | — | 15-20 min/account |
| 7 | **Formal demand letter issuance (uncooperative customers)**: If customer is uncooperative, refuses to engage, or business appears closed/abandoned: (a) Collection Officer submits field visit report with documented demand delivery, (b) Legal Counsel reviews account for formal demand letter — notarized demand letter sent via registered mail with return card per Philippine legal requirement, (c) demand letter specifies 15-day payment deadline, warns of legal action including potential filing of criminal charges for estafa under Revised Penal Code Article 315 if elements exist, (d) copy of demand letter and return receipt filed in customer account per W8 | Collection Officer | Legal Counsel | 30-45 min/account |
| 8 | **Legal escalation for non-responsive accounts**: If no response to formal demand within 15 days: (a) Legal Counsel assesses case for filing: (i) small claims case under Revised Rules on Small Claims (2019 amendment) for amounts under PHP 400K — no attorney required, faster resolution (6-8 months), (ii) regular civil case for collection of sum of money for amounts PHP 400K and above — requires external counsel, full court proceedings, (iii) criminal complaint for estafa if fraud elements present (issued bouncing check under Bouncing Checks Law BP 22), (b) Legal Counsel prepares complaint, gathers evidence: statement of account, demand letters, delivery receipts, purchase orders, communication records, (c) Finance Manager approves legal filing budget: filing fees, attorney fees, docket fees | Legal Counsel | Finance Manager | 4-8 hours/case prep |
| 9 | **Court filing and case management**: Legal Counsel or external counsel files case: (a) file complaint with appropriate court — Municipal Trial Court (small claims), Regional Trial Court (regular collection), (b) serve summons to defendant customer, (c) monitor case progression: hearings, mediation attempts per court-annexed mediation, pre-trial conferences, (d) Philippine court timeline: small claims 6-8 months to decision, regular collection 2-5 years depending on court docket, (e) update customer account with legal case reference and status | Legal Counsel | — | Ongoing; 2-4 hours/month per active case |
| 10 | **Judgment enforcement and garnishment**: Upon favorable court judgment: (a) Legal Counsel initiates execution proceedings: (i) writ of execution served to customer, (ii) sheriff levies on customer assets (bank accounts, receivables, personal property) per W280 garnishment procedures, (iii) garnishment of customer's bank accounts — served on depository bank, bank remits funds to BuildRight via court, (b) if customer is employed: garnishment of salary through employer, (c) track recovery amount vs. legal costs; Finance Manager assesses cost-effectiveness of continued enforcement for partial recoveries | Legal Counsel | Finance Manager | 4-8 hours/enforcement action |
| 11 | **Monthly field collection analytics and recovery tracking**: Collection Officer and Finance Manager review monthly: (a) field visit completion rate (scheduled vs. completed visits), (b) recovery rate: total collected via field visits vs. total assigned for field collection, (c) payment plan compliance rate — % of payment plans being honored, (d) average days from field visit to first payment, (e) legal case status tracker: active cases, judgments obtained, enforcement progress, (f) cost-per-peso-recovered analysis: travel costs + collector time + legal fees vs. total recovery, (g) bad debt provision impact per W572 credit monitoring and W81 write-off criteria | Collection Officer | Finance Manager | 3-4 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints

- W108 customer credit collection — phone/email collection and aging report source
- W81 bad debt write-off — terminal workflow for unrecoverable accounts
- W572 credit monitoring dashboard — customer risk score and credit exposure
- W94 customer deposit management — payment plan and PDC tracking
- W280 wage/bank garnishment processing — judgment enforcement
- W196 delivery route planning — field visit route optimization
- W8 customer accounts receivable — customer account ledger and payment history
- W663 credit portfolio review — portfolio-level credit risk assessment
- W340 store cash audit — on-site cash deposit procedures
- W101 customer refund & credit processing — inventory return credit offset
- W708 customer communication management — collection correspondence logging

### Pain Points / Risks

- Collector safety — field visits to delinquent customers carry physical safety risk; some customers may become hostile; Collection Officers must be trained in de-escalation and carry company ID; high-risk areas (certain Mindanao provinces) may require two-person visits
- Travel costs — fuel, toll, parking, and meal allowances for field visits across 200-store geography represent significant operational cost; cost-per-recovery must be monitored to ensure field collection is economically justified
- Customer confrontation risk — face-to-face collection can damage customer relationships permanently; must balance firmness with professionalism; risk of customer filing harassment complaint under Philippine Consumer Act
- Legal costs and timeline — Philippine court system processes collection cases in 2-5 years for regular cases; external attorney fees, filing fees, and sheriff's fees can consume 15-30% of recovered amount; small claims faster but limited to PHP 400K
- Partial recovery rates — even with successful judgment, customer may have insufficient assets for full recovery; BuildRight recovers on average 40-60% of filed amounts after legal costs
- PDC management — post-dated checks collected during negotiation require secure physical custody, timely bank presentation, and follow-up on bounced checks per BP 22
- Documentation gaps — field visit reports that lack detail weaken legal case; demand letters that do not meet Civil Code requirements can be challenged; Legal Counsel must review all demand letters before issuance
- Multi-entity complexity — customer accounts may span multiple BuildRight legal entities (Depot Inc., Logistics Inc.); consolidated demand requires coordination across entity AR subledgers

### Time Estimate

Per field visit: preparation (15-20 min) + travel and visit (2-4 hours) + documentation (15-20 min) = ~3-5 hours/visit. Monthly: ~200 visits × 3-5 hours = 600-1,000 hours across collection team. Legal case management: 2-4 hours/month per active case × ~30 active cases = 60-120 hours/month. Monthly analytics: 3-4 hours. Total: ~660-1,125 hours/month across Collection Officers and Legal Counsel.

### Staffing Implication

3 dedicated Collection Officers for chain-wide field visit coverage (1 each for Luzon-NCR, Visayas, Mindanao). Legal Counsel absorbs case management within existing role; external counsel engaged on retainer for court filings. Collection Officers also support phone-based collection per W108 during non-visit weeks. Incremental cost: 3 Collection Officer positions (annual salary ~PHP 360K-480K each + travel budget ~PHP 50K/month each).

## W813. AP Vendor Invoice Duplicate Detection & Resolution

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | System auto-detects potential duplicate vendor invoice during AP invoice entry per W7 |
| **Frequency** | Daily; ~15-25 potential duplicates flagged per month |
| **Volume** | ~15-25 potential duplicates/month out of ~9,500 total invoices; ~60% are true duplicates |
| **Owner** | AP Accountant |
| **Participants** | AP Accountant, Finance Manager, Vendor |

### Background

FIN-020 requires BuildRight Depot to implement automatic duplicate vendor invoice detection as a core financial control. With ~9,500 vendor invoices processed monthly across 5 legal entities and ~2,800 active vendors, duplicate invoice submissions are a recurring operational reality. Duplicates arise from multiple root causes: (a) vendor billing system errors where the same invoice is transmitted twice, (b) re-submission of previously paid invoices by vendor accounts receivable clerks following up on payment status, (c) PO line-level duplicates where the same PO lines appear on multiple vendor invoices (partial shipments billed incrementally vs. consolidated billing), and (d) inter-entity duplicates where the same vendor inadvertently invoices multiple BuildRight entities for the same goods or services. Manual duplicate detection is unreliable at this volume — a single AP Accountant processing 50-80 invoices daily cannot consistently identify duplicates across the full invoice population. Undetected duplicates result in overpayment, require costly recovery efforts per W244 dispute resolution, and represent a material internal control weakness. This workflow manages the systematic detection, review, resolution, and analytics of vendor invoice duplicates.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Automated duplicate detection during invoice entry**: During AP invoice entry per W7, system automatically checks new invoice against existing invoices in the AP module using multi-criteria matching: (a) exact match: same vendor ID + same invoice number + same invoice amount — automatic hard block, (b) high-probability match: same vendor ID + same invoice amount + invoice date within 7 days — flagged for review, (c) PO reference match: same PO number + same PO line number already matched to another invoice — flagged for review, (d) fuzzy match: same vendor ID + similar amount (within 5%) + same invoice date — flagged for review. System prevents posting of hard-blocked invoices and queues flagged invoices for AP Accountant review | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | **AP Accountant review of flagged potential duplicate**: AP Accountant reviews each flagged invoice pair or group: (a) pull original invoice: verify vendor name, invoice number, invoice date, line items, PO reference, and goods receipt status per W7 three-way match, (b) check payment status: is original invoice already paid per W556 payment run? If yes, risk of duplicate payment is high, (c) verify goods receipt: were goods received twice per W2 goods receipting? If two separate GRs exist for same PO, invoices may be legitimate partial shipments, (d) compare line-level detail: are identical SKUs, quantities, and unit prices repeated? Or are line items different despite similar totals? | AP Accountant | — | 10-15 min/review |
| 3 | **Confirmed duplicate — reject second invoice**: If AP Accountant confirms the second invoice is a true duplicate: (a) reject invoice with duplicate reason code in AP module: system links rejected invoice to original paid/posted invoice, (b) add notation: "Duplicate of invoice [number] dated [date], paid on [date] via payment run [reference]", (c) system updates vendor duplicate counter for analytics, (d) no vendor communication required for unpaid duplicate rejection | AP Accountant | — | 5 min/rejection |
| 4 | **Not a duplicate — approve with override**: If AP Accountant determines the flagged pair is not a true duplicate: (a) document reason for override: (i) different PO references — vendor issued separate invoices for separate POs that happen to have similar amounts, (ii) partial shipment — legitimate second invoice for remaining PO quantity, (iii) credit note re-invoice — vendor cancelled original invoice per mutual agreement and re-issued with corrected amounts, (iv) coincidental amount match — different goods/services that happen to total similar amount, (b) approve invoice with override notation in system, (c) override creates audit trail entry flagged for Finance Manager periodic review | AP Accountant | — | 5 min/override |
| 5 | **Duplicate already paid — initiate recovery**: If duplicate invoice was already paid before detection (e.g., both invoices processed in same batch, or detection triggered retroactively): (a) AP Accountant confirms duplicate payment: two payments to same vendor for same invoice reference, (b) initiate recovery per W244 vendor invoice dispute: contact vendor accounts receivable with evidence of duplicate payment, (c) request either: (i) vendor refund via wire transfer, (ii) vendor credit note applied against next payment per W556, (iii) offset against other open vendor invoices, (d) track recovery status in AP module until resolved | AP Accountant | Finance Manager | 30-60 min/recovery case |
| 6 | **Vendor notification of duplicate rejection**: For rejected duplicates where vendor may re-submit: (a) system generates automated vendor notification via W705 vendor self-service portal: "Invoice [number] rejected — duplicate of invoice [original number] processed on [date]. Please do not resubmit.", (b) for vendors not using portal: AP Accountant sends email notification with duplicate reference, (c) for repeat offenders (>3 duplicates in 6 months): Procurement Analyst addresses with vendor per W706 supplier performance scorecard | System / AP Accountant | — | Automated + 5 min/email |
| 7 | **Monthly duplicate analytics**: AP Accountant prepares monthly duplicate detection analytics: (a) total potential duplicates flagged vs. confirmed duplicates — detection precision rate, (b) duplicate rate by vendor: top 10 vendors by duplicate frequency, (c) root cause analysis: vendor billing error, re-submission of paid invoice, PO-level duplicate, inter-entity duplicate, (d) recovery status: total duplicate overpayments identified, amount recovered, amount outstanding, (e) override analysis: number of overrides and reasons — are any patterns emerging that suggest detection rules need tuning? | AP Accountant | Finance Manager | 1 hour/month |
| 8 | **Quarterly vendor duplicate rate review**: Finance Manager and AP Accountant conduct quarterly review with top offenders: (a) meet with Procurement to discuss vendors with persistent duplicate billing (>5 duplicates per quarter), (b) Procurement engages vendor accounts receivable contact to investigate root cause on vendor side — often indicates vendor ERP or billing system issue, (c) for systemic vendor issues: request vendor implement duplicate invoice controls on their side, (d) update vendor performance scorecard per W706 with duplicate billing metric, (e) adjust system detection rules if false positive rate exceeds 40% — tune matching thresholds | AP Accountant | Finance Manager | 2 hours/quarter |

### System Touchpoints

- W7 AP invoice processing — invoice entry and three-way match where duplicates are detected
- FIN-020 duplicate detection requirement — control framework mandating automated detection
- W244 vendor invoice dispute — recovery workflow for already-paid duplicates
- W100 vendor statement reconciliation — duplicate reconciliation items on vendor statements
- W705 vendor self-service portal — automated vendor notification of rejected duplicates
- W556 AP payment run — payment status verification and duplicate payment recovery offset
- W706 supplier performance scorecard — vendor duplicate billing performance metric
- W2 goods receipting — GR verification for legitimate partial shipments vs. duplicates
- W9A financial close — duplicate detection as period-end financial control

### Pain Points / Risks

- False positives blocking legitimate invoices — system detection rules may flag legitimate invoices as duplicates, delaying vendor payment and potentially triggering late payment penalties or supply disruption; override process must be efficient to avoid AP bottleneck
- Vendor workarounds — sophisticated vendors may slightly modify invoice numbers (append letter, change date) or split amounts across invoices to bypass duplicate detection; system must include fuzzy matching and vendor behavioral analysis
- Multi-entity duplicates — same vendor may invoice BuildRight Depot Inc. and BuildRight Logistics Inc. for the same shared service (e.g., facility maintenance, security); cross-entity duplicate detection requires consolidated vendor invoice register spanning all 5 legal entities
- Partial duplicates — 2 of 10 invoice lines may be duplicated while 8 are new; line-level duplicate detection requires granular matching beyond header-level checks and adds processing complexity
- Duplicate already paid — if both invoices pass through before detection triggers, the company has made a duplicate payment; recovery depends on vendor cooperation and can take 30-90 days per W244
- Detection rule tuning — overly aggressive matching creates excessive false positives and AP staff fatigue; overly lenient matching misses duplicates; requires periodic calibration based on analytics

### Time Estimate

Per review: 10-15 min × 20 potential duplicates/month = 3-5 hours/month. Recovery cases: 30-60 min × 4-6/year = 2-6 hours/year. Monthly analytics: 1 hour. Quarterly review: 2 hours/quarter. Total: ~4-7 hours/month + 2 hours/quarter.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing AP Accountant role. Monthly time commitment: ~4-7 hours. No incremental headcount. Finance Manager provides quarterly review oversight (2 hours/quarter) within existing capacity.

---

## W814. Credit Card Settlement Exception & Chargeback Recovery Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Credit card acquirer issues chargeback notice or settlement discrepancy identified per W537/W99 |
| **Frequency** | Weekly; ~80-120 chargebacks/month across 600 terminals |
| **Volume** | ~80-120 chargebacks/month (~0.003% of 2.8M transactions); ~PHP 500K-1M disputed value/month |
| **Owner** | Finance Analyst (Payments) |
| **Participants** | Finance Analyst, Store Manager, Customer, Bank/Acquirer, Finance Manager |

### Background

BuildRight Depot operates ~600 POS terminals across 200 stores, processing approximately 2.8 million monthly transactions with ~36% (approximately 1 million transactions) settled via credit or debit card through partnerships with BDO, BPI, Metrobank, and EastWest Bank as acquiring banks, supporting Visa, Mastercard, JCB, and UnionPay card schemes. At this transaction volume, card chargebacks and settlement exceptions are an operational certainty. Chargebacks arise when a cardholder disputes a transaction through their issuing bank — common reason codes include: fraudulent card-not-present transactions (relevant for BuildRight's growing ecommerce channel), "product not received" or "product not as described" disputes, duplicate charges, authorization issues, and credit not processed claims. Settlement exceptions occur when the batch settlement amount from POS terminals does not match the acquiring bank's expected deposit — caused by batch transmission errors, MDR (merchant discount rate) adjustments, interchange fee category changes, or timing differences. W537 covers card terminal settlement processing and W99 covers payment reconciliation at the aggregate level, but neither handles the granular exception resolution and chargeback dispute representment process. Philippine card scheme operating rules (governed by Visa and Mastercard global rules with BSP-registered local variations) impose strict dispute resolution timelines — typically a 30-day response window for chargeback representment. Failure to respond within deadlines results in automatic chargeback loss. BuildRight's chargeback rate must remain below card scheme thresholds (typically 0.75-1.0%) to avoid merchant account monitoring programs, excess fees, or potential account termination by the acquirer.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Chargeback notification receipt**: Finance Analyst receives chargeback notification from acquiring bank via: (a) acquirer online merchant portal alert, (b) email notification from acquirer dispute resolution team, (c) debit advice from bank for chargebacks already deducted from settlement. Each notification includes: card scheme reason code, transaction date, transaction amount, card type, cardholder dispute reason, response deadline (typically 30 calendar days from notification date) | Finance Analyst | — | 5-10 min/notification |
| 2 | **Transaction matching and initial assessment**: Finance Analyst matches chargeback to original POS transaction: (a) locate original transaction in POS system by: terminal ID, batch number, transaction reference, transaction date and time, and card last-4 digits, (b) verify transaction details: authorization code, settlement status, batch deposit date, (c) cross-reference with W537 settlement records to confirm transaction was included in batch deposit, (d) classify chargeback by reason code: (i) fraud (card not present, counterfeit card), (ii) authorization (declined authorization, no authorization obtained), (iii) processing error (duplicate charge, incorrect amount), (iv) cardholder dispute (product not received, product not as described, credit not processed), (v) friendly fraud (cardholder legitimately made purchase but disputes to avoid payment) | Finance Analyst | — | 10-15 min/case |
| 3 | **Evidence compilation**: Finance Analyst gathers representment evidence based on chargeback reason code: (a) for all chargebacks: (i) original signed or electronic receipt from POS terminal, (ii) POS transaction log showing authorization code and approval response, (iii) transaction detail from POS system including terminal ID, cashier ID, store location, (b) for "product not received" disputes: (i) goods receipt or delivery confirmation per W4 store delivery, (ii) signed delivery receipt or waybill, (iii) ecommerce tracking confirmation for online orders, (c) for "product not as described" disputes: (i) product listing/sales description at time of purchase, (ii) store display or online catalog screenshot, (iii) return policy documentation presented to customer, (d) for fraud disputes: (i) EMV chip read confirmation (if card-present), (ii) 3D Secure authentication result (if card-not-present/ecommerce), (iii) CCTV footage from store showing cardholder at POS (retention: 30 days per store policy; must request from Store Manager within 7 days), (iv) IP address and device fingerprint for ecommerce transactions per W519 | Finance Analyst | Store Manager (CCTV/evidence) | 20-40 min/case |
| 4 | **Legitimate dispute — accept chargeback**: If Finance Analyst determines chargeback is legitimate: (a) customer was correctly charged twice for same transaction (duplicate), (b) customer returned product but refund not processed per W101, (c) transaction amount was incorrectly entered at POS, (d) Finance Analyst accepts chargeback in acquirer portal: system posts journal entry — Dr. Chargeback Expense (cost of doing business) / Cr. Bank (chargeback already deducted from settlement), (e) if root cause is store-level error: notify Store Manager for cashier training per W5B, (f) update chargeback tracker with acceptance reason | Finance Analyst | — | 10 min/case |
| 5 | **Contestable chargeback — compile representment package**: If Finance Analyst determines chargeback is contestable (evidence supports that transaction was legitimate): (a) compile representment package per card scheme requirements: (i) cover letter summarizing dispute and evidence, (ii) copy of signed receipt or electronic signature capture, (iii) proof of delivery or service completion, (iv) authorization response documentation, (v) refund/return policy documentation, (vi) any prior correspondence with cardholder, (b) for Visa: submit via Visa Resolve Online (VROL) system or acquirer portal, (c) for Mastercard: submit via Mastercom or acquirer portal, (d) for JCB/UnionPay: submit through acquiring bank's dispute channel, (e) submission must be completed within 30-day deadline — no extensions granted by card schemes | Finance Analyst | — | 30-60 min/case |
| 6 | **Representment status tracking**: Finance Analyst tracks representment through acquirer arbitration: (a) acquirer reviews representment package and submits to issuing bank, (b) issuing bank reviews and either: (i) accepts representment — chargeback reversed, funds returned to BuildRight's settlement account, (ii) rejects representment — chargeback stands, (iii) escalates to second chargeback or pre-arbitration (Visa) / arbitration (Mastercard), (c) second chargeback/pre-arbitration: Finance Analyst evaluates whether to pursue further based on: (i) disputed amount, (ii) strength of additional evidence, (iii) arbitration filing fees (Visa: USD 500, Mastercard: USD 250-400), (d) update chargeback tracker with each status change and timeline | Finance Analyst | Finance Manager (> PHP 50K) | 10-15 min/case/week |
| 7 | **Representment outcome and root cause analysis**: Upon final representment outcome: (a) if successful (chargeback reversed): system reverses chargeback entry — Dr. Bank / Cr. Chargeback Expense; confirm settlement deposit includes reversal amount in next batch per W537, (b) if unsuccessful (chargeback stands): chargeback remains as loss; Finance Analyst analyzes root cause: (i) was evidence sufficient? (ii) was deadline met? (iii) was store-level process deficient? (iv) is customer a repeat dispute filer (friendly fraud pattern)?, (c) update chargeback tracker with final outcome, root cause classification, and action items | Finance Analyst | — | 10-15 min/case |
| 8 | **Monthly chargeback analytics**: Finance Analyst prepares monthly chargeback analytics report: (a) chargeback volume: total count and value vs. prior month and same month prior year, (b) chargeback rate: chargeback count / total card transaction count — must remain below 0.75% card scheme threshold, (c) breakdown by reason code: fraud, authorization, processing error, cardholder dispute, (d) breakdown by card type: Visa, Mastercard, JCB, UnionPay, (e) representment success rate: % of contested chargebacks successfully reversed, (f) top 10 stores by chargeback rate — identify stores needing cashier training or fraud prevention improvements, (g) top 10 stores by chargeback volume, (h) average chargeback value, (i) total chargeback expense (net of recoveries) vs. budget, (j) settlement exception count and value — batch discrepancies unresolved per W537, (k) present to Finance Manager with recommendations | Finance Analyst | Finance Manager | 2 hours/month |
| 9 | **Quarterly merchant fee analysis and cost optimization**: Finance Analyst conducts quarterly review linked to W640 merchant fee analysis: (a) calculate effective MDR rate by card type and acquiring bank — actual MDR charges / card transaction volume, (b) compare MDR rates across BDO, BPI, Metrobank, EastWest — identify opportunities to renegotiate or consolidate acquiring relationships, (c) assess interchange fee category accuracy — ensure BuildRight is categorized correctly (retail / big-box vs. supermarket rates differ), (d) evaluate chargeback prevention ROI: cost of prevention measures (3D Secure, EMV, staff training) vs. chargeback losses avoided, (e) if chargeback rate exceeds 0.5%: implement enhanced prevention — mandatory chip-and-PIN for transactions above PHP 10K, enhanced ecommerce fraud screening per W519, (f) present analysis to Finance Manager for acquirer negotiation strategy | Finance Analyst | Finance Manager | 4 hours/quarter |

### System Touchpoints

- W537 card terminal settlement — batch settlement and deposit reconciliation
- W99 payment settlement reconciliation — aggregate payment reconciliation where exceptions surface
- W640 merchant fee analysis & payment cost optimization — MDR rate monitoring and acquirer comparison
- W488 credit card chargeback dispute management — chargeback workflow (if exists; this workflow provides detailed operational procedure)
- W89 bank reconciliation — chargeback entries on bank statements
- W5B in-store selling — POS transaction origination and cashier procedures
- W519 POS AML reporting — ecommerce fraud screening and transaction monitoring
- W340 store cash audit — store-level transaction documentation and evidence
- W101 customer refund & credit processing — refund-related chargeback root cause
- W4 store delivery — delivery confirmation evidence for "product not received" disputes

### Pain Points / Risks

- Tight dispute response deadlines — 30-calendar-day window from chargeback notification to representment submission; missed deadline results in automatic chargeback loss regardless of evidence strength; weekends and holidays consume deadline days
- Evidence collection from stores — CCTV footage retained only 30 days at most stores; signed receipts may be misfiled or damaged; Store Managers must prioritize evidence requests from Finance Analyst within 7 days to allow adequate representment preparation time
- Friendly fraud — cardholders who legitimately made purchases but file chargebacks to avoid payment; difficult to prove with card-present transactions; represents ~20-30% of chargebacks by volume; creates tension between customer relationship management and financial loss prevention
- Acquirer decision finality — once acquirer issues final chargeback decision after arbitration, BuildRight has no further recourse; must absorb loss; Philippine legal options against cardholder are theoretically available but impractical for typical retail transaction amounts
- Chargeback ratio thresholds — Visa and Mastercard monitor merchant chargeback ratios; exceeding 0.75% triggers Early Warning Program (Visa) or Excessive Chargeback Program (Mastercard); consequences include mandatory remediation plans, additional fees (USD 50-100 per chargeback in monitoring programs), and potential merchant account termination
- Multi-acquirer complexity — 4 acquiring banks with different portal interfaces, dispute procedures, and reporting formats; Finance Analyst must manage login credentials, track deadlines, and compile analytics across all platforms
- Settlement exception volume — beyond chargebacks, batch settlement discrepancies (short deposits, MDR adjustments, interchange corrections) require separate reconciliation effort per W537 and W99; combined exception volume can overwhelm Finance Analyst capacity during peak retail periods (Ber months, holiday season)
- Ecommerce channel growth — as BuildRight expands ecommerce, card-not-present transactions will increase chargeback exposure; CNP transactions have 3-5x higher chargeback rates than card-present transactions

### Time Estimate

Per chargeback: notification and matching (15-25 min) + evidence compilation (20-40 min) + representment (30-60 min for contestable; 10 min for accepted) + tracking (10-15 min/week while active) = 75-140 min/contestable chargeback. Monthly: ~100 chargebacks × weighted average 60-90 min = 100-150 hours/month. Monthly analytics: 2 hours. Quarterly review: 4 hours. Total: ~100-155 hours/month.

### Staffing Implication

1 dedicated Finance Analyst (Payments) with primary responsibility for chargeback and settlement exception management. Supported by Store Managers for evidence collection (approximately 2-3 evidence requests/store/month = ~30 min/store/month). Finance Analyst role also supports W537 and W99 reconciliation activities. Position: Finance Analyst (Payments), annual salary ~PHP 420K-540K. No additional headcount required for stores — absorbed within existing Store Manager administrative time.

## W919. Intercompany Inventory Movement Accounting & Goods-in-Transit Reconciliation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Goods movement between BuildRight Depot, Inc. (inventory owner) and BuildRight Logistics, Inc. (DC operator); inter-DC transfer; or store replenishment order dispatch from DC |
| **Frequency** | ~5,000 store replenishment orders/month + ~30–40 inter-DC transfers/month + ~600 DSD receipts/month = ~5,640–6,640 IC inventory movements/month |
| **Volume** | ~PHP 3.5–4.0B/month in inventory value moving through IC supply chain |
| **Owner** | Logistics Finance Analyst |
| **Participants** | DC Operations (BuildRight Logistics), Supply Planning, Logistics Finance Analyst, Intercompany Accountant, AP/AR Team |

### Background

BuildRight's corporate structure creates a unique inventory accounting challenge: BuildRight Depot, Inc. owns all merchandise inventory throughout the supply chain (at DCs and in stores), but the DCs are operated by BuildRight Logistics, Inc. under a service fee arrangement. This means every inventory movement — DC receiving, DC putaway, DC picking, DC-to-store shipment, inter-DC transfer — involves goods owned by one entity physically handled by another. While the intercompany service fee billing is handled by W435 (IC SLA fee billing) and W752 (IC management fee allocation), the inventory movement itself requires precise accounting treatment: (a) goods-in-transit accounting — when inventory leaves DC but has not yet been received at store, it must be tracked as "in-transit" inventory (still owned by Depot Inc., in the custody of Logistics Inc.); (b) IC custody transfer documentation — every movement must be documented for transfer pricing compliance per W235; (c) damage/loss accountability — goods damaged or lost while in Logistics Inc. custody must be charged to Logistics Inc. per W500; (d) inventory valuation reconciliation — Depot Inc.'s inventory subledger must reconcile with Logistics Inc.'s warehouse management system at month-end. This workflow ensures accurate intercompany inventory accounting across 4 DCs, 200 stores, and ~5,000+ monthly movements, supporting consolidation per W765 and external audit per W351.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **DC Outbound Dispatch & Goods-in-Transit Creation**: (a) DC picks and dispatches store replenishment order per W106 (outbound dispatch); (b) at dispatch confirmation, system creates goods-in-transit (GIT) record: DR number, departure DC, destination store, SKU list, quantities, inventory value (WAC per INV-003), dispatch timestamp, and carrier/vehicle ID; (c) accounting entries in BuildRight Depot, Inc. books: Dr. Inventory – In-Transit / Cr. Inventory – DC [location]; no entry in BuildRight Logistics, Inc. books (Logistics Inc. does not own inventory — it handles physical movement); (d) custody acknowledgment: DC dispatches goods with electronic proof of dispatch (ePDD) signed by truck driver; Logistics Inc. acknowledges custody transfer of physical goods; (e) GIT inventory visible in supply chain control tower per W250 with estimated arrival date; (f) average GIT duration: 1–3 days (DC to store); GIT value at any point: ~PHP 150–250M (2–3 days of inventory in transit across 200 stores) | DC Operations / System | Logistics Finance Analyst | Automated per dispatch |
| 2 | **Store Receiving & GIT Clearance**: (a) store receives goods and processes goods receipt per W109; (b) at GR confirmation, system clears GIT record and posts: Dr. Inventory – Store [location] / Cr. Inventory – In-Transit; (c) receiving discrepancy handling: (i) quantity shortage: GIT partially cleared — shortage quantity remains in GIT as "pending investigation"; (ii) damaged goods: GIT cleared to store inventory, damage claim created against Logistics Inc. or carrier per W500; (iii) wrong items: GIT not cleared — items returned to DC via W22B; (d) all GIT records must be cleared within 7 days of dispatch; uncleared GIT aged > 7 days flagged for investigation; aged > 14 days escalated to VP Supply Chain per W250; (e) daily GIT aging report transmitted to Logistics Finance Analyst | Receiving Clerk / System | Logistics Finance Analyst | Automated per receipt |
| 3 | **Inter-DC Transfer Accounting**: (a) inter-DC transfer orders initiated by Supply Planning per W22; (b) since all inventory is owned by BuildRight Depot, Inc., inter-DC transfers are purely physical movements within the same entity — no IC accounting entries required for inventory ownership; (c) however, custody transfer between DCs (both operated by Logistics Inc.) is documented for: (i) warehouse management reconciliation, (ii) DC performance KPI tracking per W586 (inventory at each DC), and (iii) insurance coverage verification per W857 (which DC's insurance covers goods in transit); (d) inter-DC GIT tracked similarly to DC-to-store GIT with same clearing SLA; (e) monthly: inter-DC transfer volume, value, and average GIT duration reported in supply chain analytics per W680 | Supply Planning / DC Operations / System | Logistics Finance Analyst | Automated per transfer |
| 4 | **Month-End GIT Reconciliation & IC Inventory Verification**: (a) at month-end per W9A, Logistics Finance Analyst reconciles: (i) BuildRight Depot, Inc. inventory subledger (by location: DC1, DC2, DC3, DC4, Store 1–200, In-Transit) vs. (ii) BuildRight Logistics, Inc. WMS physical inventory records (by DC); (iii) any discrepancy investigated and resolved before close per W514; (b) GIT balance verification: sum of all uncleared GIT records must equal the "Inventory – In-Transit" GL balance; discrepancy indicates unprocessed receipts, system errors, or timing differences; (c) IC custody reconciliation: Logistics Inc. provides certificate of physical inventory held at each DC as of month-end; reconciled against Depot Inc.'s DC inventory balances; (d) damage/loss reconciliation: accumulated damage claims per W500 reconciled against Logistics Inc. monthly service fee deduction per W435; (e) GIT provisions: uncleared GIT aged > 30 days provisioned as potential loss per W92; (f) reconciliation sign-off: Logistics Finance Analyst + Intercompany Accountant + DC Operations Manager | Logistics Finance Analyst / Intercompany Accountant / DC Operations | CFO | 8–12 hours/month |
| 5 | **Quarterly IC Inventory Audit & Annual Compliance**: (a) quarterly: Internal Audit per W342 selects 2–3 stores and 1 DC for surprise IC inventory count; physical count reconciled against Depot Inc. subledger and Logistics Inc. WMS; (b) annual: full IC inventory reconciliation as part of year-end close per W9B; external auditor observes IC inventory reconciliation per W351; (c) transfer pricing documentation: inventory movement patterns between Depot Inc. and Logistics Inc. documented for BIR per W235 and PAS 24 related party disclosure per W486; (d) annual IC inventory movement analysis: volume, value, GIT duration trends, damage rates, and reconciliation accuracy; (e) process improvement recommendations from Internal Audit findings per W333 | Internal Audit / Logistics Finance Analyst / CFO | CFO | 20–30 hours/quarter |

### System Touchpoints

- ERP inventory management (INV-001) for perpetual inventory by location with GIT tracking
- WMS integration (WMS-008) for DC inventory and dispatch confirmation
- Store receiving system (W109) for GR processing and GIT clearing
- Supply chain control tower (W250) for GIT visibility
- Transfer order system (W22) for inter-DC movements
- GL system for IC inventory accounting entries
- Intercompany module (IC-001 through IC-005) for IC reconciliation
- Damage claim system (W500) for GIT loss/damage tracking
- Consolidation module (W765) for IC elimination
- BI dashboard for GIT aging and IC inventory analytics

### Pain Points / Risks

- **GIT accumulation**: uncleared GIT records accumulate when stores delay GR processing; mitigated by 7-day clearing SLA with daily aging report and Store Manager escalation for GIT > 14 days
- **Reconciliation complexity**: reconciling Depot Inc. subledger against Logistics Inc. WMS across 205 locations is time-intensive; mitigated by automated reconciliation system that matches WMS quantities to ERP balances daily, flagging exceptions for manual review
- **Damage accountability disputes**: Logistics Inc. may dispute damage claims (arguing damage occurred after store receipt); mitigated by photographic evidence requirement at DC dispatch and store receiving, and independent LP investigation per W843
- **Month-end timing pressure**: IC inventory reconciliation is on the critical path for 5-day close per W9A; mitigated by daily reconciliation throughout the month (reducing month-end workload to exception resolution only) and dedicated Logistics Finance Analyst role
- **Audit complexity**: external auditors must understand the Depot/Logistics inventory ownership structure; mitigated by clear documentation in IC memo and process walkthrough during audit planning per W351

### Staffing Implication

- **Logistics Finance Analyst**: primary owner of this workflow; ~20–25 hours/month on daily monitoring, reconciliation, and month-end close; dedicated role at HQ per model company profile
- **Intercompany Accountant**: ~4–6 hours/month on IC reconciliation support; absorbed by existing role
- **DC Operations**: reconciliation support ~2–3 hours/month per DC; absorbed by existing role
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate

- Daily GIT monitoring: 30–60 min/day
- GIT clearing exception resolution: 30–60 min/day
- Month-end reconciliation: 8–12 hours
- Quarterly audit support: 20–30 hours/quarter
- **Total per month**: ~30–40 hours
